## W17. Customer Loyalty Program Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer transaction (earn) or redemption request (redeem) |
| **Frequency** | Continuous at POS; monthly batch processing |
| **Volume** | ~2.8M POS transactions/month (earn); ~50,000 redemptions/month |
| **Owner** | Marketing — Loyalty Manager |
| **Participants** | Cashier (POS), Loyalty Manager, Customer Service, Marketing |

### Loyalty Program Economics

| Parameter | Value | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Earn rate | 1 point per PHP 100 spent | Applied to transaction value after discounts, before VAT |
| Redemption value | PHP 1.00 per point | Each point can be redeemed for PHP 1.00 discount at checkout |
| Face value of points | 1% of qualifying transaction value | Face value = (1 point × PHP 1.00) ÷ PHP 100 = 1% of spend |
| PFRS 15 deferred revenue allocation | ~0.75–0.85% of qualifying transaction value | Standalone selling price allocation = face value (1%) × expected redemption rate (~75–85%); this is the actual amount allocated to deferred revenue at POS earning; the ~1.0% face value is NOT the PFRS 15 allocation rate — the allocation rate is lower because ~15–25% of points are expected to expire unredeemed (breakage) and are excluded from the standalone selling price of the points |
| Estimated monthly points earned | ~29M points (PHP ~2.9B qualifying loyalty-earning revenue ÷ PHP 100; ≈343M/year per W1316) | Before breakage adjustment |
| Estimated monthly deferred revenue allocation (PFRS 15) | ~PHP 22–25M/month (PHP ~2.9B qualifying loyalty-earning revenue × ~0.75–0.85% allocation, consistent with the points row above) | Monthly flow: new deferred revenue from points earned; recognized proportionally at redemption or as breakage at expiry |
| Estimated cumulative loyalty points liability (balance sheet) | ~PHP 150–600M (outstanding unredeemed points × PHP 1.00 × expected redemption rate ~75–85%, net of redemptions within the 24-month expiry window) | Cumulative stock: outstanding points not yet redeemed or expired; reconciled monthly by Cost Accountant per W17.11a |
| Breakage (unredeemed expired points) | Recognized at 24-month expiry per W17.11 | Historically ~15–25% of points expire unredeemed |
| Tier upgrade thresholds | Bronze: 0–PHP 50K/year; Silver: PHP 50K–150K; Gold: PHP 150K–300K; Platinum: > PHP 300K | Based on trailing 12-month spend |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer signs up for loyalty program (in-store, online, or via app) | Customer / CSR | Loyalty Manager | 3 min (in-store) |
| 2 | System creates loyalty account with tier status (Bronze default); captures data privacy consent flag with purpose, date, and consent version per RA 10173 (Data Privacy Act) | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | At each POS transaction, cashier scans loyalty card or enters mobile number | Cashier | — | 15 sec |
| 4 | System calculates points earned (1 point per PHP 100 spent on transaction value after discounts, before VAT) and updates balance; per PFRS 15, system allocates ~0.75–0.85% of transaction revenue to loyalty deferred revenue (1.0% face value × 75–85% expected redemption, per the economics table) based on relative standalone selling price of points (Dr. Cash or Accounts Receivable / Cr. Revenue for product portion ~99%, Cr. Deferred Revenue — Loyalty Points for points portion ~1%); allocation rate configurable based on expected redemption rate | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | Customer checks points balance via app, receipt, or in-store kiosk | Customer | — | Self-service |
| 6 | At checkout, customer requests points redemption; cashier selects redemption option | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 7 | System validates sufficient points; converts to peso value; applies as discount; system recognizes deferred revenue proportionally for redeemed points (Dr. Deferred Revenue — Loyalty Points / Cr. Revenue) | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | Monthly: Loyalty Manager reviews tier movement (upgrade/downgrade based on trailing 12-month spend) | System | Loyalty Manager | Automated + 1 hour review |
| 8a | **Tier downgrade proactive communication**: 60 days before a tier downgrade would take effect (based on trailing 12-month spend trajectory), system generates an alert for the Loyalty Manager; Loyalty Manager reviews at-risk accounts and triggers proactive communication: (a) system sends personalized email/SMS to customer notifying them of impending downgrade with specific spend threshold needed to maintain current tier, (b) includes a "stay" offer if configured (e.g., double-points weekend, member-exclusive discount coupon), (c) tracks customer response and incremental spend during the 60-day save window; monthly: Loyalty Manager reviews save rate (percentage of at-risk customers who maintain their tier) as a program engagement KPI; system does not send downgrade notices to customers on the lowest tier (Bronze) | System / Loyalty Manager | Loyalty Manager | Automated + 2 hours/month review |
| 9 | Monthly: generate loyalty program report (enrollment, active rate, points liability, redemption rate) | System | Loyalty Manager | 30 min |
| 10 | Quarterly: Loyalty Manager plans double-points weekends and member-exclusive promos | Loyalty Manager | CMO | 2 hours/quarter |
| 11 | Annual: points expiry batch run (points unused after 24 months expire); system recognizes breakage revenue from expired points (Dr. Deferred Revenue — Loyalty Points / Cr. Revenue) | System | Loyalty Manager | Automated |
| 11a | Monthly: Cost Accountant estimates total loyalty liability (outstanding points × redemption value × expected redemption rate based on trailing 12-month actual redemption data); reconciles to deferred revenue balance on balance sheet; adjusts deferred revenue if estimate differs from recorded balance | Cost Accountant | Controller | 30 min/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Loyalty enrollment (in-store, online, app) (W17.1–2)
- Points earning at POS with real-time update (W17.3–4)
- Points balance inquiry (app, receipt, kiosk) (W17.5)
- Points redemption at POS as payment method (W17.6–7)
- Tier auto-calculation based on trailing spend (W17.8)
- Loyalty analytics dashboard (W17.9)
- Scheduled points expiry with breakage revenue recognition (W17.11)
- Loyalty deferred revenue: allocation at POS earning (PFRS 15), proportional recognition at redemption, breakage recognition at expiry, monthly liability estimation and reconciliation (W17.4, W17.7, W17.11, W17.11a)
- Manual points adjustment: Loyalty Manager (or authorized CSR) initiates adjustment with reason code and supporting documentation (system error, complaint resolution per W41, retroactive earning for missing transaction, dispute resolution); approval required for adjustments above threshold (> 500 points: Loyalty Manager; > 5,000 points: Marketing Head); system logs adjustment with initiator ID, approver ID, reason, and original transaction reference; monthly audit of all adjustments by Loyalty Manager (W17)
- Loyalty fraud resolution process: when W37 loyalty fraud detection flags a suspected fraud pattern — (a) LPO creates fraud investigation case in system with detection rule triggered, affected account(s), and evidence; (b) Loyalty Manager reviews case within 2 business days and classifies: confirmed fraud, suspected fraud (requires further monitoring), or false positive; (c) **if confirmed fraud** — Loyalty Manager suspends the affected loyalty account (account cannot earn or redeem points; customer notified via email/SMS of suspension with reason and contact for appeal); LPO deducts fraudulently earned points (manual adjustment per approval tier above); if fraud involves employee (cashier self-scanning), LPO escalates per W37.5 confirmed irregularity process; (d) **if suspected fraud** — Loyalty Manager places account on monitoring status (all transactions flagged for 30-day observation period; no account suspension); if fraud confirmed during monitoring, escalate to confirmed fraud; if no further suspicious activity in 30 days, remove monitoring status; (e) **customer appeal**: customer may appeal suspension by contacting Customer Service; Loyalty Manager reviews appeal within 5 business days; if appeal upheld, account reinstated with apology communication and compensatory points per W41 complaint resolution; if appeal denied, account remains suspended; (f) **permanent ban**: for repeat offenders (2+ confirmed fraud incidents), Loyalty Manager recommends permanent account ban to Marketing Head for approval; permanently banned accounts cannot be reactivated; (g) monthly: Loyalty Manager generates fraud resolution summary — cases opened, confirmed, false positive rate, accounts suspended, points recovered; feeds into W37 shrinkage reporting
- Customer data deduplication: system performs fuzzy-match deduplication at enrollment (blocks creation if > 85% match on name + phone or name + email); Loyalty Manager reviews weekly deduplication queue of potential duplicates; merges approved records with points consolidation; monthly data quality dashboard showing duplicate rate, incomplete records, and invalid contact info (W17.1–2)
- Ecommerce loyalty points earning: for online orders (W11 BOPIS and W19 home delivery), system awards points when order is fulfilled (picked up for BOPIS, delivered for home delivery), not at order placement, because revenue recognition occurs at fulfillment; customer identifies loyalty account at online checkout via registered account linkage; guest checkout customers do not earn points; system calculates points using the same earn rate (1 point per PHP 100); PFRS 15 deferred revenue allocation follows same logic as in-store earning (W17.4); order fulfillment event triggers points earning via ecommerce-to-ERP integration

### Pain Points / Risks
- PFRS 15 deferred revenue complexity — if the expected redemption rate estimate is inaccurate, quarterly adjustments create earnings volatility and audit scrutiny
- Loyalty fraud schemes (cashier self-scanning, points farming, account takeovers) can erode program value; detection relies on W37 analytics that may lag behind new fraud patterns
- Customer data privacy risk under RA 10173 — loyalty database with 1M+ customer records is a high-value target; any breach triggers NPC notification requirements and reputational damage
- Points liability growth outpacing revenue — if redemption rates increase without corresponding earn rate adjustments, the loyalty program becomes a material margin drag

---


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "account with tier" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-capture of "enters mobile number" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates points earned" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-32 (ensure loyalty liability accuracy.); CTL-46 (ensure loyalty fraud investigation and resolution.)
- operational: Marketing — Loyalty Manager review and approval gate; operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Time Estimate
~5 hours/month for Loyalty Manager (1 hour monthly report + 2 hours tier review/communication + 2 hours quarterly promo planning). Cost Accountant adds 30 min/month for liability reconciliation. POS points earning/redemption is automated — no incremental cashier time. Annual points expiry batch run is automated.

### Staffing Implication
- **1 Loyalty Manager** (within Marketing): Day-to-day program management is largely automated. Monthly reviews and quarterly promo planning = ~5 hours/month. One person within the 25-person Marketing team (profile §3.3) handles this alongside other duties.

## W273. In-Store Endless Aisle & Vendor Direct-to-Customer Delivery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer wants to purchase an out-of-stock or non-ranged bulky item while physically in the store. |
| **Frequency** | Moderate |
| **Volume** | ~1–2 per store per week (a subset of the ~3,000–5,000 monthly special orders chain-wide per the canon) |
| **Owner** | Store Selling Staff |
| **Participants** | Customer, Cashier, Vendor, Ecommerce Fulfillment |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sales Associate helps customer browse the extended catalog (Endless Aisle) on in-store tablet or POS kiosk, checking vendor real-time inventory (ECOM-001) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 15 min |
| 2 | Associate creates a Drop-Ship Sales Order in the system, linked to the customer's profile (W156) and the selected vendor/SKU | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 3 | Customer pays at the physical POS — cashier processes as "Endless Aisle / Special Order" tender type | Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 4 | System auto-generates a Drop-Ship Purchase Order (W38) to the vendor with customer delivery address and expected delivery date | System | Purchasing Dept | Automated |
| 5 | Vendor picks, packs, and ships directly to the customer's home; system sends tracking SMS/email to customer | Vendor / 3PL | Logistics | 1-5 days |
| 6 | Upon delivery, customer signs POD (Proof of Delivery); vendor uploads to BuildRight portal | Vendor | AP Clerk | 10 min |
| 7 | System 3-way matches: POS payment ↔ Drop-Ship PO ↔ Vendor invoice + POD; settles vendor payment per W7 | System | AP Clerk | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Endless Aisle module (catalog browsing, special order tender)
- Ecommerce extended catalog / PIM (W50) for vendor product data
- Procurement auto-PO generation (W38 special order fulfillment)
- AP 3-way match for drop-ship settlement (W7)
- CRM customer profile linkage (W156)
- Customer notification engine (SMS/email tracking)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Vendor inventory inaccuracy — item shown as available online but out of stock at vendor, causing order cancellation and customer frustration
- Delivery delays reflecting poorly on BuildRight's brand even though vendor is responsible
- Drop-ship PO and POS payment reconciliation complexity (timing mismatch between customer payment at POS and vendor invoice receipt)
- Vendor inability to deliver to provincial/remote addresses (Philippine logistics coverage gaps)
- No physical quality check before delivery — defective items shipped directly to customer
- Commission/attribution tracking for sales associates on endless aisle orders


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "Sales Associate helps customer browse the extended catalog" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-create of "creates a Drop-Ship Sales Order in the system, linked to the customer's profile" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "generates a Drop-Ship Purchase Order" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
~1–2 orders per store per week × 200 stores = ~200–400 orders/week (~900–1,700/month) chain-wide, within the ~3,000–5,000 monthly special-order canon. Each order takes ~25 min of store staff time (browsing + order creation + POS). Absorbed by existing Sales Associates and Cashiers. AP reconciliation adds ~1–2 FTE at shared services for the drop-ship invoice matching volume.

### Time Estimate
~25 min per transaction (in-store); 1-5 days for vendor delivery; automated settlement.
Breakdown: 15 min catalog browsing with customer + 5 min drop-ship order creation + 5 min POS payment processing.
AP reconciliation of drop-ship invoices adds ~1–2 FTE at shared services for the ~200–400 weekly orders chain-wide.

## W274. Third-Party Customer Financing / Equipment Leasing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer opts to finance a high-value purchase using a third-party financing partner (e.g., Home Credit) in-store. |
| **Frequency** | Daily |
| **Volume** | 5-15 per store per day |
| **Owner** | Cashier |
| **Participants** | Customer, Financing Rep, Store Cashier, Treasury |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer selects high-value goods (e.g., appliances, power tools) and applies for financing with the in-store financing rep (e.g., Home Credit, CGCC) | Financing Rep | — | 15 min |
| 2 | Financing partner runs credit check and approves the loan; issues a reference code/voucher valid for 24 hours | Financing Partner | — | 10 min |
| 3 | Cashier rings up the sale on POS, selects "Third-Party Financing" as tender type, and inputs the reference code | Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 4 | Customer takes the goods; system records a receivable from the financing partner (not from the customer) | Customer | — | — |
| 5 | Treasury reconciles the batch of financed sales against the remittance from the financing partner (minus MDR of 3-7%) within T+3 business days (W261) | Treasury Analyst | Finance Manager | 2 hours/batch |
| 6 | Finance posts any MDR variance or unreconciled reference codes to a suspense account for follow-up | Finance Analyst | Controller | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Tender Type configuration (Third-Party Financing with reference code validation)
- Financing Partner API integration (real-time credit approval)
- AR module (partner receivable, not customer receivable)
- Treasury reconciliation dashboard (W261 settlement matching)
- GL suspense account for unreconciled financing transactions
- Reporting: financing volume by partner, by store, by SKU category

### Pain Points / Risks
- Reference code mismatches between POS entry and partner system causing reconciliation failures
- Delayed remittances from financing partners (T+3 often becomes T+5+ in Philippine banking)
- MDR (Merchant Discount Rate) of 3-7% eroding margins on high-value items
- Financing partner system outages halting the entire checkout flow for financed purchases
- Customer disputes on financing terms (interest rates, hidden fees) blamed on BuildRight staff
- BSP regulations on consumer financing disclosures
- Risk of fraud — fictitious financing applications using stolen IDs (RA 10173 implications)


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "check and approves the loan" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-record of "records a receivable from the financing partner" (replaces manual Step 4).
- System auto-reconcile of "reconciles the batch of financed sales against the remittance from the…" (replaces manual Step 5).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: causing reconciliation failures; operational: outages halting the entire checkout flow for financed purchases

### Staffing Implication
~15–20 financing transactions per store per day × 200 stores ≈ ~4,000/day chain-wide (per the installment canon). POS processing ~5 min per transaction — absorbed by existing Cashiers. Treasury reconciliation ~2 hours/day for batch processing — 1 Treasury Analyst dedicated to financing partner reconciliation. Financing reps are employed by the partner, not BuildRight.

### Time Estimate
30 min per application (customer-facing); 5 min at POS; 2 hours daily for Treasury batch reconciliation.
Breakdown: 15 min customer credit application with financing rep + 10 min credit approval + 5 min POS tender processing.
Treasury batch reconciliation: 2 hours/day for 1,000-3,000 daily transactions across 200 stores. MDR variance follow-up: 30 min/day.

## W438. Yard Dispatch & Customer Vehicle Loading Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer completes purchase of bulky items (Lumber, Cement, Steel, Sand) at POS |
| **Frequency** | High; ~7–10 customer loading events per store per day (matching W950's monthly volume) |
| **Volume** | Variable (from 5 bags of cement to full truckloads of lumber) |
| **Owner** | Yard Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Customer, Yard Associate (Porter), Forklift Operator, Security Guard |

### Background

Bulk building materials are stored in the outdoor yard or heavy-loading zone. Unlike standard retail checkout, the customer must drive their vehicle to the loading bay after payment. Ensuring the correct quantity is loaded and safety protocols are followed is critical for both loss prevention and public safety.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Dispatch Notification**: Upon payment at POS, system sends "Loading Instruction" to the Yard Supervisor's tablet | System | Yard Supervisor | Automated |
| 2 | **Vehicle Arrival**: Customer drives to the Loading Bay; presents "Paid" receipt or "Loading Slip" to the Guard | Customer / Guard | Yard Supervisor | 2 min |
| 3 | **Order Verification**: Yard Associate scans the Loading Slip barcode; system displays items and quantities to be loaded | Yard Associate | Yard Supervisor | 1 min |
| 4 | **Loading**: Associate or Forklift Operator loads materials into the customer's vehicle; follows safety guidelines (e.g., no overloading, proper weight distribution) | Yard Associate / Forklift Op | — | 5–15 min |
| 5 | **Validation & Signature**: Associate confirms quantity loaded on tablet; customer signs digitally to acknowledge receipt of goods in good condition | Yard Associate | Customer | 1 min |
| 6 | **Exit Clearance**: Guard checks vehicle interior/bed against the "Cleared Loading Slip"; verifies exit timestamp; releases vehicle | Security Guard | — | 2 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Yard Dispatch Queue (Real-time feed from POS)
- Mobile Loading App (Quantity confirmation & Digital Signature)
- Gate Pass Integration (Security exit clearance)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Inaccurate Loading**: Loading more units than paid for (Loss Prevention risk) or wrong SKU (e.g., wrong grade of plywood).
- **Vehicle Overloading**: Safety risk to the customer and liability for BuildRight if the vehicle fails on public roads due to improper loading.
- **Congestion**: Slow loading times lead to vehicle queues blocking store entry/exit points.


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-send of "sends" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-scan of "scans the Loading Slip barcode" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-validate of "checks vehicle interior/bed against" (replaces manual Step 6).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- Yard Associates: 2–4 per store (depending on volume).
- Forklift Operators: 1–2 per store.
- Security Guards (Gate): 1 per exit point.

### Time Estimate
~11–21 min per loading event (vehicle arrival 2 min + order verification 1 min + loading 5–15 min + validation/signature 1 min + exit clearance 2 min; dispatch notification automated); at ~7–10 loading events/store/day ≈ 1.5–3.5 hours/store/day of yard labor (per the Frequency and W950) — standing loading-bay staffing (loading associates and forklift operators) absorbed within store headcount.

## W604. Store-Level Customer Experience Standards & Daily Service Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store operating hours begin (part of W5A store opening) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | ~14,000 customer transactions per store per month; ~467 per day |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisors, all customer-facing staff, Customer Service Counter staff |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Store Manager or Assistant Store Manager conducts 5-minute CX briefing during morning huddle: highlights today's CX focus (greeting standard, wait time, service recovery from yesterday's issues); shares one CX success story | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 2 | Department Supervisors verify CX touchpoints are ready: checkout areas clean and organized, customer service counter staffed, returns area clear, BOPIS pickup zone staged, promotional signage accurate | Dept. Supervisor | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 3 | Throughout the day: customer-facing staff execute CX standards — greeting within 10 seconds of customer entering department, proactive offer of assistance, product knowledge support, and farewell at checkout | All Staff | Dept. Supervisor | Ongoing |
| 4 | Customer Service Counter: staff handle walk-in inquiries, returns (W12), BOPIS pickups (W11), special orders (W545), warranty claims (W33), and complaints (W41) per SLA targets; system tracks queue wait times | CS Counter Staff | Store Manager | Ongoing |
| 5 | **Service recovery**: when a customer expresses dissatisfaction (complaint, long wait, stock-out): (a) frontline staff acknowledges immediately, (b) offers immediate resolution per empowerment guidelines (10% discount, free delivery, expedited order), (c) if unresolved, escalates to Department Supervisor then Store Manager, (d) system logs service recovery action | Frontline Staff | Store Manager | 5-15 min |
| 6 | Mid-day: Store Manager walks sales floor for 15-minute CX observation — staff greeting compliance, department appearance, customer queue lengths, bathroom cleanliness, parking lot order | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 7 | End of day: system generates CX daily dashboard — average queue wait time, service recovery count, customer feedback scores (if collected via W608), BOPIS pickup SLA compliance, complaint count | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | Weekly: Store Manager reviews CX metrics in weekly operations review (W575); identifies departments needing CX improvement; assigns CX coaching targets | Store Manager | — | 15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP customer module: complaint tracking, service recovery logging, BOPIS SLA
- POS system: queue wait time analytics, checkout speed metrics
- BI dashboard: store-level CX daily dashboard, weekly CX scorecard
- Mobile app: Store Manager CX observation checklist

### Pain Points / Risks
- **CX inconsistency across 200 stores**: without daily enforcement, CX standards drift; mitigated by morning briefing, mid-day walkthrough, and mystery shopping program (W566)
- **Service recovery empowerment limits**: frontline staff unsure how much they can offer without approval; mitigated by clear empowerment matrix and system-enforced authorization tiers
- **Customer feedback fatigue**: daily feedback collection may annoy customers; mitigated by rotating feedback methods (SMS survey, QR code, in-app) and limiting to random sample


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-issue of "Store Manager or Assistant Store Manager conducts 5-minute CX" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "checkout areas clean and organized, customer service counter staffed, returns…" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-capture of "entering department, proactive offer of assistance, product knowledge support,…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: morning briefing, mid-day walkthrough, and mystery shopping program (W566); operational: clear empowerment matrix and system-enforced authorization tiers; operational: rotating feedback methods (SMS survey, QR code, in-app) and limiting to random sample

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~20 min/day on CX operations (briefing + walkthrough) + ~15 min/week on CX review = ~2.5 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Department Supervisors**: ~10 min/day on CX touchpoint verification. Absorbed within existing role.
- **No incremental headcount**: CX standards are embedded in daily operations of existing staff.

### Time Estimate
~30 min per store-day of dedicated CX management (morning briefing 5 min + touchpoint readiness 10 min + mid-day floor observation 15 min; floor execution and the service counter run within standing staffing, service recovery 5–15 min per event) ≈ 182 hours/store/year ≈ 36,500 hours/year chain-wide, plus the weekly CX metrics review 15 min ≈ 13 hours/store/year (daily dashboard automated).

## W607. Store-Level Product Demo & Trial Station Daily Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store operating hours begin; part of daily store operations |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | ~2–4 demo stations per store (power tool testing, paint mixing, tile/lumber cutting, appliance testing) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Sales Associate, Stock Associate, Maintenance Staff |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sales Associate powers on demo stations during store opening (W5A); verifies equipment operational — power tool testing station (drill, sander, circular saw with safety guards), paint mixing station (colorant levels, tinting machine calibration), appliance display (plugged in, functioning) | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 10 min |
| 2 | Sales Associate verifies demo station supplies: safety goggles and ear protection available, test material stock (wood scraps for cutting station, paint sample cards, tile samples for display), product information brochures stocked | Sales Associate | — | 5 min |
| 3 | Throughout the day: Sales Associate conducts product demonstrations for interested customers; demonstrates power tool features, explains paint mixing options (W168), shows appliance features; logs demonstration count in system for vendor reporting | Sales Associate | — | 5-15 min/demo |
| 4 | Customer trial: Sales Associate supervises customer hands-on trial with mandatory safety briefing (eye protection, proper grip, emergency shutoff location); customer signs trial waiver for power tools | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 10 min |
| 5 | Mid-day: Department Supervisor spot-checks demo stations for cleanliness, safety compliance, and supply levels; replenishes test materials as needed | Dept. Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 6 | End of day: Sales Associate powers down demo equipment; cleans station; secures power tools in locked cabinet; records demo station usage count and any equipment issues in system | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 10 min |
| 7 | Weekly: Department Supervisor reviews demo station usage analytics and sales conversion rate (demos conducted → products sold); identifies underperforming stations for repositioning or removal; reports to Store Manager | Dept. Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 8 | Monthly: Merchandising team reviews demo station effectiveness across 200 stores; updates demo product selection based on new arrivals (W564) and seasonal trends; coordinates with vendor for demo unit replacement per W524 | Category Manager | VP Merchandising | 2 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP POS module: demo unit sales tracking, demo-to-sale conversion analytics
- ERP Maintenance module: equipment issue logging, preventive maintenance scheduling
- Mobile app: demo count logging, supply checklist
- BI dashboard: store-level demo station usage, conversion rates, equipment uptime

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Safety liability**: power tool demo stations carry injury risk; mitigated by mandatory safety briefing, safety equipment provision, and customer trial waiver
- **Equipment wear and tear**: demo units experience heavy use and degrade; mitigated by scheduled preventive maintenance and quarterly demo unit replacement program
- **Low demo-to-sale conversion**: poorly positioned or maintained demo stations may not generate sales; mitigated by weekly usage analytics and station optimization
- **Vendor cost recovery**: some demo units are vendor-supplied; tracking vendor-provided vs. company-purchased demo inventory requires separate SKU treatment per W97


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-test of "Sales Associate powers on demo stations during store opening" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "logs demonstration count in system for vendor reporting" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-validate of "checks demo stations for cleanliness, safety compliance, and supply levels" (replaces manual Step 5).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: mandatory safety briefing, safety equipment provision, and customer trial waiver; operational: safety briefing, safety equipment provision, and customer trial waiver; operational: scheduled preventive maintenance and quarterly demo unit replacement program

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: ~20 min/day on demo station operations (opening + closing + demonstrations). Absorbed within existing role.
- **Department Supervisor**: ~10 min/day spot-check + ~15 min/week review. Absorbed within existing role.
- **No incremental headcount**: demo operations are part of daily selling activities.

### Time Estimate
~35 min per store-day of demo-station upkeep (power-on 10 min + supplies check 5 min + mid-day spot-check 10 min + end-of-day shutdown 10 min) ≈ 213 hours/store/year ≈ 42,600 hours/year chain-wide; customer demonstrations 5–15 min each at realized demand; weekly analytics review 15 min ≈ 13 hours/store/year; monthly effectiveness review 2 hours at Merchandising level.

## W608. Store-Level Customer Feedback Collection & Daily CX Pulse Monitoring

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer completes transaction at POS or visits store |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | ~467 transactions/store/day; target ~1–2 feedback responses/store/day via random sampling (2% sample × 5–15% response, plus QR walk-ins) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Customer Service Counter staff, CX Analyst (HQ) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System randomly selects ~2% of daily transactions for feedback invitation; customer receives SMS/email survey link 2 hours after transaction (configurable); survey is 3-question micro-survey: overall satisfaction (1-5), recommend likelihood (NPS 0-10), free-text comment | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | In-store: Customer Service Counter displays QR code for instant feedback; BOPIS pickup customers receive feedback prompt at counter | CS Counter Staff | — | 1 min |
| 3 | System aggregates daily feedback into CX Pulse dashboard: NPS score, satisfaction rating, sentiment analysis of free-text comments, trending keywords (positive and negative) | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Store Manager reviews CX Pulse dashboard as part of morning routine; focuses on: (a) previous day's NPS change, (b) negative feedback requiring follow-up, (c) recurring themes by department | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 5 | For negative feedback (rating ≤ 2 or NPS detractor): system auto-creates follow-up task assigned to Store Manager; Store Manager reviews specific transaction details and determines appropriate service recovery action per W604 Step 5 | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 6 | Weekly: Store Manager reviews CX Pulse trend in weekly operations review (W575); identifies top 3 customer pain points for the week; assigns corrective actions to Department Supervisors | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 7 | Monthly: CX Analyst generates store CX benchmark report — NPS by store, satisfaction by department, feedback volume, response rate, improvement actions completed; identifies top/bottom 10 stores; distributes to Regional Managers | CX Analyst | CMO | 4 hours |
| 8 | Quarterly: CX team reviews feedback pattern analysis feeding into systemic improvement workflow (W507); adjusts survey questions based on current CX priorities; reviews survey fatigue metrics and adjusts sampling rate | CX Analyst | CMO | 2 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP CRM module: customer feedback collection, NPS calculation, sentiment analysis
- POS system: transaction-triggered survey invitation
- SMS/email gateway: automated survey delivery
- BI dashboard: CX Pulse daily scorecard, trend analysis, benchmarking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Low response rate**: typical retail SMS survey response rate is 5-15%; mitigated by loyalty points incentive for survey completion and in-store QR code alternative
- **Survey fatigue**: customers who shop frequently may receive multiple survey invitations; mitigated by 30-day cooldown period per customer and rotating survey methods
- **Negative feedback overwhelm**: high-volume stores may generate many negative responses requiring follow-up; mitigated by automated triage (only responses mentioning specific issues trigger follow-up tasks)
- **Action closure gap**: collecting feedback without acting on it erodes customer trust; mitigated by weekly corrective action assignment and monthly action completion tracking


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "Counter displays QR code for instant feedback" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-review of "reviews CX Pulse dashboard as part of morning routine" (replaces manual Step 4).
- System auto-create of "creates follow-up task assigned to Store Manager" (replaces manual Step 5).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: loyalty points incentive for survey completion and in-store QR code alternative; operational: 30-day cooldown period per customer and rotating survey methods; operational: automated triage (only responses mentioning specific issues trigger follow-up tasks)

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~15 min/day on CX Pulse review and follow-up = ~1.5 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- **CX Analyst (HQ)**: ~4 hours/month on reporting + ~2 hours/quarter on review = ~4.5 hours/month. Absorbed within existing CX team role.

### Time Estimate
~16 min per store-day (QR counter display 1 min + CX Pulse dashboard review 5 min + negative-feedback follow-up 10 min per flagged response) ≈ 97 hours/store/year ≈ 19,500 hours/year chain-wide; weekly trend review 10 min ≈ 8.7 hours/store/year; monthly benchmark report 4 hours and quarterly pattern analysis 2 hours at CX-team level (sampling and aggregation automated).

## W740. Store-Level Customer Complaint On-the-Spot Resolution & Floor Staff Empowerment

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer expresses dissatisfaction to any floor associate or department supervisor |
| **Frequency** | Daily across 200 stores; estimated 200–400 on-the-spot resolutions/day chain-wide |
| **Volume** | ~1–2 per store per day (200–400/day chain-wide — 80%+ of all customer issues resolved at floor level before the ~2,000–3,000/month formal W41 complaints) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Floor Associate, Department Supervisor, Store Manager (escalation), Customer |

### Background

Not all customer issues should escalate to the formal complaint process (W41). BuildRight Depot empowers floor associates and department supervisors with resolution authority to address common issues immediately — product availability, pricing discrepancies, minor defects, slow service, and product knowledge gaps. Resolving issues on the spot improves customer satisfaction and reduces the burden on formal complaint handling.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Issue identification — Floor associate observes or is informed of customer dissatisfaction; determines issue category (pricing, availability, quality, service, policy) | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 min |
| 2 | Empathy & acknowledgment — Associate acknowledges the concern using trained empathy phrases; assures customer the issue will be addressed immediately | Floor Associate | — | 1 min |
| 3 | Issue assessment — Associate determines if within their resolution authority: pricing discrepancy ≤ PHP 500, product exchange of same value, rain check for out-of-stock, fetching item from backroom, calling department expert | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 min |
| 4 | Resolution execution — If within authority: apply resolution immediately (price adjustment via W5B, exchange via W12A, rain check issuance, expedited retrieval); if outside authority, escalate to Step 5 | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 5 | Supervisor escalation — For issues exceeding associate authority: pricing discrepancy > PHP 500, return without receipt, defective high-value item, policy exception request — Department Supervisor applies resolution using extended authority matrix | Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 6 | Store Manager escalation — For issues exceeding supervisor authority (refund > PHP 5,000, safety concern, discrimination complaint, threat of DTI escalation) — escalate to Store Manager | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Resolution logging — Regardless of resolution level, associate logs the issue in the system: category, resolution type, time to resolve, customer satisfaction indicator (satisfied/neutral/dissatisfied) | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 min |
| 8 | Pattern flagging — System aggregates daily on-the-spot resolutions; flags recurring issues (same SKU pricing error, same department service complaints) for root cause action via W507 | System / Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | Automated |

### System Touchpoints

- Resolution authority matrix configured per role in ERP (associate vs. supervisor vs. manager thresholds)
- Quick price adjustment function at POS linked to W5B with authorization tracking
- Rain check issuance module with auto-restock notification to W596
- On-the-spot resolution logging form (mobile-friendly, < 60 seconds to complete)
- W507 complaint root cause analysis — pattern data feed from on-the-spot resolutions
- W608 customer feedback pulse — daily satisfaction data feed

### Pain Points / Risks

- Over-empowerment risk — associates granting excessive discounts or unauthorized exceptions erode margin
- Under-empowerment risk — associates refusing to resolve minor issues lead to formal complaints and DTI escalations that cost 10× more to resolve
- Resolution logging compliance — busy associates may skip logging, creating blind spots in W507 pattern analysis
- Inconsistent resolution quality across 200 stores without standardized training and authority matrices


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-issue of "Issue identification — Floor associate observes or is informed of customer…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "check for out-of-stock, fetching item from backroom, calling department expert" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-escalate of "escalate to Step 5" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Time Estimate

2–15 minutes per resolution depending on escalation level. Most resolved at Step 4 in under 5 minutes.

### Staffing Implication

No incremental headcount. Resolutions are absorbed within existing floor associate and supervisor duties. ~200–400 resolutions/day × 5 min avg = ~17–33 staff-hours/day, distributed across existing floor staff. Training investment required for resolution authority and empathy training (1 day per associate, refreshed annually per W518).

---

## W744. Store-Level Special Order Follow-Up & Proactive Customer Notification

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Special order (W545) status change (item shipped, item received at store, item ready for pickup, order overdue) |
| **Frequency** | Daily; triggered by open special orders |
| **Volume** | ~10–25 active special orders per store at any time; ~300–500 status changes/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | CSR, Stock Associate, Customer, Supply Chain (W4/W22) |

### Background

Special orders (non-stock items, custom orders, items from other stores) represent a significant revenue stream for a hardware retailer. Unlike routine replenishment, special orders have longer lead times and higher customer anxiety. Proactive follow-up and notification ensures customer satisfaction, reduces abandoned orders, and prevents the "I forgot about my order" problem that leads to unclaimed merchandise and revenue loss.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Daily open order review — Department Supervisor reviews all open special orders in their department sorted by age; system highlights orders approaching or past promised delivery date | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 2 | Supply chain status check — For orders with pending fulfillment, Supervisor checks shipment status via W250 control tower or W4 replenishment tracker; contacts DC or supplying store if no update | Department Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 3 | Customer proactive notification — For significant status changes (item shipped, item received, ready for pickup, delay notification), system sends automated SMS/email to customer; Supervisor calls for high-value orders (> PHP 10,000) | System / Department Supervisor | — | Automated + 5 min per high-value |
| 4 | Received item inspection — When special order arrives at store (W109), Stock Associate inspects item condition, verifies against order specifications (correct model, color, size, quantity) | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 10 min |
| 5 | Customer pickup scheduling — Supervisor contacts customer to confirm pickup date/time; for bulky items, coordinates carry-out assistance (W438) or home delivery scheduling (W5D) | Department Supervisor | — | 5 min |
| 6 | Overdue order escalation — Orders past promised date by > 5 business days auto-escalate to Store Manager; Store Manager contacts customer with revised estimate and service recovery offer | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Unclaimed order management — Orders not picked up within 14 days of notification: system sends final notice; after 21 days, order cancellation initiated per W545 cancellation policy; deposit retained per terms | System / CSR | Store Manager | Automated + 5 min |
| 8 | Monthly special order analytics — System generates report: fill rate, average lead time, on-time pickup rate, cancellation rate, top categories; Store Manager reviews with team during W575 weekly review | System / Store Manager | Store Manager | Automated + 15 min review |

### System Touchpoints

- Special order tracking module with real-time status and age-based prioritization
- W250 supply chain control tower — shipment status integration
- Automated customer notification engine (SMS/email) triggered by status changes
- W109 store receiving — arrival trigger for special orders
- W545 special order — parent workflow for order creation
- W5D / W438 — delivery and carry-out coordination
- Deposit management linked to W754 customer deposit aging

### Pain Points / Risks

- Customer frustration from lack of proactive communication — the #1 complaint for special orders in retail
- Items arriving damaged or incorrect after long wait — the damage/return process (W91/W12) is especially frustrating for customers who waited weeks
- Unclaimed orders consuming valuable backroom storage — 14/21-day unclaimed policy must be enforced consistently
- Special order visibility gap — Department Supervisors managing 10–25 open orders (per the Volume) need system prioritization to prevent overdue orders from falling through cracks


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "review — Department Supervisor reviews all open special orders in their…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "check — For orders with pending fulfillment, Supervisor checks shipment status…" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-send of "sends automated SMS/email to customer" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: prioritization to prevent overdue orders from falling through cracks

### Time Estimate

Daily review: 15 min. Status checks: 10 min. Customer notifications: 5 min per high-value × ~5 per day = 25 min. Received item inspection: 10 min × ~1 per day (15–25 special orders/store/month) = ~10 min. Total daily per store: ~60–70 minutes.

### Staffing Implication

Department Supervisors absorb follow-up duties within existing roles. 200 stores × 60–90 min/day = ~200–300 staff-hours/day across the chain. High-volume stores (near construction sites, with large trade customer base) may need dedicated CSR for special order management.

## W776. Store-Level Product Recall Customer Notification Execution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Product recall activated per W29; customer notification required |
| **Frequency** | Ad-hoc; ~2–4 product recalls per year requiring customer notification |
| **Volume** | Per recall: ~100–5,000 affected customer transactions depending on SKU velocity |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, CSR, LP Analyst, Customer, HQ Compliance |

### Background
When a product recall is activated per W29, BuildRight Depot must notify customers who purchased the affected product. While W29 manages the recall process at the enterprise level and W627 handles recall effectiveness verification, this workflow addresses the store-level execution of customer notification — identifying affected customers from POS transaction history, contacting them, and coordinating product return or exchange. Philippine regulations (DTI, FDA, DENR depending on product type) and corporate liability management require documented customer notification efforts.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Recall notification receipt**: Store Manager receives recall notification from HQ Compliance per W29: (a) affected SKU(s), (b) recall reason and risk level, (c) affected date range of sales, (d) required customer action (return, dispose, exchange), (e) customer notification template approved by Legal | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 2 | **Affected transaction identification**: System queries POS transaction history per store for affected SKU within specified date range: (a) transaction date/time, (b) customer identification (loyalty member, trade account, or anonymous cash sale), (c) quantity purchased, (d) payment method for refund reference | System | Store Manager | Automated + 15 min review |
| 3 | **Loyalty member notification**: For identified loyalty members: (a) system sends notification via W708 (SMS, email, app push) using approved template, (b) notification includes: product description and image, recall reason, risk level, required action, return instructions, refund/exchange options, store contact number, (c) system tracks notification delivery status | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Trade account notification**: For identified trade/B2B accounts: (a) Store Manager calls Key Account Manager for direct customer notification per W617, (b) notification logged in customer account per CRM, (c) return/exchange logistics coordinated (bulk pickup or store return) | Store Manager | Sales Manager | 1-2 hours |
| 5 | **In-store recall signage**: CSR posts recall notice at: (a) store entrance, (b) customer service counter, (c) product's former shelf location (with "RECALLED — Do Not Sell" sign), (d) store social media pages; notice includes product image, recall reason, and action instructions | CSR | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 6 | **Customer return processing**: Customers returning recalled products: (a) CSR processes return per W12 with special recall reason code, (b) no receipt required for recalled items, (c) full refund regardless of item condition (safety recall), (d) system flags return as recall-related for W627 effectiveness tracking | CSR | — | 5-10 min/return |
| 7 | **Daily recall status reporting**: Store Manager reports daily to HQ Compliance: (a) customers notified vs. total affected, (b) returns received, (c) notification delivery failures, (d) customer inquiries or complaints about the recall | Store Manager | — | 15 min/day during recall |
| 8 | **Recall close-out**: When recall period ends per W627: (a) Store Manager confirms all customer notification attempts completed, (b) removes in-store recall signage, (c) files recall documentation per W256 retention policy, (d) submits final store-level recall effectiveness report | Store Manager | HQ Compliance | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints

- W29 product recall execution — recall activation and management
- W627 product recall effectiveness verification — recall tracking and effectiveness measurement
- POS transaction history — affected customer identification by SKU and date range
- W708 customer communication management — automated and manual customer notifications
- W12 returns & exchanges — recall-specific return processing
- W540 BIR invoice management — refund documentation for recalled items
- W256 document retention — recall documentation archival
- W476 BFP fire safety / W479 FDA / W447 DTI-BPS — regulatory agency notification integration

### Pain Points / Risks

- Anonymous transaction gap — cash sales without loyalty identification (~40% of transactions) cannot be directly notified; in-store signage and media coverage are the only outreach channels
- Customer panic — overly alarming recall notifications may create disproportionate customer concern; notification language must be reviewed by Legal and balanced with safety obligation
- Return volume surge — high-velocity recalled items may generate significant return volume; store staffing may need temporary adjustment
- Recall fatigue — frequent recalls (especially for low-risk items) may desensitize customers to notifications; recall communication must be calibrated to risk level
- Regulatory documentation — DTI/FDA/DENR may require proof of customer notification efforts; detailed logging of all notification attempts essential for W627 effectiveness verification


### Automation Opportunity
- System rule-based auto-approval of "approved by Legal" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "account, or anonymous cash sale" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-send of "sends notification via W708" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Store Manager review and approval gate

### Time Estimate

Per recall (store-level): setup and notification (2-4 hours) + daily reporting (15 min/day × recall duration) + return processing (absorbed in W12) + close-out (30 min) = ~4–8 hours over recall period.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing Store Manager and CSR duties. Per recall: ~4–8 hours concentrated at activation. No incremental headcount.

## W897. Store-Level Trade Professional Verification & Pro Badge Issuance for Discount Program

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Trade professional (contractor, electrician, plumber, interior designer, architect, carpenter) applies for BuildRight Pro discount program at store Pro Desk or online |
| **Frequency** | ~200–300 new Pro badge applications/month chain-wide; ~1,500–2,000 badge renewals/year |
| **Volume** | 1 applicant per verification session |
| **Owner** | Pro Desk Coordinator (Department Supervisor) |
| **Participants** | Applicant (Trade Professional), Pro Desk Coordinator, Store Manager, HR (PCAB/PRC license verification), Loyalty Program Manager |

### Background

BuildRight's trade customers (30% of revenue) receive 5–15% trade discount per the pricing structure in the model company profile. While W24 covers trade account credit applications and W112 covers trade counter operations, the initial verification that a walk-in customer genuinely qualifies as a trade professional — and the issuance of a physical/digital Pro Badge that unlocks trade pricing at POS — is a distinct gap. Currently, trade pricing is tied to the credit/trade account (W24), but many legitimate trade professionals (especially sole-proprietor electricians, freelance carpenters, small-time masons) may not want or qualify for a full credit account — they pay cash or card but still expect trade pricing. This workflow establishes a "Pro Badge" program: a verified credential (physical card + digital wallet pass) that any qualified trade professional can obtain, which automatically triggers trade pricing at POS without requiring a credit account. Distinct from: (a) W24 trade credit application (financial product), (b) W696 contractor safety badge (site safety), (c) W263 loyalty enrollment (consumer program). The Pro Badge is a discount credential, not a credit instrument or safety badge.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Application Intake**: Trade professional presents at Pro Desk (W112) or applies online: (a) full name, contact number, email; (b) trade category: contractor, electrician, plumber, mason, carpenter, painter, welder, interior designer, architect, landscape professional, other; (c) employment type: sole proprietor, small business (1–5 employees), medium business (6–20 employees), large business (20+); (d) primary project type: residential renovation, commercial construction, institutional, general; (e) government-issued ID presented (in-store) or uploaded (online) | Applicant / Pro Desk Coordinator | Pro Desk Coordinator | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Professional Credential Verification**: Pro Desk Coordinator verifies applicant qualifies as a trade professional: (a) **PCAB (Philippine Contractors Accreditation Board) license**: for contractors — verify license number via PCAB online portal; (b) **PRC (Professional Regulation Commission) license**: for engineers, architects, interior designers — verify license number and status (active/inactive) via PRC online verification; (c) **TESDA NC (National Certificate)**: for electricians, plumbers, welders, carpenters, masons — verify NC II/III certificate number via TESDA registry; (d) **DTI Business Registration**: for sole proprietors and small businesses — verify DTI registration; (e) **Alternative verification** (if no license/certificate): (i) present 3 recent project photos with business card or company signage visible; (ii) provide 2 supplier invoices from other hardware/building material suppliers showing trade purchases; (iii) provide barangay business clearance; (f) verification result: Approved (valid credential), Conditionally Approved (alternative verification — 6-month provisional badge), or Rejected (insufficient evidence) | Pro Desk Coordinator | Store Manager | 10–20 min |
| 3 | **Badge Issuance**: Upon approval: (a) system creates Pro Badge record linked to loyalty account (new or existing); (b) Pro Badge level assigned: (i) **Blue Badge** — standard trade professional: 5% trade discount on eligible categories; (ii) **Silver Badge** — licensed professional (PCAB, PRC, TESDA NC III): 10% trade discount; (iii) **Gold Badge** — large contractor/business with PCAB Specialty license or A category: 15% trade discount; (c) physical Pro Badge card printed in-store (standard template — name, badge level, expiry date, barcode); (d) digital Pro Badge issued to mobile app wallet (Apple Wallet / Google Pay integration via W615); (e) badge expiry: 1 year from issuance; (f) system activates trade pricing for the badge level in POS; (g) applicant receives welcome kit: Pro Badge card, trade pricing guide by category, Pro Desk contact card, loyalty enrollment if not already member (W263) | Pro Desk Coordinator / System | Store Manager | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **POS Integration & Usage**: At checkout: (a) cashier scans Pro Badge barcode or customer presents digital badge; (b) system automatically applies trade discount per badge level to eligible categories (trade discount does not apply to: appliances, clearance items, services, gift cards); (c) trade discount stacks with quantity break pricing (POS-010) but NOT with promotional pricing — system applies best price for customer; (d) if customer also has a trade credit account (W24): Pro Badge is linked to trade account for consolidated statements and credit limit management; (e) transaction logged against Pro Badge for purchase analytics | Cashier / System | — | Automated at POS |
| 5 | **Annual Renewal**: (a) system sends renewal reminder 30 days before badge expiry via SMS/email/app; (b) trade professional visits any BuildRight store with updated credentials; (c) Pro Desk Coordinator re-verifies: (i) license/registration still active; (ii) purchase activity in prior year (minimum: 1 purchase in last 12 months to maintain active status); (d) badge renewal assessment: (i) if purchase volume ≥ PHP 100K/year: eligible for badge level upgrade; (ii) if purchase volume < PHP 100K/year: maintain current badge level; (iii) if no purchases in 12 months: badge downgraded to Blue (5%) or not renewed; (e) renewed badge printed/digital updated; new expiry date set | Pro Desk Coordinator | Store Manager | 10–15 min |
| 6 | **Program Analytics & Abuse Prevention**: (a) monthly: Loyalty Program Manager reviews Pro Badge analytics — active badges, new issuances, renewal rates, avg transaction value by badge level, discount impact on margin; (b) system flags anomalies: (i) Pro Badge used by different individual (photo mismatch at POS via W538 LP monitoring); (ii) unusually high transaction volume suggesting badge sharing; (iii) trade discount applied to personal-use items (appliances, seasonal décor); (c) LP team investigates flagged badges per W837/W839; (d) badge suspension for verified abuse: Store Manager notifies badge holder; badge deactivated in system; holder may reapply after 6-month suspension | Loyalty Program Manager / LP Team | Marketing Manager | 4–6 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints

- Pro Badge master data module (badge number, level, linked loyalty account, expiry date, credential references)
- POS discount engine for automatic trade pricing application
- Loyalty system integration for badge-to-account linkage (W17)
- PCAB online portal for contractor license verification
- PRC online verification system for professional license validation
- TESDA registry for National Certificate verification
- Mobile app wallet integration (W615) for digital badge
- Customer CDP (W156) for trade professional profile
- LP exception monitoring (W837, W838) for badge abuse detection
- BI dashboard for Pro Badge program analytics

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Credential verification burden**: manually verifying PCAB, PRC, and TESDA credentials for 200–300 applications/month is time-consuming; online portal availability is inconsistent for Philippine government systems
- **Alternative verification subjectivity**: the alternative verification pathway (project photos, supplier invoices) requires judgment by Pro Desk Coordinator — inconsistent standards across 200 stores may lead to ineligible badge holders
- **Badge sharing and lending**: a single Pro Badge shared among multiple workers on a construction site dilutes program intent and margin impact; photo verification at POS mitigates but adds checkout time
- **Margin erosion from generous discount tiers**: 15% trade discount on already-thin-margin categories (lumber, cement) may reduce category profitability; discount levels must be reviewed annually against category margin
- **Pro Badge vs. trade account confusion**: customers may not understand the distinction between a Pro Badge (discount credential, no credit) and a trade account (credit facility with pricing); store staff must clearly explain
- **Renewal non-compliance**: trade professionals with expired credentials (e.g., PCAB license not renewed) continuing to use Pro Badge; automated credential re-verification at renewal is essential


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "carpenter, painter, welder, interior designer, architect, landscape professional…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "verify license number via PCAB online portal" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "printed in-store" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Pro Desk Coordinator (Department Supervisor) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication

- **Pro Desk Coordinator**: ~20–30 min per application (intake + verification + issuance); ~100–150 hours/month chain-wide (~30–45 min/store/month); absorbed by Department Supervisor
- **Loyalty Program Manager**: ~4–6 hours/month on program analytics and oversight; absorbed by existing role
- **Store Manager**: ~1–2 hours/month on application approvals and renewal reviews
- **No incremental headcount**.

### Time Estimate

- Application intake: 10–15 min
- Credential verification: 10–20 min (online portal) to 1–2 days (manual verification for alternative pathway)
- Badge issuance: 10–15 min
- POS usage: automated (< 5 sec added to checkout)
- Annual renewal: 10–15 min
- Monthly analytics: 4–6 hours
- **Total per application**: 30–50 min of staff time (standard pathway)
