## W913. Store-Level Emergency Generator Fuel Reserve Management & DOE Compliance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily fuel level monitoring; also triggered by PAGASA typhoon advisory (W576), rotational brownout schedule (W470), or fuel reorder point |
| **Frequency** | Daily monitoring; fuel delivery ~2–4 times/month per store; 200 stores × ~2–4 deliveries/month = ~400–800 fuel deliveries/month chain-wide |
| **Volume** | Average 500–1,000 liters diesel per store per month; 8-hour reserve = ~200–270 liters per store (at the 25–33 L/h rate) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Stock Associate (fuel handling), Fuel Vendor, CIT (fuel delivery), Facilities Maintenance (generator service W808), Regional Manager (emergency escalation) |

### Background

Philippine electricity reliability varies significantly across BuildRight's 200-store footprint. Provincial stores experience rotational brownouts (scheduled power outages) 2–4 times per week, some lasting 4–8 hours. Each BuildRight store has a standby generator (per W808) to maintain POS operations, lighting, and critical systems (CCTV, security alarms, refrigeration for paint/chemicals) during outages. However, generator fuel management is often neglected — stores run out of diesel during extended outages, forcing manual operations (W580) or closure. Typhoons compound the problem: widespread outages may last 24–72 hours, during which fuel stations also lose power and cannot dispense diesel. This workflow formalizes daily generator fuel reserve management across 200 stores, ensuring minimum 8-hour fuel reserve at all times (per NFR-011 POS offline requirement), with emergency reserves during typhoon season. DOE (Department of Energy) compliance for diesel storage at retail locations is also addressed.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Daily Fuel Level Monitoring**: (a) Stock Associate performs daily morning generator fuel level check using dipstick or digital gauge; (b) fuel level recorded in store operations mobile app with: current liters, generator run hours since last refuel, estimated remaining runtime at typical load (POS + CCTV + lighting + refrigeration = ~60–70% generator load = ~6–8 hours per 200 liters); (c) system compares current fuel level against minimum reserve threshold: Green (≥8-hour reserve), Amber (4–8 hour reserve), Red (<4-hour reserve); (d) Amber: auto-generates fuel delivery order to contracted fuel vendor; (e) Red: urgent fuel delivery request + Store Manager notification + Regional Manager alert; (f) daily fuel data consolidated in store KPI dashboard per W665 | Stock Associate / System | Store Manager | 5 min/day |
| 2 | **Fuel Replenishment & Delivery**: (a) standard replenishment: fuel vendor delivers diesel in 200-liter drums or via mini-tanker to store; (b) delivery scheduling: system auto-schedules delivery when fuel drops below Amber threshold; delivery within 24 hours (metro), 48 hours (provincial); (c) delivery receiving: Stock Associate verifies delivery quantity (dipstick reading before and after, or flow meter reading from tanker), checks fuel quality (visual inspection — no water contamination), and signs delivery receipt; (d) fuel storage: diesel stored in approved container(s) per DOE guidelines — properly labeled, ventilated area, away from ignition sources, with spill containment per W238 hazmat spill protocol; (e) delivery reconciliation: delivery receipt entered in system against PO; 3-way match per W7 for fuel vendor invoice; (f) emergency fuel procurement: if contracted vendor cannot deliver within SLA (e.g., during typhoon), Store Manager authorized to purchase from nearest fuel station using petty cash per W25 with receipt | Stock Associate / Fuel Vendor / System | Store Manager | 30–45 min per delivery |
| 3 | **Typhoon Season Fuel Surge Protocol**: (a) PAGASA Signal No. 1 advisory for store location (per W576): system triggers fuel surge order — additional 400 liters (total reserve: ~18–24 hours at the 6–8 h/200 L rate) above minimum; (b) fuel vendor pre-positions surge deliveries within 24 hours of Signal No. 1; (c) Signal No. 2: fuel reserve increased to 24-hour capacity (600–800 liters); (d) post-typhoon: extended outage areas receive priority fuel delivery coordination from Regional Manager; (e) fuel expense tracking during typhoon events for insurance claim documentation per W858 (business interruption claim requires proof of extra expenses incurred); (f) annual: pre-typhoon season (May) fuel vendor capacity confirmation and surge delivery SLA review | Store Manager / Fuel Vendor / Regional Manager | VP Store Operations | 30 min per event |
| 4 | **Generator Fuel Consumption & Efficiency Monitoring**: (a) system tracks: fuel consumption rate (liters/hour) per generator load level, total monthly fuel cost per store, generator runtime hours, and outage frequency/duration per store; (b) monthly: fuel consumption benchmarking across 200 stores (liters/month, liters/run-hour, cost/store); stores consuming >20% above benchmark flagged for generator maintenance per W808 (dirty fuel filters, aging generators consume more fuel); (c) quarterly: fuel vendor performance review — delivery reliability, price competitiveness, fuel quality; fuel cost per store integrated into store operating budget per W489; (d) annual: generator fuel cost trend analysis and generator replacement ROI (new efficient generator vs. continued high fuel cost of aging unit) per W799 vehicle/equipment lifecycle logic | System / Store Manager / Facilities Maintenance | VP Store Operations | 2–3 hours/month |
| 5 | **DOE & Environmental Compliance**: (a) DENR-EMB Permit to Operate (PTO) for standby generators per W477 (air-pollution source equipment under RA 8749/Clean Air Act IRR; DOE governs fuel, not equipment PTOs) maintained for each store with >500 liters diesel storage; (b) fuel storage compliance: containers meet DOE safety standards (proper labeling, fire extinguisher within 5 meters, no smoking signage, spill containment); (c) emissions: generator exhaust emissions checked during quarterly maintenance per W808; DOE emission standards compliance; (d) fuel purchase records retained for 10 years per BIR/NIRC requirements (Sec. 235 as amended by TRAIN) (fuel expense for tax deduction per W590); (e) DENR spill contingency: minor spill (<5 liters) — sand/absorbent cleanup per W238; major spill (>5 liters) — DENR notification within 24 hours per W433; (f) annual: DOE compliance self-assessment integrated into W506 unified compliance calendar | Store Manager / Facilities Maintenance | VP Store Operations | 2–3 hours/year |

### System Touchpoints

- Store operations mobile app for daily fuel level recording
- Generator telemetry (if available) for automated fuel level and runtime monitoring
- PO system for automated fuel delivery order generation
- Petty cash system (W25) for emergency fuel purchases
- Typhoon alert integration (W576) for surge protocol trigger
- Store KPI dashboard (W665) for fuel metrics
- Generator maintenance system (W808) for fuel efficiency correlation
- Facility compliance module (W477) for DENR-EMB generator PTO tracking
- Environmental compliance system (W433/W238) for spill reporting
- Financial system for fuel expense tracking and budget allocation (W489)
- BI dashboard for fuel consumption benchmarking

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Fuel theft**: diesel is a target for theft from storage containers; tamper-evident container seals, daily reconciliation (dipstick vs. expected consumption based on runtime), and CCTV coverage of fuel storage area per W838 mitigate; discrepancy >5% triggers LP investigation per W842
- **Fuel degradation**: diesel stored >6 months degrades (microbial growth, water accumulation); first-in-first-out rotation and maximum 3-month storage duration per container mitigate; fuel quality check at delivery
- **Vendor delivery reliability during typhoons**: fuel vendors may be unable to deliver during widespread disasters; pre-positioning at Signal No. 1 (before roads close) and dual-vendor strategy per store mitigate; regional fuel reserve arrangement with 3PL fuel tanker
- **Environmental liability**: diesel spills from storage containers or during delivery create DENR reporting obligations and potential fines; spill containment infrastructure, staff training per W238, and vendor liability clause in fuel delivery contract per W688 mitigate
- **Inconsistent manual monitoring**: Stock Associate may forget or skip daily fuel check; system-generated daily task per W554 replenishment checklist with Store Manager non-compliance escalation; automated telemetry (where generator supports) preferred


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-record of "recorded in store operations mobile app" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-capture of "entered in system against PO" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-document of "documentation per W858" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Store Manager review and approval gate; operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Staffing Implication

- **Stock Associate**: ~5 min/day for fuel level check + ~30–45 min per fuel delivery (2–4/month); absorbed by existing role
- **Store Manager**: ~15 min/day on fuel status review during typhoon season; absorbed by existing role
- **Facilities Maintenance (HQ)**: ~2–3 hours/quarter on generator fuel efficiency analysis per W808; absorbed by existing role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate

- Daily fuel level check: 5 min
- Fuel delivery receiving: 30–45 min
- Typhoon surge protocol: 30 min per event
- Monthly fuel analytics: 2–3 hours (consolidated)
- Annual DOE compliance: 2–3 hours
- **Total per store per month**: ~3–4 hours of staff time (monitoring + deliveries)

## W916. Customer Trade-In & Used Power Tool Buy-Back Program

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer brings used power tool to store for trade-in evaluation; or customer requests trade-in quote via ecommerce/app |
| **Frequency** | ~150–250 trade-in transactions/month across 200 stores (~1 per store per month) |
| **Volume** | Average trade-in value: PHP 1,500–8,000 per unit; ~200 units/month |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (power tools department), Department Supervisor (Tools/Hardware), Store Manager, Merchandising (used equipment pricing), Finance (store credit issuance) |

### Background

Power tools represent ~5% of active SKUs (1,750 SKUs) and are among the highest-value items BuildRight sells. Customers — especially professional contractors and serious DIYers — frequently upgrade to newer models, creating a secondary market for their used equipment. A trade-in/buy-back program serves multiple strategic purposes: (a) it incentivizes new power tool purchases by reducing the effective cost through trade-in credit; (b) it creates a "certified pre-owned" revenue stream from refurbished units sold at a discount to price-sensitive customers; (c) it drives foot traffic and positions BuildRight as a full-lifecycle partner for tool owners; and (d) it differentiates BuildRight from competitors who do not offer such programs. The program covers power drills, grinders, circular saws, sanders, impact drivers, and compressors from major brands (Bosch, Makita, DeWalt, Milwaukee). Hand tools are excluded (low residual value). Each store processes roughly one trade-in per month, with the refurbished inventory sold through the store's "certified pre-owned tools" display section.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Trade-In Request & Initial Assessment**: (a) customer brings used power tool to Tools/Hardware department with proof of purchase (if available) or identification; (b) Sales Associate performs initial assessment: brand, model, age (estimated), cosmetic condition (A/B/C/D grade), functional test (power on, run for 30 seconds, check for unusual noise/vibration), accessories included (case, charger, batteries, blades); (c) system lookup: tool brand/model registered in item master; system displays original SRP, current new price, and estimated trade-in value range based on condition matrix (A: 30–40% of current new price; B: 20–30%; C: 10–20%; D: 0–10% or declined); (d) if tool is not in item master (obscure/discontinued brand), Sales Associate manually estimates condition and flags for Department Supervisor review; (e) safety check: tool inspected for damaged cords, cracked housings, missing guards — tools failing safety check are declined for trade-in and customer advised on proper disposal per W502 | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Trade-In Valuation & Offer**: (a) Department Supervisor confirms condition assessment and verifies trade-in value against system-recommended range; (b) for trade-in values > PHP 5,000: Store Manager approval required; (c) customer presented with trade-in offer: "Your [tool] has been assessed at [condition grade]. Trade-in value: PHP [X,XXX]. This value can be applied as store credit toward any new power tool purchase today"; (d) customer options: (i) accept trade-in credit toward new purchase immediately (most common — 70% of cases); (ii) accept trade-in as store credit voucher valid for 30 days; (iii) decline trade-in and retain tool; (e) if customer accepts: system generates trade-in receipt (non-BIR sales invoice — it is a goods receipt for used inventory) documenting tool description, serial number, condition grade, and trade-in value; (f) store credit or discount applied to new purchase transaction at POS per W5B | Department Supervisor / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Used Tool Intake & Inventory Registration**: (a) traded-in tool registered in ERP as "certified pre-owned" inventory with: original SKU reference, condition grade, serial number, trade-in value (carrying cost), date received, store location; (b) non-valuated receipt — tool is carried at trade-in value (not original SRP) in a separate "pre-owned" inventory pool per store; (c) tool tagged with pre-owned barcode label and moved to backroom staging area; (d) if tool requires refurbishment (cleaning, blade/bit replacement, cord repair, accessory kit addition): Service Work Order created per W544 for in-store minor refurbishment or flagged for DC-level refurbishment per W440 (power tool service center); (e) refurbished cost = trade-in value + refurbishment labor/parts cost; system updates carrying cost accordingly | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **Certified Pre-Owned Pricing & Display**: (a) Merchandising sets pre-owned selling price: condition A = 50–60% of new SRP; condition B = 40–50%; condition C = 30–40%; (b) margin target: minimum 25% gross margin over refurbished cost; system validates pricing against margin floor; (c) pre-owned tool listed on ecommerce with "Certified Pre-Owned" badge, condition description, warranty terms (90-day BuildRight warranty vs. standard manufacturer warranty on new), and photos; (d) in-store: pre-owned tools displayed in dedicated section within Tools/Hardware department with clear "Certified Pre-Owned — Inspected & Tested" signage; (e) price tag includes condition grade, original SRP comparison, and pre-owned price per W181 | Department Supervisor / Merchandising | VP Merchandising | 15–20 min per unit |
| 5 | **Pre-Owned Tool Sale & Warranty Processing**: (a) pre-owned tool sold through standard POS flow per W5B with pre-owned SKU; (b) POS applies standard VAT (12%) on pre-owned selling price; (c) customer receives: sales receipt, 90-day warranty card, condition assessment report, and return policy (7-day return for functional defects, no cosmetic returns); (d) warranty claims processed per W33 with pre-owned warranty pool fund; (e) if pre-owned tool is unsold after 90 days: markdown to 70% of current price per W93; after 180 days: clearance at cost recovery or donate to vocational school per W135 CSR program | Sales Associate / Store Manager | Store Manager | Standard POS transaction |
| 6 | **Program Analytics & Inventory Management**: (a) monthly: trade-in count, average trade-in value, new tool attachment rate (what % of trade-ins result in new purchase), pre-owned sell-through rate, pre-owned inventory aging, margin analysis (new tool margin + pre-owned margin vs. standalone new tool sale); (b) quarterly: program ROI analysis — incremental new tool revenue generated by trade-in program vs. trade-in credits issued + refurbishment costs + display space opportunity cost; (c) brand-level analysis: which brands have highest trade-in volume (indicates upgrade cycle) and which pre-owned brands sell fastest (indicates secondary market demand); (d) annual: program expansion review — eligible categories (garden power tools, pressure washers), pricing matrix calibration, and vendor partnership (major brands co-funding trade-in promotions) | Store Manager / Merchandising / System | VP Merchandising | 4–6 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints

- POS system (W5B) for trade-in receipt generation and store credit application
- Inventory management for pre-owned inventory pool with separate carrying cost
- Item master (W252) for pre-owned SKU creation with condition-grade variants
- Service work order system (W544) for refurbishment tracking
- Pricing system for pre-owned pricing matrix and margin validation
- Ecommerce platform for "Certified Pre-Owned" listing with condition details
- Loyalty system (W17) for points earning on pre-owned purchases
- Warranty system (W33) for 90-day pre-owned warranty claims
- Markdown/clearance system (W93) for aged pre-owned inventory
- BI dashboard for program analytics

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Valuation disputes**: customers may disagree with trade-in value; mitigated by system-generated value range based on condition matrix, and escalation to Store Manager for discretionary adjustment up to 10% above recommended range
- **Safety liability**: pre-owned tools may have latent defects not caught during assessment; mitigated by mandatory functional test, 90-day warranty with clear exclusion of consequential damages, and product liability insurance per W185
- **Refurbishment cost overrun**: repair costs may exceed margin; mitigated by refurbishment cost cap at 50% of trade-in value — tools exceeding cap are recycled/disposed per W502
- **Channel conflict with vendors**: major brands may object to pre-owned sales cannibalizing new tool sales; mitigated by vendor communication that trade-in program drives new tool purchases (70% attachment rate) and builds brand loyalty
- **Inventory shrinkage**: high-value pre-owned tools may be theft targets; mitigated by EAS tagging per W844, locked display case for tools > PHP 5,000, and CCTV coverage per W838


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "manually estimates condition and flags for Department Supervisor review" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-document of "documenting tool description, serial number, condition grade, and trade-in value" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-create of "created per W544 for in-store minor refurbishment or flagged for DC-level…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: system-generated value range based on condition matrix, and escalation to Store Manager for discretionary adjustment up to 10% above recommended range; operational: mandatory functional test, 90-day warranty with clear exclusion of consequential damages, and product liability insurance per W185; operational: refurbishment cost cap at 50% of trade-in value — tools exceeding cap are recycled/disposed per W502

### Staffing Implication

- **Sales Associate (Tools/Hardware)**: ~15–20 min per trade-in assessment; ~1 per month ≈ under 1 hour per month per store; absorbed by existing role
- **Department Supervisor**: ~10 min per trade-in confirmation; absorbed by existing role
- **Store Manager**: ~5 min per high-value trade-in approval; absorbed by existing role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate

- Trade-in assessment and valuation: 15–25 min
- Inventory registration and tagging: 10–15 min
- Refurbishment: 30 min–2 hours (varies by condition)
- Pricing and display: 15–20 min
- Monthly program analytics: 2–3 hours (consolidated)
- **Total per trade-in**: ~45–60 min of staff time (excluding refurbishment)

## W920. Store-Level Vendor-Led Product Knowledge Training & Staff Certification Program

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New product introduction (W564); seasonal category transition (W264); new vendor onboarding (W36); quarterly training calendar cycle; or store-level skill gap identified via W567 cross-training assessment |
| **Frequency** | ~4–6 vendor training sessions per store per month; 200 stores × 5 sessions = ~800–1,000 sessions/month chain-wide |
| **Volume** | Average 8–12 store staff per session; ~6,400–12,000 staff-attendances/month (~3,200–12,000 training-hours at 30–60 min/session) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (host) |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisors, Sales Associates, Vendor Representative/Trainer, HR Training Coordinator, Category Manager (content approval) |

### Background

BuildRight carries 35,000 active SKUs across highly technical categories: electrical wiring and circuit breakers, plumbing fixtures and pipe specifications, paint chemistry and color matching, power tool features and safety, tile material science (ceramic vs. porcelain vs. granite), and lumber grades and treatment specifications. Unlike grocery or fashion retail where product knowledge is intuitive, hardware/DIY retail demands deep technical understanding — a customer asking "what wire gauge for a 20-amp circuit?" or "which tile adhesive for outdoor use in a rainy climate?" needs an informed answer. Incorrect advice can cause safety hazards (electrical fire, structural failure) and product liability exposure per W185. Vendor-led product training leverages the technical expertise of BuildRight's ~800–1,000 vendor partners: each vendor knows their products best and can train staff on features, specifications, application, installation, safety, and common customer questions. This workflow formalizes vendor-led training at store level, tracks staff certification per product category, and ensures training coverage across all departments.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Quarterly Training Calendar Planning**: (a) Category Manager identifies training priorities for the quarter based on: new product launches (W564), seasonal transitions (W264), low-scoring categories from previous quarter's mystery shopping (W566), and skill gap analysis from W567 cross-training matrix; (b) HR Training Coordinator invites vendors to schedule training sessions via vendor portal (W865); (c) training calendar created per store cluster: sessions scheduled during off-peak hours (weekday mornings 8:00–9:00 AM before store opening, or during the 2:00–3:00 PM lull); (d) each session allocated 30–60 minutes; (e) calendar distributed to all 200 stores via daily communication system (W571); (f) vendor training materials (product guides, FAQ cards, demonstration units) shipped to stores 1 week before scheduled session | Category Manager / HR Training Coordinator | VP Merchandising | 8–10 hours/quarter |
| 2 | **Vendor Training Session Execution**: (a) Vendor Representative arrives at store per W581 vendor access management; (b) Department Supervisor gathers scheduled staff in training area (breakroom or department floor); (c) Vendor Representative conducts training: product features and benefits (10 min), technical specifications and application scenarios (10 min), demonstration and hands-on practice (15 min), common customer questions and objections (5 min), competitor product comparison (5 min), safety and handling notes (5 min); (d) Sales Associates practice customer interaction using role-play scenarios provided by vendor; (e) Department Supervisor takes attendance in training log (system records: date, vendor, category, topic, attendee list, duration); (f) vendor provides quick-reference cards for department posting | Vendor Representative / Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | 30–60 min |
| 3 | **Post-Training Knowledge Assessment**: (a) within 48 hours of training session, Sales Associates complete 10-question assessment via store mobile app: multiple choice and scenario-based questions covering product features, specification selection, and customer advisory; (b) passing score: 80%; (c) associates scoring < 80% assigned supplementary self-paced review and re-test within 1 week; (d) assessment results recorded in employee skill matrix per W567 with certification status update; (e) Department Supervisor reviews results and identifies associates needing additional coaching; (f) assessment data aggregated for Category Manager quarterly review | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 10 min per associate |
| 4 | **Category Certification & Skill Progression**: (a) associates completing all required training sessions for a product category and passing all assessments earn "Category Certified" status; (b) certification levels: Bronze (basic product knowledge — completed foundational training), Silver (advanced — passed all quarterly updates + mystery shopping score ≥ 85%), Gold (expert — can train other associates + consistent 90%+ assessment scores + zero customer complaints on product advice); (c) certified associates identified by category badge on uniform name tag per W780; (d) certification status linked to: shift scheduling preference (certified associates scheduled during peak hours for their category), performance evaluation per W72, and eligibility for higher-level cross-training per W567; (e) certification expires after 6 months without re-certification (quarterly training sessions maintain active status); (f) annual recognition: top "Gold" certified associates per region recognized in company awards per W630 | Department Supervisor / HR Training Coordinator | Store Manager | 2–3 hours/month |
| 5 | **Vendor Training Effectiveness & Compliance Monitoring**: (a) monthly: training completion rate by store (target: ≥ 95% of scheduled sessions conducted), assessment pass rate, category certification coverage rate; (b) quarterly: correlation analysis between training completion and: (i) customer satisfaction scores per W608 for trained categories, (ii) sales conversion rate in trained departments, (iii) product return rate for trained categories (lower returns indicate better product recommendations); (c) vendor training quality rating: stores rate vendor trainer effectiveness (1–5 scale) after each session; low-rated vendors (< 3.0 average) receive feedback from Category Manager; (d) compliance: training attendance tracked per DOLE-mandated training hours for safety-related categories (electrical, hazmat, power tools); (e) annual: program ROI analysis — training investment (vendor time, staff time, materials) vs. sales improvement and return reduction | HR Training Coordinator / Category Manager / System | CHRO | 4–6 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints

- Vendor portal (W865) for training session scheduling and material distribution
- Store operations mobile app for assessment delivery and attendance logging
- Employee skill matrix (W567) for certification status tracking
- Training management module linked to HR system (W51) for training history
- Employee master (W292) for certification badge and scheduling preference
- Daily communication system (W571) for training calendar distribution
- BI dashboard for training analytics and effectiveness measurement
- Mystery shopping system (W566) for validation of training impact
- Performance management system (W72) for certification-linked evaluation

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Vendor no-show**: vendor representative fails to arrive for scheduled training; mitigated by backup self-paced training module (pre-recorded video accessible on store tablet) and vendor SLA penalty in vendor agreement per W669
- **Peak-hour disruption**: pulling sales associates off the floor during peak hours reduces customer service; mitigated by scheduling training during off-peak hours and limiting session size to avoid full department depopulation
- **Assessment fatigue**: staff may resent frequent testing; mitigated by gamification (leaderboards, team competitions, small rewards per W630), keeping assessments short (10 questions), and linking certification to tangible benefits (scheduling preference, recognition)
- **Knowledge decay**: staff forget training content over time; mitigated by quarterly refresher sessions, quick-reference cards posted in departments, and just-in-time product info accessible via mobile app during customer interactions
- **Training quality inconsistency**: some vendor trainers are excellent, others are poor; mitigated by vendor training effectiveness rating system, Category Manager feedback loop, and standardized training outline provided to vendors


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-create of "created per store cluster" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "Vendor Training Session Execution" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-record of "recorded in employee skill matrix per W567 with certification status update" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: backup self-paced training module (pre-recorded video accessible on store tablet) and vendor SLA penalty in vendor agreement per W669; operational: scheduling training during off-peak hours and limiting session size to avoid full department depopulation; operational: gamification (leaderboards, team competitions, small rewards per W630), keeping assessments short (10 questions), and linking certification to tangible benefits (scheduling preference, recognition)

### Staffing Implication

- **Department Supervisor**: ~2–3 hours/month hosting and administering training sessions; absorbed by existing role
- **Sales Associates**: ~30–60 min/month attending training + 10 min assessment per session = ~1–2 hours/month; absorbed within scheduled shift hours
- **HR Training Coordinator**: ~8–10 hours/quarter on calendar planning + 4–6 hours/month on monitoring; absorbed by existing role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate

- Calendar planning: 8–10 hours/quarter (consolidated)
- Training session: 30–60 min per session
- Assessment: 10 min per associate per session
- Certification management: 2–3 hours/month (consolidated)
- Analytics: 4–6 hours/month (consolidated)
- **Total per store per month**: ~16–72 hours of staff time (4–6 sessions × 8–12 attendees × 30–60 min, plus administration)

## W922. Customer Walk-In Bulk Purchase Negotiation & Volume Pricing Approval

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Walk-in customer (typically contractor, small builder, or institutional buyer) requests volume discount on a multi-unit or high-value purchase at store |
| **Frequency** | ~600–800 bulk negotiation transactions/month across 200 stores (~3–4 per store per month) |
| **Volume** | Average bulk transaction value: PHP 15,000–150,000; ~700 transactions/month |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Department Supervisor, Store Manager (pricing approval), Finance (for credit terms if applicable) |

### Background

BuildRight's customer mix includes 30% trade professionals (contractors, builders, interior designers) and 10% corporate/institutional buyers. Many of these customers walk into stores to purchase materials for active projects and expect volume pricing — a standard practice in the Philippine construction supply industry where negotiated pricing is the norm rather than the exception. A contractor buying 200 bags of cement, 500 hollow blocks, or 50 sheets of plywood for a project expects a discount commensurate with the volume. Currently, these negotiations happen informally and inconsistently — some Sales Associates offer discounts without authorization, others turn away customers by rigidly adhering to SRP, and the lack of a structured workflow creates margin leakage and customer experience inconsistency. This workflow formalizes walk-in bulk purchase negotiation with tiered discount authority, minimum volume thresholds, real-time margin verification, and post-sale analytics to balance revenue optimization with customer satisfaction. It complements the existing trade account pricing (W163) and corporate account pricing (W58) workflows by handling ad-hoc, walk-in bulk transactions that are not covered by pre-negotiated contracts.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Bulk Purchase Request Identification**: (a) customer approaches Sales Associate or Pro Desk (W112) with bulk purchase intent — either a specific product list ("I need 200 bags of cement") or a project description ("I'm building a 2-story house, what do I need?"); (b) Sales Associate assesses request: product(s), quantities, project type (residential, commercial, renovation), customer type (walk-in, loyalty member, trade account holder); (c) if customer is a trade account holder: existing trade discount per W163 applies first; bulk discount applies on top only if volume exceeds trade agreement threshold; (d) Sales Associate enters request into POS quotation module per W542 as a bulk quotation request; (e) customer identified: loyalty card scanned for purchase history and customer tier, or new customer registration offered per W551 | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Volume Threshold & Discount Matrix Verification**: (a) system displays bulk discount matrix for the requested SKU(s): minimum quantity for discount eligibility (typically 10+ units for A-items, 20+ for B-items, 50+ for C-items), tiered discount rates (e.g., 10–49 units: 5%; 50–99 units: 8%; 100+ units: 12%), and margin floor (minimum acceptable margin — typically 15% for building materials, 20% for tools/hardware); (b) Sales Associate enters requested quantity and proposed discount into POS quotation module per W542; (c) system calculates: discounted price, resulting margin %, total transaction value, and margin impact vs. SRP; (d) if proposed discount is within Sales Associate authority (≤ 3% and margin > margin floor): Sales Associate approves on the spot; (e) if discount exceeds Sales Associate authority: escalation to Department Supervisor (≤ 8%) or Store Manager (≤ 15%); (f) discounts > 15% or margin below floor require Regional Manager approval per W67 — customer asked to wait or quoted a callback time | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor / Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Negotiation & Deal Closure**: (a) Store Manager (or authorized approver) reviews quotation with real-time margin data and inventory availability at store and nearby DCs per W4; (b) negotiation considerations: customer purchase history (repeat buyers may warrant higher discount), inventory position (overstock items may warrant deeper discount to clear), competitive intelligence per W130 (is customer price-shopping against competitor?), project potential (will this project generate repeat visits?); (c) Store Manager approves final discount within authority matrix; (d) quotation finalized with: item list, quantities, discounted unit price, total value, delivery option (if applicable per W5D), validity period (24 hours for bulk quotes); (e) customer accepts: quotation converts to sales order at POS; (f) payment: cash, card, or — if customer has trade account — charge to account per W5B.4c; (g) if customer requests delivery of bulky items: delivery scheduling per W5D with delivery fee waived for transactions > PHP 50,000 as bulk purchase benefit | Store Manager / Sales Associate / Customer | Store Manager | 10–20 min |
| 4 | **Bulk Order Fulfillment & Delivery**: (a) if items are in stock at store: immediate fulfillment from floor stock or backroom; (b) if partial stock: Sales Associate checks DC availability per W4 and offers: (i) partial fulfillment now + DC delivery within 2–3 days, or (ii) full DC delivery within 2–3 days; (c) for DC delivery: store replenishment order created with priority flag for customer order; (d) customer notified of delivery schedule per W708; (e) delivery executed per W5D with proof of delivery; (f) if customer is a trade account holder: invoice generated per W8 with agreed payment terms | Sales Associate / Receiving Clerk / System | Store Manager | Varies by fulfillment |
| 5 | **Bulk Transaction Analytics & Margin Monitoring**: (a) daily: Store Manager reviews bulk transactions from previous day — discount depth, margin impact, customer type; (b) weekly: bulk transaction summary — count, average discount, average transaction value, margin dilution vs. SRP, conversion rate (quotes generated vs. closed); (c) monthly: bulk discount analysis by category and customer segment — are certain categories being over-discounted? Are walk-in bulk customers converting to trade account holders?; (d) quarterly: discount matrix calibration — adjust thresholds and rates based on competitive landscape per W130, inventory turns per category, and margin targets per W489; (e) annual: bulk pricing strategy review with Merchandising and Finance | Store Manager / Finance / Merchandising | VP Store Operations | 4–6 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints

- POS quotation module (W542) for bulk quotation generation and conversion to sale
- POS system (W5B) for transaction processing with volume discount application
- Inventory/ATP system for stock availability at store and DC
- Pricing system for bulk discount matrix and margin floor validation
- Customer master (W253) for loyalty/trade account lookup
- Replenishment system (W4) for DC delivery scheduling
- Customer communication module (W708) for delivery notifications
- Trade account billing (W8) for charge-to-account transactions
- Competitor intelligence system (W130) for competitive pricing context
- BI dashboard for bulk transaction analytics
- Store KPI dashboard (W665) for margin and discount monitoring

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Margin erosion**: excessive discounting reduces profitability; mitigated by margin floor enforcement in system (transaction blocked if below margin floor without Regional Manager override), discount authority matrix, and monthly margin dilution monitoring
- **Inconsistent pricing**: different stores offer different discounts for the same product and volume, creating customer dissatisfaction if they discover the discrepancy; mitigated by standardized discount matrix chain-wide and POS-enforced discount limits
- **Stock-out on bulk orders**: customer negotiates a deal but store cannot fulfill the full quantity; mitigated by real-time ATP check before quotation finalization and DC delivery option per W5D
- **Quotation abuse**: customer uses BuildRight quotation to negotiate a better price at a competitor; mitigated by 24-hour quotation validity and competitive pricing intelligence per W130
- **Staff gaming the discount system**: Sales Associates offering discounts to friends/family without legitimate bulk purchase; mitigated by POS override logging per W538, minimum quantity enforcement, and exception-based reporting per W837


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters requested quantity and proposed discount into POS quotation module per…" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-scan of "scanned for purchase history and customer tier, or new customer registration…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-review of "reviews quotation with real-time margin data and inventory availability at…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: margin floor enforcement in system (transaction blocked if below margin floor without Regional Manager override), discount authority matrix, and monthly margin dilution monitoring; operational: standardized discount matrix chain-wide and POS-enforced discount limits; operational: real-time ATP check before quotation finalization and DC delivery option per W5D

### Staffing Implication

- **Sales Associate**: ~10–15 min per bulk negotiation; 3–4 per store per month (per the Frequency) = ~45–60 min/month; absorbed by existing role
- **Store Manager**: ~10 min per escalated negotiation (50% of bulk transactions); ~25–30 min/week; absorbed by existing role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate

- Request identification and quotation: 5–10 min
- Discount verification and approval: 5–10 min
- Negotiation and closure: 10–20 min
- Fulfillment arrangement: 5–10 min
- **Total per bulk transaction**: ~25–50 min of staff time

## W943. Customer Glass Cutting & Custom Flat Glass Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests custom-cut flat glass at the in-store glass cutting station or via ecommerce special order |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–4,000 transactions/month chain-wide (~15–20/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 pieces per transaction; average 2 cuts per piece |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Glass Specialist), Customer, Receiving Clerk (glass inventory handling), Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Flat glass (clear, tinted, frosted, textured, mirrored) is a core home improvement category. Customers frequently need glass cut to exact dimensions for window replacements, cabinet doors, tabletops, shower enclosures, and picture frames. Off-the-shelf standard sizes do not cover the vast majority of Philippine residential window dimensions, which vary widely due to non-standardized construction practices. BuildRight's in-store glass cutting service differentiates it from competitors who sell only standard sizes. The service generates incremental revenue through cutting fees and drives additional sales of associated products (glazing compound, silicone sealant, window putty, glass clips, moulding). Safety is paramount — glass handling, cutting, and disposal require strict PPE and handling protocols per W140 and W759.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Request & Measurement**: Customer approaches glass cutting station with dimensions or physical item (broken glass, window frame) for measurement; the Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) verifies glass type (clear 3mm/4mm/5mm/6mm, tinted, frosted, textured, mirror), dimensions (max 1.22m × 2.44m standard sheet), edge finish (plain cut, polished, beveled), and quantity; for walk-in: Sales Associate measures physical item with steel tape; for ecommerce: customer provides dimensions via online order form (W949 visualizer integration for photo-to-dimension estimation) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Feasibility Check & Quotation**: Sales Associate confirms: (a) glass type availability at store — system checks real-time stock per W533; if unavailable, Sales Associate checks nearest store (W22 transfer) or DC replenishment lead time; (b) dimensional feasibility — maximum cut size, minimum cut size (10cm × 10cm), tolerances (±2mm manual, ±1mm CNC); (c) safety compliance — tempered glass required for shower enclosures, large panels > 0.5sqm per National Building Code; tempered glass outsourced to accredited fabricator per W600 with 3–5 day lead time; (d) system generates quotation: glass material cost (per sqm) + cutting fee (per cut) + edge finish surcharge (per linear meter) + optional delivery fee; quotation valid for 7 days | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 3 | **Order Acceptance & Payment**: Customer accepts quotation; Sales Associate creates service work order (SWO) in POS system per W544; customer pays at POS — material cost + cutting fee + edge finish + associated products (sealant, clips, moulding) in single transaction; POS prints SWO receipt with: order number, glass specifications, dimensions, edge finish, promised ready time, and customer copy; system allocates glass sheet from inventory and creates cutting instruction for the Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 4 | **Glass Cutting Execution**: The Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) retrieves glass sheet from storage rack (backroom glass storage area, vertical rack with dividers by type/thickness); the Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) dons PPE per W140 (cut-resistant gloves, safety goggles, closed-toe shoes); performs cuts using manual glass cutter (score-and-snap for straight cuts) or CNC glass cutting table (available at top 30 stores); applies edge finish: plain cut (default), polished edge (hand polisher), or beveled edge (beveling machine — top 30 stores); measures finished piece against order dimensions; records cut dimensions and waste factor on SWO | Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 10–25 min/piece |
| 5 | **Quality Inspection & Customer Handover**: The Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) inspects: (a) dimensional accuracy (±2mm tolerance); (b) edge quality (no chips, cracks, burrs); (c) glass type and thickness match to order; failed inspection → recut from new sheet, waste piece dispositioned per W91; wraps finished piece in protective packing material (bubble wrap or corrugated sheet); customer inspects and signs acceptance on SWO; system closes SWO and posts sale; system updates inventory: glass sheet consumed + waste factor (average 10–15% waste per sheet) posted to scrap per W443 | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 6 | **Daily Station Cleanup & Waste Disposal**: At end of day: The Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) cleans glass cutting station; collects glass waste (offcuts, broken pieces) in designated sharps/glass waste container; waste glass disposed per W82 hazardous/non-hazardous waste protocol (flat glass is non-hazardous but requires special handling for sharp edges); system records daily glass waste volume for inventory reconciliation; glass cutting tools inspected and maintained (cutter wheel replacement every 200 linear meters of cut) | Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 15 min/day |

### System Touchpoints
- POS service work order module (W544) for order creation and payment
- Real-time inventory availability check per glass type and store (W533)
- Inventory allocation and consumption posting with waste factor
- Quotation generation with dynamic pricing (material + cutting fee + edge finish)
- Ecommerce special order integration (W545) for online glass orders
- AI visualizer integration (W949) for photo-to-dimension estimation
- PPE compliance tracking per W140
- Waste disposal tracking per W82, W443
- Service SKU catalog (W794) for glass cutting service items and fee structure
- Glass waste inventory reconciliation for shrinkage monitoring

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Injury risk**: Glass cutting is inherently dangerous — cuts, lacerations, eye injuries; mandatory PPE enforcement, first aid kit proximity per W501, and incident reporting per W140 mitigate; buildRight liability insurance per W863 provides coverage
- **Waste factor variance**: Manual cutting generates 10–15% waste; CNC cutting reduces to 5–8%; waste factor directly impacts margin; pricing must account for average waste without overcharging customers
- **Dimensional disputes**: Customer-provided dimensions that are wrong lead to unusable pieces; Sales Associate must verify dimensions independently and have customer sign off before cutting; re-cuts due to customer error are chargeable; re-cuts due to BuildRight error are absorbed
- **Tempered glass lead time**: Shower enclosures and large panels require tempered glass which cannot be cut in-store; 3–5 day outsourced lead time may disappoint walk-in customers expecting same-day service; clear communication of lead time at quotation stage
- **Glass storage fragility**: Glass sheets are fragile and bulky; vertical storage racks require dedicated floor space; breakage during handling (receiving, storage, retrieval) is a shrinkage risk; proper rack maintenance and handling training reduce breakage
- **Seasonal demand spikes**: Post-typhoon glass replacement demand can surge 300–400% in affected areas; pre-positioned buffer stock per W960 and emergency procurement per W60 address surge demand


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-request of "Request & Measurement" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "Check & Quotation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "prints SWO receipt" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: PPE enforcement, first aid kit proximity per W501, and incident reporting per W140 mitigate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Glass Specialist)**: 1 per store designated as the Sales Associate (Glass Specialist) with certified glass cutting training; cutting activity consumes ~15–40 min/day at 15–20 transactions/month (per the Frequency); absorbed by existing Department Supervisor or Sales Associate role with specialized training certification per W518
- **Training**: Initial 40-hour glass cutting certification + 8-hour annual refresher per W51; CNC cutting machine operation training for top 30 stores (16 hours)
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Customer request & measurement: 5–10 min
- Feasibility check & quotation: 5–8 min
- Order acceptance & payment: 5 min
- Glass cutting execution: 10–25 min/piece
- Quality inspection & handover: 5–10 min
- Daily cleanup: 15 min
- **Total per transaction**: 30–60 min of staff time

## W944. Customer Pipe Threading, Cutting & Fabrication Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests custom-cut or threaded pipe at the in-store pipe fabrication station or via ecommerce special order |
| **Frequency** | ~4,000–6,000 transactions/month chain-wide (~20–30/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 pieces per transaction; average 2 cuts + 1 thread per transaction |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Plumbing/Electrical) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Pipe Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing), vendor (special alloy sourcing) |

### Background

Pipes (galvanized iron/GI, black iron, stainless steel, copper, PVC) are sold in standard lengths (typically 6 meters for GI, 3 meters for copper). Customers — especially plumbers, contractors, and DIY renovators — frequently need pipes cut to specific lengths for installations where standard lengths produce excessive waste. Threaded pipe ends (BSPT/NPT) are required for GI pipe connections and are not always available in all sizes from vendors. BuildRight's in-store pipe cutting and threading service provides immediate, custom-length pipes while driving incremental revenue through cutting fees, threading fees, and associated product sales (fittings, Teflon tape, pipe joint compound, valves). This service is especially valuable for the Philippine market where residential plumbing systems predominantly use GI pipes with threaded connections, and on-site threading is common practice but many contractors prefer pre-threaded pieces for precision.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Request & Specification**: Customer provides pipe specification: (a) pipe type — GI (galvanized iron), black iron, stainless steel, copper, or PVC; (b) nominal diameter — ½" to 4" for GI/black iron; ¼" to 2" for copper; PVC cut only (no threading); (c) cut lengths — specific lengths in cm; (d) thread requirements — both ends, one end, or no threads; (e) thread type — BSPT (standard in Philippines) or NPT; Sales Associate verifies specifications and selects appropriate pipe from inventory | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Feasibility Check & Quotation**: Sales Associate confirms: (a) pipe type and diameter availability at store — system checks real-time stock per W533; (b) cutting feasibility — pipe cutter capacity (manual for ≤ 2", power cutter for 2½"–4"); (c) threading feasibility — pipe threading machine capacity (½"–4" BSPT); (d) minimum piece length — 5cm for cuts, 3cm minimum threaded section; (e) system generates quotation: pipe material cost (charged per standard length consumed, not per cut piece) + cutting fee (per cut) + threading fee (per end) + optional delivery; customer informed that leftover pipe pieces are customer's property per standard practice | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 3 | **Order Acceptance & Payment**: Customer accepts quotation; Sales Associate creates SWO in POS per W544; customer pays at POS; POS prints SWO receipt with: order number, pipe specifications, cut lengths, thread details, promised ready time; system allocates pipe from inventory; for large orders (≥10 pieces or ≥4" diameter): Sales Associate schedules pickup time within 2 hours to avoid station congestion | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 4 | **Pipe Cutting & Threading Execution**: The Sales Associate (Pipe Specialist) retrieves pipe from storage rack; dons PPE per W140 (safety goggles, cut-resistant gloves, steel-toe boots, ear protection for power cutting); measures and marks cut points with measuring tape and marker; performs cuts: (a) PVC — manual PVC cutter (quick, clean cut); (b) GI/copper ≤ 2" — manual pipe cutter (rotating blade); (c) GI/black iron 2½"–4" — power pipe cutter (abrasive chop saw); deburrs cut ends with file or deburring tool; for threaded ends: mounts pipe in pipe threading machine (½"–4" capacity), applies cutting oil, threads to BSPT standard (11 threads per inch for ½"–¾", taper per BS 21); cleans threaded ends (removes metal chips, applies anti-rust coating for GI); measures finished pieces against order | Sales Associate (Pipe Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 10–20 min/piece |
| 5 | **Quality Inspection & Customer Handover**: The Sales Associate (Pipe Specialist) inspects: (a) cut length accuracy (±3mm); (b) thread quality — thread gauge fit check (go/no-go gauge for BSPT threads); (c) deburr quality — no sharp edges; (d) anti-rust coating applied (GI only); customer inspects and signs acceptance on SWO; system closes SWO; leftover pipe pieces offered to customer or disposed per W91 if customer declines | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 6 | **Weekly Machine Maintenance**: Pipe threading machine: weekly oil level check, die head cleaning, blade inspection; pipe cutter: blade replacement every 500 cuts; maintenance logged in equipment maintenance module per W650; consumable tracking: cutting oil, deburring tools, anti-rust spray replenished from store inventory | Sales Associate (Pipe Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 30 min/week |

### System Touchpoints
- POS service work order module (W544) for order creation and payment
- Real-time inventory availability check per pipe type, diameter, and store (W533)
- Inventory allocation and consumption posting
- Quotation generation with dynamic pricing
- Service SKU catalog (W794) for pipe cutting and threading service items
- Ecommerce special order integration (W545)
- Equipment maintenance module (W650) for machine maintenance tracking
- PPE compliance tracking per W140

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Metal chip and cutting oil waste**: Threading generates metal chips and uses cutting oil that requires proper disposal; chips collected in metal waste bin for recycling per W167; cutting oil stored for reuse with periodic disposal per W82; environmental compliance per DENR regulations
- **Machine breakdown**: Pipe threading machine is critical equipment; breakdown halts service; spare die heads and blades maintained in stock; backup manual threading die set available; maintenance per W650 prevents unplanned downtime
- **Thread quality inconsistency**: Worn dies produce poor threads that leak; thread gauge quality check at step 5 catches defects; die replacement schedule (every 1,000 threads or quarterly) prevents quality degradation
- **Noise**: Power pipe cutting and threading generates significant noise (>85dB); ear protection mandatory; operations limited to store operating hours; station positioned away from customer consultation areas
- **Customer dimension errors**: Incorrect measurements lead to unusable pieces; Sales Associate must independently verify critical dimensions; re-fabrication due to customer error is chargeable; re-fabrication due to BuildRight error is absorbed


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-request of "Request & Specification" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "Check & Quotation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "prints SWO receipt" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Pipe Specialist)**: 1 per store designated with pipe threading certification; activity consumes ~20–55 min/day at 20–30 transactions/month (per the Frequency); absorbed by existing role
- **Training**: 24-hour pipe cutting and threading certification + 4-hour annual refresher per W51
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Customer request & specification: 5–10 min
- Feasibility check & quotation: 5–8 min
- Order acceptance & payment: 5 min
- Cutting & threading execution: 10–20 min/piece
- Quality inspection & handover: 5–10 min
- Weekly maintenance: 30 min
- **Total per transaction**: 25–55 min of staff time

## W945. Customer Key Duplication & Lock Rekeying Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests key duplication or lock rekeying at the in-store hardware service counter |
| **Frequency** | ~6,000–8,000 transactions/month chain-wide (~30–40/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 keys per duplication transaction; 1–3 locks per rekeying transaction |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Tools/Hardware) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Hardware Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (key blank inventory) |

### Background

Key duplication is a high-frequency, low-complexity service that drives significant foot traffic and incremental sales of associated security products (padlocks, deadbolts, door locks, security chains, peepholes). In the Philippine residential market, homeowners frequently need duplicate keys for family members, tenants, and domestic helpers. Lock rekeying (changing the internal pin configuration so old keys no longer work) is commonly requested during tenant turnover, after domestic helper separation, or when moving into a previously occupied residence. BuildRight's key duplication service takes 2–5 minutes per key, creating strong customer engagement at the hardware counter that leads to additional hardware purchases. The service is a proven traffic driver in the Philippine hardware retail market.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Request & Key Identification**: Customer presents original key(s) for duplication or lock cylinder for rekeying; Sales Associate identifies: (a) key type — standard flat key (Yale, Master, Weiser, local brands), dimple key, tubular key, automotive key (basic — no transponder), or high-security restricted key (system flags restricted brands that cannot be duplicated per IP agreements per W126); (b) key blank availability — system checks key blank inventory per type; (c) for rekeying: lock brand, number of pins, and cylinder type (rim cylinder, mortise cylinder, tubular latch) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Service Execution — Key Duplication**: Sales Associate selects matching key blank from inventory; mounts original and blank in key duplicating machine; aligns cutting wheel; operates machine (trace-and-cut, 30–60 seconds per key); deburrs cut key on brush wheel; tests key in test lock (store maintains a test lock set for common key types); if key does not operate smoothly, adjusts and re-cuts on new blank (defective blank dispositioned per W91); repeats for additional copies | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 2–5 min/key |
| 3 | **Service Execution — Lock Rekeying**: Sales Associate removes lock cylinder from customer-provided lock or from new lock purchased in-store; disassembles cylinder using pin kit; removes old pins; installs new pin configuration matching new key (randomly selected from key blank stock or customer-provided new key); reassembles cylinder; tests new key operation; old pins returned to pin kit inventory; customer receives new key(s) with rekeyed lock | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 15–30 min/lock |
| 4 | **Payment & Handover**: Customer verifies key(s) operate correctly in original lock (for duplication) or test lock (for rekeying); Sales Associate processes payment at POS: duplication fee per key + rekeying fee per lock + key blank cost + new lock (if purchased); system creates service ticket and posts revenue to service income account; customer receives keys with BuildRight key tag (branded, includes store phone number for reorders) | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 5 | **Daily Key Blank Replenishment**: At end of day, Sales Associate counts key blank inventory by type; system auto-generates replenishment transfer request from DC or vendor for blanks below reorder point; key blanks are low-cost, high-turn items maintained at 200–300 blanks per store across common types | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 15 min/day |

### System Touchpoints
- POS service item for key duplication and lock rekeying fees
- Key blank inventory tracking and auto-replenishment per SKU
- Restricted key brand database (IP compliance per W126)
- Service SKU catalog (W794) for key-related service items
- Revenue posting to service income GL account
- Customer communication module (W708) for service-ready notification (ecommerce orders)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Restricted key duplication liability**: Some lock brands restrict key duplication to authorized locksmiths only; duplicating restricted keys exposes BuildRight to IP infringement claims per W126; system must flag restricted key types and Sales Associates must decline with customer explanation; signage at service counter displays restricted brands
- **Key accuracy complaints**: Poorly cut keys damage locks over time; quality test at step 2 catches most defects; customer satisfaction guarantee: free re-cut within 30 days with original receipt; estimated re-cut rate: 2–3%
- **Security concern perception**: Some customers are uncomfortable having keys duplicated outside a locksmith shop; counter staff must be professional, maintain key security (customer keeps original key in sight at all times), and never retain customer keys overnight
- **Automotive transponder keys**: Modern car keys with transponder chips cannot be duplicated on standard key machines; Sales Associates must identify and decline transponder keys to avoid customer frustration; signage at counter clarifies service scope
- **Key blank stockout**: Running out of common key blanks causes immediate customer dissatisfaction; safety stock of 200–300 blanks per store with weekly replenishment; emergency procurement from local locksmith suppliers per W921 if DC stockout


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "checks key blank inventory per type" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-match of "matching key blank from inventory" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-test of "tests new key operation" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: must flag restricted key types and Sales Associates must decline with customer explanation

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Hardware Specialist)**: Key duplication consumes ~10–20 min/day at 30–40 transactions/month (per the Frequency); absorbed by existing Hardware counter Sales Associate role
- **Training**: 8-hour key cutting and lock rekeying certification per W51
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Key identification: 3–5 min
- Key duplication: 2–5 min/key
- Lock rekeying: 15–30 min/lock
- Payment & handover: 3–5 min
- Daily replenishment: 15 min
- **Total per duplication transaction**: 8–15 min
- **Total per rekeying transaction**: 25–40 min

## W946. Customer Screen Door & Window Screen Custom Fabrication Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests custom-fabricated screen door or window screen at the in-store service counter or via ecommerce |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 transactions/month chain-wide (~8–12/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 screens per transaction |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Fabrication Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (screen material pricing) |

### Background

Screen doors and window screens are essential in the Philippine tropical climate for insect control (mosquitoes, flies) while allowing natural ventilation. Standard pre-fabricated screen sizes rarely fit Philippine residential openings due to non-standardized window and door dimensions, especially in older homes. Custom fabrication — cutting aluminum or wood frames to size, stretching fiberglass or stainless steel mesh, and assembling with spline and groove — is a high-value service that competitors without fabrication capabilities cannot offer. The service drives significant incremental sales of associated products (hinges, handles, locking mechanisms, weather stripping, mounting hardware). With rising dengue awareness and government health campaigns, demand for window screens has grown steadily.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Request & Measurement**: Customer provides window or door opening dimensions (width × height), or brings old/damaged screen for replication; Sales Associate records: (a) frame material — aluminum (standard) or wood (premium); (b) frame profile — standard flat, decorative, or retractable; (c) mesh type — fiberglass (standard, 18×16 mesh), stainless steel (security screen, 12×12 mesh), or pet-resistant (heavy-duty fiberglass); (d) mesh color — charcoal (standard), gray, or silver; (e) frame color — white, brown, bronze, or natural aluminum; (f) hardware — hinge type (butt hinge, piano hinge), handle type, latch/lock type; (g) installation type — surface mount or recessed; Sales Associate independently measures customer-provided physical items for verification | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 8–15 min |
| 2 | **Quotation & Order Acceptance**: System generates quotation: (a) frame material cost (per linear meter of aluminum/wood profile); (b) mesh material cost (per sqm); (c) hardware kit (hinges, handle, latch); (d) fabrication labor fee (per screen, based on size); (e) optional installation fee (outsourced per W138, only for BuildRight service area); customer accepts; Sales Associate creates SWO per W544; customer pays material deposit (50%) with balance due at pickup; system allocates materials from inventory | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Frame Cutting & Assembly**: The Sales Associate (Fabrication Specialist) retrieves aluminum profile stock or wood stock; cuts frame pieces to dimensions using miter saw (aluminum) or table saw (wood) at 45° corners; for aluminum: joins corners with aluminum corner keys and crimping tool; for wood: joins with wood glue, dowels, and corner braces; dry-fits frame to verify dimensions | Sales Associate (Fabrication Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 20–30 min/screen |
| 4 | **Mesh Installation**: The Sales Associate (Fabrication Specialist) cuts mesh to frame dimensions plus 2cm overhang on all sides; lays mesh over frame with groove facing up; presses rubber spline into groove over mesh using spline roller tool, starting from one corner and working around; trims excess mesh with utility knife; inspects mesh tension (uniform, no wrinkles, no gaps) and spline seating (fully seated in groove, no protrusion) | Sales Associate (Fabrication Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min/screen |
| 5 | **Hardware Installation & Quality Check**: The Sales Associate (Fabrication Specialist) installs hinges (2–3 per screen depending on size), handle, and latch/lock per customer specification; quality check: (a) frame squareness (diagonal measurement within ±3mm); (b) mesh tension and uniformity; (c) hardware alignment and operation; (d) overall dimensions match order (±3mm tolerance); marks BuildRight label on frame (date, store, order number) | Sales Associate (Fabrication Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min/screen |
| 6 | **Customer Pickup & Final Payment**: Customer picks up completed screen(s) within 7 days of notification; inspects and verifies fit against physical opening (or Customer Service Counter assists with measurement verification); customer pays remaining balance at POS; system closes SWO; if customer requests delivery, W668 delivery coordination triggered | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS service work order module (W544)
- Inventory availability and allocation for aluminum profile, mesh, spline, hardware
- Service SKU catalog (W794) for screen fabrication service items
- Quotation generation with material + labor pricing
- Customer communication module (W708) for fabrication-ready notification
- Delivery coordination (W668) for delivered orders
- Installation service referral (W138) for customers needing professional installation

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Measurement accuracy**: Incorrect dimensions are the #1 source of customer complaints; Sales Associate must independently verify all customer-provided measurements; for high-value orders (≥PHP 5,000), recommend customer bring physical frame/opening dimensions confirmed by contractor
- **Lead time management**: Screen fabrication is labor-intensive (40–60 min per screen); at peak demand (summer, post-dengue outbreaks), backlog can reach 3–5 days; customer expectations must be set at order acceptance; priority queue for loyalty Gold/Platinum members
- **Mesh inventory complexity**: Multiple mesh types × colors × roll widths create inventory management challenges; top 3 combinations (fiberglass charcoal 1.2m, fiberglass gray 1.5m, stainless steel 1.2m) cover 80% of demand; specialty mesh ordered per W545 special order
- **Workstation space**: Screen fabrication requires dedicated workbench (2m × 1m) with cutting and assembly tools; competing for backroom space with receiving, BOPIS staging, and stockroom; workstation scheduling per store capacity


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-record of "Customer Request & Measurement" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "Quotation & Order Acceptance" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "Frame Cutting & Assembly" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Fabrication Specialist)**: 1 per store with screen fabrication skills; fabrication activity consumes ~0.5–2.5 hours/day at 8–12 transactions/month × 1–3 screens × 65–100 min/screen (≈25–165 min/day, peaking seasonally); absorbed by existing roles with specialized training
- **Training**: 16-hour screen fabrication certification per W51
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Customer request & measurement: 8–15 min
- Quotation & order acceptance: 5–10 min
- Frame cutting & assembly: 20–30 min/screen
- Mesh installation: 10–15 min/screen
- Hardware installation & QC: 10–15 min/screen
- Customer pickup: 10–15 min
- **Total per screen**: 65–100 min of staff time (across multiple steps, often batch-processed)
