## W953. Customer Contractor Micro-Lending Partnership Program Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Trade/B2B customer requests short-term financing for materials purchase, or lending partner submits loan application derived from BuildRight purchase history |
| **Frequency** | ~500–800 loan applications/month; ~70% approval rate |
| **Volume** | PHP 10,000–500,000 per loan; average PHP 80,000 |
| **Owner** | Head of Trade / Account Management |
| **Participants** | Customer (Contractor/Trade), Lending Partner (accredited microfinance institution/bank), Trade Sales Representative, Finance (AR), Legal |

### Background

Small-to-medium contractors — who represent 30% of BuildRight's revenue — frequently face cash flow gaps between purchasing materials and receiving payment from their clients (homeowners, developers). Traditional bank loans are often inaccessible due to documentation requirements, processing time, and lack of formal credit history (many Filipino contractors operate as sole proprietors without audited financial statements). BuildRight's Contractor Micro-Lending Partnership Program connects qualified trade customers with accredited lending partners (rural banks, microfinance institutions, fintech lenders) who offer short-term working capital loans (30–90 day terms) based on the contractor's purchase history and payment behavior with BuildRight. BuildRight does NOT lend directly — it facilitates the connection and shares (with customer consent) verified purchase data to help lending partners assess creditworthiness. BuildRight benefits from: (a) increased trade customer loyalty and purchase volume; (b) reduced credit risk (lending partner absorbs default risk); (c) referral fee income from lending partners (1–2% of loan amount). This is distinct from W24 (Trade Credit Application, where BuildRight extends direct credit) — W953 is third-party lending facilitation.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Enrollment & Consent**: Trade customer enrolls in the micro-lending program: (a) Trade Sales Representative explains program — BuildRight partners with [accredited lenders], customer applies for short-term financing through the partner, BuildRight shares verified purchase data (with consent) to support credit assessment, BuildRight does NOT guarantee or co-sign loans; (b) customer signs consent form per RA 10173 authorizing BuildRight to share purchase history and payment behavior data with accredited lending partners; (c) consent captured in ERP with revocable option; (d) customer selects preferred lending partner from accredited list (2–3 partners per region) | Trade Sales Rep / Customer | Head of Trade / Account Management | 15–20 min |
| 2 | **Loan Application & Data Sharing**: Customer applies for loan: (a) via BuildRight mobile app — customer selects loan amount and term, system auto-populates application with verified BuildRight purchase data (12-month purchase history, average monthly spend, payment timeliness, active trade account standing); (b) via lending partner directly — customer references BuildRight trade account number; lending partner requests data share via API; system validates customer consent and transmits authorized data; (c) lending partner performs independent credit assessment using: shared BuildRight data + partner's own credit scoring + other data sources; BuildRight does NOT influence lending decisions | Customer / System / Lending Partner | Head of Trade / Account Management | 10–15 min (customer side); automated data sharing |
| 3 | **Loan Disbursement & Material Purchase**: Lending partner approves loan and disburses: (a) directly to customer's bank account or e-wallet; (b) customer purchases materials from BuildRight using: (i) cash/card payment with loan proceeds; (ii) lending partner's virtual card (if partner offers co-branded card); (iii) BuildRight trade account (W24) with loan proceeds used to pay trade balance; system tracks loan-funded purchases for program analytics; customer is NOT required to spend loan proceeds at BuildRight (no tie-in per BSP regulations), but BuildRight expects high share-of-wallet based on convenience and loyalty | Customer / System / Lending Partner | Head of Trade / Account Management | 5–10 min (purchase) |
| 4 | **Repayment Monitoring & Data Refresh**: (a) lending partner manages loan repayment independently — customer repays lending partner per agreed terms; (b) BuildRight receives monthly anonymized portfolio data from lending partners: approval rate, average loan size, default rate, repayment performance; (c) BuildRight refreshes customer's shared data monthly: updated purchase history and payment behavior transmitted to lending partners for ongoing credit assessment; (d) if customer's BuildRight trade account goes on credit hold per W888, lending partner is notified (per consent agreement) as a risk indicator | System / Lending Partner | Head of Trade / Account Management / CFO | Automated |
| 5 | **Program Governance & Partner Management**: (a) quarterly: Head of Trade / Account Management reviews program metrics — enrollment count, application volume, approval rate, default rate, average loan size, BuildRight revenue impact, referral fee income; (b) semi-annually: Legal reviews lending partner compliance with BSP regulations, the Truth in Lending Act (RA 3765), and data privacy (RA 10173); (c) annually: lending partner accreditation renewal per W36 vendor onboarding framework; partner performance scorecard: approval rate, customer satisfaction, default rate, complaint resolution; (d) referral fee reconciliation: Finance verifies lending partner referral fee payments against loan disbursement records | Head of Trade / Account Management / Legal / Finance | CEO | Quarterly review + annual renewal |

### System Touchpoints
- Customer consent management module (RA 10173 compliant per W405)
- Purchase history data sharing API (secure, encrypted, consent-verified per request)
- Lending partner API integration for real-time data sharing
- Loan-funded purchase tracking for program analytics
- Trade account integration (W24) for payment behavior data
- Credit hold notification integration (W888)
- Mobile app lending module for customer self-service application
- BI dashboard for program analytics
- Vendor management module (W36) for lending partner accreditation
- Referral fee revenue tracking and reconciliation

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Regulatory compliance**: Micro-lending is regulated by BSP; BuildRight must ensure it is NOT acting as a lender (no co-signing, no guarantee, no interest collection) to avoid requiring a lending license; legal review per step 5b validates compliance structure; any change in BSP regulations affecting referral arrangements triggers immediate legal review per W657
- **Data privacy liability**: Sharing customer purchase data with third parties carries RA 10173 compliance risk; explicit consent required per step 1; customer can revoke consent at any time; data sharing limited to authorized purposes only; data breach notification per W53 if lending partner experiences breach
- **Customer over-indebtedness**: Facilitating easy access to credit may contribute to contractor over-indebtedness; lending partners responsible for responsible lending practices; BuildRight provides financial literacy resources but does not assess borrower capacity; program positioned as "facilitating access" not "encouraging borrowing"
- **Lending partner default impact on BuildRight**: If lending partner experiences high default rates and restricts lending, BuildRight trade customers may reduce purchases due to cash flow constraints; multiple lending partners per region provide diversification; BuildRight can also extend direct trade credit per W24 as fallback
- **Reputational risk**: If lending partner engages in predatory practices (excessive interest, aggressive collection), BuildRight's brand may be associated with the negative experience; partner selection criteria include fair lending practices; complaint handling per W41 extended to lending-related complaints with escalation to lending partner


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "captured in ERP with revocable option" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "validates customer consent and transmits authorized data" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "Loan Disbursement & Material Purchase" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: per step 1

### Staffing Implication
- **Trade Sales Representatives**: ~1–2 hours/week on program enrollment and customer communication per region; absorbed by existing trade sales team
- **Legal**: ~4–6 hours/quarter on partner compliance review; absorbed by existing Legal team
- **Finance**: ~2–3 hours/month on referral fee reconciliation; absorbed by existing Finance team
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Customer enrollment & consent: 15–20 min
- Loan application & data sharing: 10–15 min (customer) + automated
- Loan disbursement & purchase: 5–10 min
- Repayment monitoring: automated
- Program governance: quarterly + annual
- **Total per customer enrollment**: 30–45 min of staff time

## W954. Customer Delivery & Installation Quality Follow-Up Verification

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Delivery confirmed (W19, W668) or installation completed (W138) — system triggers post-service follow-up |
| **Frequency** | ~24,000–29,000 follow-ups/month (covering ~17,200 ecommerce home deliveries + 5,000–8,000 in-store delivery orders + 2,200–3,500 installations) |
| **Volume** | 1 follow-up per delivery/installation event |
| **Owner** | Customer Experience Manager |
| **Participants** | Customer, Customer Service Representative, Installation Technician, Category Manager (quality issues), Store Manager (delivery issues) |

### Background

Post-delivery and post-installation follow-up is a critical yet often neglected customer touchpoint. For BuildRight's home improvement customers, delivery and installation are high-anxiety moments — "Did the right items arrive undamaged?" "Was the installation done correctly?" "Will there be follow-up issues?". Proactive follow-up within 24–48 hours: (a) catches problems early before they escalate to negative reviews, DTI complaints (W469), or social media posts; (b) demonstrates BuildRight's commitment to customer satisfaction beyond the transaction; (c) generates valuable quality data on delivery partners and installation technicians; (d) creates opportunities for additional product recommendations and loyalty engagement. This workflow is distinct from W65 (customer satisfaction measurement, which is periodic/survey-based) and W608 (store-level CX pulse monitoring, which is in-store focused). W954 is specifically triggered by delivery/installation completion events and focuses on service quality verification.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Automated Follow-Up Trigger**: System detects delivery confirmation (proof-of-delivery scanned per W592) or installation completion (technician signs off in system per W138); 24 hours after delivery/installation: system sends automated follow-up via: (a) SMS — brief quality check (3 questions) with link to mobile app survey; (b) email — detailed satisfaction survey with photo upload capability; (c) mobile app push notification — quick rating (1–5 stars) + optional feedback text; customer selects preferred channel from profile settings | System | Customer Experience Manager | Automated |
| 2 | **Customer Response Collection**: Customer responds: (a) positive response (4–5 stars, no issues reported) → system sends thank-you message, offers related product recommendations, and credits loyalty points (25 points for survey completion); triggers review solicitation (W510) for positive experiences; (b) neutral response (3 stars, no specific issues) → system sends thank-you and invites detailed feedback; Customer Service Representative reviews within 48 hours; (c) negative response (1–2 stars OR specific issue reported) → immediately escalated to Customer Service Representative for live follow-up per step 3 | Customer / System | Customer Experience Manager | 2–5 min (customer) |
| 3 | **Issue Resolution Follow-Up**: For negative responses: (a) Customer Service Representative calls customer within 4 hours of negative response during business hours; (b) documents issue: delivery damage, wrong item, missing item, installation quality, installation behavior/professionalism, timing/punctuality; (c) categorizes issue per root cause taxonomy (delivery, installation, product, logistics); (d) resolves or escalates: (i) delivery damage → claim per W860 insurance or W91 disposition; (ii) wrong/missing item → replacement per W12 returns; (iii) installation quality → service rework per W795; (iv) technician behavior → escalated to the Installation Services Coordinator per W600 contractor management; (e) resolution tracked per W597 SLA (target: 48-hour resolution for delivery issues, 72-hour for installation issues) | Customer Service Rep | Customer Experience Manager | 15–30 min/case |
| 4 | **Quality Data Aggregation & Vendor/Technician Scorecard**: (a) weekly: system aggregates follow-up data by: delivery partner, installation technician, product category, store, region; (b) delivery partner scorecard: on-time rate, damage rate, customer satisfaction score, POD compliance; shared with delivery partners per W242 performance review; (c) installation technician scorecard: quality rating, rework rate, professionalism score, timeliness; feeds into W600 contractor accreditation; (d) product category quality: identifies products with high damage-in-transit rates for packaging improvement review with Category Manager | System / Customer Experience Manager | Head of Trade / Account Management / COO | 2–3 hours/week |
| 5 | **Monthly CX Reporting & Continuous Improvement**: Monthly report to C-suite: (a) follow-up response rate (target: ≥ 40%); (b) CSAT score for deliveries and installations; (c) top 5 issues by frequency and severity; (d) resolution rate and time; (e) delivery partner and technician rankings; (f) trend analysis (month-over-month, quarter-over-quarter); findings feed into: (i) delivery partner contract renewal per W62B; (ii) installation technician accreditation per W600; (iii) product packaging improvement per Category Manager; (iv) store operations improvement per W575 weekly review | Customer Experience Manager | CMO / COO | 3–4 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Automated follow-up trigger integration with delivery (W592) and installation (W138) modules
- Multi-channel communication module (W708) — SMS, email, push notification
- Customer satisfaction survey module (W65)
- Loyalty points system for survey completion incentive
- Review/rating solicitation (W510) for positive experiences
- Customer complaint and ticketing system (W258) for issue escalation
- Service rework module (W795) for installation quality issues
- Delivery partner scorecard module (W242)
- Installation technician scorecard (W600)
- BI dashboard for quality analytics and trending
- Mobile app (W615) for survey collection

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Low response rate**: Post-service surveys typically achieve 20–30% response rates; loyalty points incentive (25 points = PHP 25 value per the 1-point-per-PHP-1 rule, profile §9.2) may be insufficient; consider higher incentive for first-time follow-up responders; multi-channel approach (SMS + app + email) maximizes reach
- **Negative feedback fatigue**: Customers who repeatedly experience issues may disengage; every negative response must receive live follow-up to demonstrate commitment; service recovery per W660 for chronically dissatisfied customers
- **Installation technician resistance**: Technicians may feel unfairly judged by customer ratings that don't account for difficult installations (poor site conditions, unrealistic customer expectations); technician scorecard includes context (job complexity rating); dispute process for technicians who believe rating is unfair
- **Survey timing**: 24 hours post-delivery may be too soon (customer hasn't used product yet); 24 hours post-installation is appropriate for installation quality but product quality issues may emerge later; follow-up sequence: 24-hour delivery check + 7-day installation check + 30-day product satisfaction check for high-value installations (≥ PHP 50,000)


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-scan of "scanned per W592" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-review of "Customer Response Collection" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-log of "Issue Resolution Follow-Up" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Customer Experience Manager review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Customer Service Representatives**: ~25–50 hours/day across the CSR team on issue resolution follow-ups (~10% negative response rate × ~27,000 follow-ups/month ≈ 3,000 cases/month ≈ 100/day × 15–30 min per case, per step 3; ≈ 4–7 FTE-equivalents chain-wide); existing call center team of 34 (W259) absorbs; dedicated 3–4 representatives assigned to post-delivery/installation follow-up queue
- **Customer Experience Manager**: ~6–8 hours/week on analytics and reporting; absorbed by existing role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Automated trigger: 0 min (automated)
- Customer response: 2–5 min (customer time)
- Issue resolution: 15–30 min/case (negative responses only)
- Quality data aggregation: 2–3 hours/week
- Monthly reporting: 3–4 hours/month
- **Total staff time per follow-up**: ~5 min average (most are automated or positive responses)

## W955. Customer Daily-Wage Construction Worker Hiring Facilitation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests assistance finding daily-wage construction workers, or workers register at BuildRight for daily work availability |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 hiring facilitations/month chain-wide (~40–60/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 workers per hiring request |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Customer, Construction Worker (applicant), Customer Service Representative, Store Manager |

### Background

In the Philippines, hardware stores serve as informal gathering places for daily-wage construction workers ("pakyaw" or "arawan" workers — carpenters, masons, plumbers, electricians, painters, laborers) who seek daily employment. Homeowners and small contractors visit hardware stores both to buy materials and to find available workers. This informal ecosystem has operated for decades but lacks structure — workers have no verified credentials, customers have no quality assurance, and BuildRight has no visibility into this activity despite it being a significant driver of store foot traffic. BuildRight's Daily-Wage Worker Hiring Facilitation Service formalizes this ecosystem: workers register with verified skills and contact information; customers can browse available workers at an in-store kiosk or via mobile app; BuildRight does NOT employ, guarantee, or take responsibility for workers — it provides a bulletin board and matching service. This service drives: (a) increased store foot traffic from workers and hiring customers; (b) loyalty program enrollment (workers and customers); (c) incremental material sales (workers recommend BuildRight to their employers); (d) community goodwill per W209. This is distinct from W904 (Contractor Referral & Matchmaking, which refers licensed, accredited contractors for projects) — W955 covers casual daily-wage workers for short-term, unskilled-to-semi-skilled labor.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Worker Registration**: Construction worker visits BuildRight customer service counter to register: (a) provides: full name, mobile number, barangay/address, trade/skill (carpenter, mason, plumber, electrician, painter, tile setter, laborer/handyman, welder), years of experience, daily rate expectation, availability (Mon–Sat selection), and preferred BuildRight store; (b) Customer Service Representative verifies: valid government ID (presented and visually verified — PhilHealth, barangay clearance, voter's ID, or any valid ID); (c) system creates worker profile — NOT an employee record, but a facilitation directory entry; (d) worker receives BuildRight loyalty card enrollment per W551 (optional but encouraged — tracks store visits for activity-based availability status); (e) worker receives printed registration card with BuildRight registration ID; (f) worker profile active for 90 days, renewable with re-verification; (g) registration is FREE — no fee to worker or customer | Customer Service Rep / Worker | Store Manager | 10–15 min/worker |
| 2 | **Worker Availability Management**: Registered workers maintain availability: (a) active status: worker checks in at store kiosk or via SMS/App each morning by 7:00 AM to signal availability for the day; (b) if worker does not check in for 3 consecutive working days, system marks profile "Inactive — last checked in [date]"; (c) worker can deactivate profile anytime (found long-term employment, traveling, etc.); (d) system purges profiles inactive > 90 days — worker must re-register; (e) worker can update: daily rate, skills, and contact number at any time via kiosk or app | Worker / System | Store Manager | 1 min/day (worker check-in) |
| 3 | **Customer Hiring Request**: Customer browses available workers: (a) in-store: dedicated kiosk (W924) displays today's available workers by trade, daily rate, and years of experience; no names or photos displayed until customer selects — only trade, rate range, experience, and registration ID; (b) mobile app: same directory with distance filter (workers registered at nearby stores); (c) customer selects worker(s) and requests contact information; system sends worker's mobile number to customer via SMS (with worker's consent per registration terms); (d) alternatively: customer posts a job request at the kiosk (trade needed, number of workers, date, location, offered daily rate) and available workers see the posting at the store kiosk; (e) BuildRight does NOT negotiate rates, verify worker quality, or guarantee workmanship | Customer / System | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Hiring Confirmation & Feedback**: After hiring: (a) customer optionally reports hiring outcome via app or SMS: "hired," "no-show," "unsatisfactory"; (b) worker optionally reports: "hired," "customer did not show," "unsafe conditions"; (c) system tracks: hiring frequency per worker, no-show rate, customer feedback (positive/negative/no report); (d) workers with > 3 customer "no-show" reports in 30 days are flagged for Store Manager review — may be suspended from directory; (e) workers with consistently positive feedback receive "Recommended" badge in directory (based on ≥ 5 positive reports, no negative reports in 90 days); (f) customers with > 3 worker "no-show-for-customer" reports are flagged — workers can see customer rating before accepting | Customer / Worker / System | Store Manager | 2–3 min |
| 5 | **Program Governance & Compliance**: (a) Store Manager reviews worker directory weekly: new registrations, active counts, flagged workers, customer complaints; (b) quarterly: HR reviews program for DOLE compliance — BuildRight is a facilitation/directory service, NOT an employer or contractor; workers are NOT BuildRight employees; no co-employment risk per DOLE DO 174 analysis; (c) annual: Legal reviews terms of service and liability disclaimers; workers sign acknowledgement that BuildRight is not their employer; customers acknowledge BuildRight does not guarantee worker quality; (d) if worker or customer reports safety incident: Store Manager logs per W140 and advises both parties of their independent responsibilities | Store Manager / HR / Legal | COO / VP Legal | 1–2 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Worker registration module (standalone, not employee HR module)
- In-store kiosk (W924) for worker directory display and job posting
- Mobile app (W615) for worker check-in and customer browsing
- SMS gateway for contact sharing and hiring confirmation
- Loyalty system (W551) for worker enrollment and store visit tracking
- Customer feedback module for hiring outcome tracking
- BI dashboard for program analytics (registrations, active workers, hiring frequency)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Co-employment liability**: The primary legal risk is that DOLE or workers may claim BuildRight is their de facto employer; mitigated by: (a) clear written terms that BuildRight is a bulletin board service only; (b) no fee charged to workers or customers; (c) BuildRight does not set rates, schedules, or working conditions; (d) workers are not required to purchase from BuildRight; (e) legal review per step 5c; (f) similar services (e.g., barangay livelihood centers) operate legally in the Philippines
- **Worker quality/safety incidents**: BuildRight does not verify worker skills or qualifications; a worker may perform substandard or dangerous work; clear disclaimers protect BuildRight; customer feedback system provides community self-regulation; workers with multiple negative reports are removed from directory
- **Worker disputes**: Workers and customers may have payment disputes; BuildRight does not mediate; directory terms explicitly state that BuildRight is not a party to any hiring arrangement; customer and worker resolve disputes independently
- **Data privacy**: Worker personal information (name, mobile number, address) must be protected per RA 10173; worker consent obtained at registration; customer access limited to selected worker's contact information; worker profiles not publicly searchable on the internet
- **Profiling/scam risk**: Fake worker profiles could be created to gain access to customer homes; government ID verification at step 1 mitigates; customer feedback system flags suspicious profiles; Store Manager review per step 5a


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "carpenter, mason, plumber, electrician, painter, tile setter, laborer/handyman…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "checks in at store kiosk or via SMS/App each morning by 7:00 AM to signal…" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-send of "sends worker's mobile number to customer via SMS" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: to purchase from BuildRight; operational: provides community self-regulation; operational: flags suspicious profiles

### Staffing Implication
- **Customer Service Representative**: ~30–60 min/day on worker registration (2–4 new registrations/day average); absorbed by existing role
- **Store Manager**: ~1–2 hours/month on program review; absorbed by existing role
- **HR**: ~2–3 hours/quarter on DOLE compliance review; absorbed by existing role
- **Legal**: ~4–6 hours/year on terms review and updates; absorbed by existing role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Worker registration: 10–15 min/worker
- Worker check-in: 1 min/day
- Customer hiring request: 5–10 min
- Hiring feedback: 2–3 min
- Program governance: 1–2 hours/month
- **Total per hiring facilitation**: ~15–25 min of staff time (mostly self-service)

## W960. Seasonal Forward Stock Pre-Positioning & Regional Buffer Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Seasonal demand forecast indicates upcoming spike requiring pre-positioned buffer stock at regional hubs (DCs and forward locations) |
| **Frequency** | 4–6 pre-positioning events/year (aligned with Philippine seasonal calendar: rainy season, summer, Christmas, back-to-school) |
| **Volume** | 50–200 SKUs per event; buffer stock value PHP 50–200M per event |
| **Owner** | Supply Chain Planning Manager |
| **Participants** | Merchandise Planner, Category Manager, DC Manager, Finance (working capital), Procurement, Transport Manager |

### Background

The Philippines' tropical climate and island geography create pronounced seasonal demand patterns: waterproofing materials and flood control items surge during the rainy season (June–August), garden and outdoor products peak in summer (March–May), and Christmas décor/appliances peak during the "ber" months (September–December). Post-typhoon reconstruction creates sudden, massive demand spikes for plywood, roofing, nails, cement, and hardware in affected regions — often overwhelming normal supply chains. Existing workflow W32 (Seasonal Buy Planning) covers the planning aspect; W960 extends into the operational execution of pre-positioning buffer stock at forward locations before seasonal demand hits. This includes: strategic buffer quantities at DCs, temporary forward staging areas for island-province stores, pre-allocated seasonal stock separate from regular replenishment, and post-season buffer drawdown. Effective pre-positioning ensures BuildRight has stock when competitors run out (capturing market share during peak demand) while avoiding post-season overstock (SLOB per W220).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Seasonal Demand Forecast & Buffer Quantification**: (a) Merchandise Planner runs seasonal demand model per W31 using: (i) historical seasonal sales (3-year rolling); (ii) weather forecast (PAGASA seasonal outlook); (iii) typhoon probability models for island regions; (iv) planned promotions per W13 calendar; (v) macroeconomic factors (remittance inflows for OFW construction projects, election-related construction spending); (b) Supply Chain Planning Manager quantifies buffer stock requirements per SKU per DC: buffer = (forecasted peak month demand × 1.5 safety factor) − (regular replenishment capacity during peak); (c) buffer stock segmented by: (i) guaranteed sellers (seasonal staples — cement, plywood, waterproofing, paint, nails — minimal markdown risk); (ii) seasonal specifics (Christmas lights, garden tools — higher markdown risk if unsold); (iii) emergency response items (tarpaulins, rope, flashlights — critical for post-typhoon demand, minimal markdown risk as items are evergreen) | Merchandise Planner / Supply Chain Manager | VP Supply Chain | 8–12 hours/event |
| 2 | **Working Capital Approval & Procurement**: (a) Finance reviews buffer stock investment: total buffer value, incremental carrying cost, and cash flow impact per W233; (b) tiered approval: (i) buffer value ≤ PHP 20M → VP Supply Chain approves; (ii) PHP 20–50M → COO approves; (iii) > PHP 50M → CEO approves; (c) Procurement executes seasonal buy orders per W2: (i) import orders (45–90 day lead time) placed 3–4 months before season; (ii) local orders placed 4–6 weeks before season; (iii) blanket PO releases (W2C) for guaranteed sellers with vendor volume commitment; (d) vendor delivery scheduled to arrive at DC 2–4 weeks before projected demand start | Finance / Procurement | COO / CEO | 4–8 hours (procurement) |
| 3 | **Buffer Stock Receipt & Pre-Positioning**: (a) DC receives seasonal buffer stock per W3; (b) DC Manager allocates storage: (i) dedicated seasonal buffer zone in DC (physically separated from regular stock for tracking); (ii) for island-region stores (Visayas, Mindanao provincial): Merchandise Planner arranges forward staging — pre-ship buffer to key stores or rented forward warehouse locations 2 weeks before season; (iii) buffer stock tagged in WMS with "SEASONAL BUFFER" status — not available for regular replenishment until season start date; (iv) buffer stock tracked separately in inventory module for cost and sell-through analysis | DC Manager / Merchandise Planner | Supply Chain Manager | 1–2 weeks |
| 4 | **In-Season Buffer Release & Monitoring**: (a) at season start date: system releases buffer stock for replenishment allocation; (b) weekly monitoring during season: (i) actual demand vs. forecast by SKU by region; (ii) buffer stock consumption rate; (iii) stockout risk assessment for fast-depleting buffers; (c) if demand exceeds forecast by > 20%: Supply Chain Manager triggers emergency procurement per W60 for top-selling items; (d) if demand trails forecast by > 20% at mid-season: Merchandise Planner adjusts replenishment to slow buffer consumption and begins planning post-season drawdown | Supply Chain Manager / Merchandise Planner | VP Supply Chain | 2–3 hours/week during season |
| 5 | **Post-Season Buffer Drawdown & Disposition**: At season end: (a) Merchandise Planner generates buffer drawdown report: (i) remaining buffer stock by SKU and location; (ii) classification: evergreen items (can be sold year-round — cement, nails, tools), seasonal-only items (Christmas lights, flood control), and perishable/degrading items (paint with shelf life); (b) disposition per classification: (i) evergreen items: released to regular inventory pool; no markdown needed; (ii) seasonal-only items: markdown per W93 (clearance) if not sold within 30 days post-season; (iii) last season's seasonal items: bulk liquidation or vendor return per W901 seasonal buy-back; (c) Finance calculates seasonal buffer ROI: (incremental gross margin from buffer sales + stockout avoidance value) − (incremental carrying cost + markdown cost + working capital opportunity cost); (d) lessons learned feed into next season's planning per W32 | Merchandise Planner / Category Manager / Finance | VP Supply Chain / CFO | 4–6 hours/post-season |

### System Touchpoints
- Demand forecasting module (W31) with seasonal modeling
- Inventory planning module with buffer stock functionality
- WMS (W3) with seasonal buffer zone management
- Procurement module (W2) for seasonal buy execution
- Working capital and cash flow module (W233) for investment approval
- Real-time inventory monitoring (W533) for buffer consumption tracking
- Replenishment planning module for buffer release timing
- Markdown management (W93) for post-season disposition
- Vendor buy-back agreement module (W901) for seasonal returns
- BI dashboard for seasonal buffer ROI analysis
- Financial reporting (W102) for seasonal category P&L

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Forecast accuracy**: Seasonal demand is inherently volatile — typhoon paths are unpredictable, consumer spending fluctuates with economic conditions; buffer stock either insufficient (stockouts, lost sales) or excessive (carrying cost, markdowns); mitigated by: (a) buffer segmented by risk (evergreen vs. seasonal-only); (b) phased ordering (60% upfront, 40% based on in-season actuals); (c) vendor buy-back agreements per W901 for seasonal items
- **Working capital constraint**: PHP 50–200M per event in buffer stock ties up significant working capital; Finance must model impact on cash flow per W233 and may limit buffer to available capital; prioritize highest-ROI SKUs (evergreen essentials with low markdown risk)
- **DC storage capacity**: Seasonal buffer competes with regular inventory for DC space; dedicated buffer zones prevent commingling; overflow may require temporary warehouse rental; DC surge planning per W652 addresses seasonal space requirements
- **Post-typhoon emergency demand**: The most valuable buffer deployment occurs after typhoons, which cannot be precisely forecasted; maintaining a standing "emergency response buffer" (tarpaulins, rope, plywood, nails, cement — PHP 10–20M value, evergreen items) at each DC year-round ensures rapid response per W927 without seasonal timing risk
- **Island logistics delay**: Forward staging to island locations requires advance shipping (1–2 weeks lead time for inter-island freight per W66); missed staging window means stock arrives after demand peak; tight coordination between Merchandise Planner and Transport Manager per W786


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-post of "post-typhoon demand, minimal markdown risk as items are evergreen" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-review of "reviews buffer stock investment" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-track of "Buffer Stock Receipt & Pre-Positioning" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Supply Chain Planning Manager review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Merchandise Planners**: ~8–12 hours/event on buffer quantification; absorbed by the existing Merchandise Planner team (5)
- **Supply Chain Planning Manager**: ~4–6 hours/week during season on buffer monitoring; absorbed by existing role
- **DC Managers**: ~2–4 hours/event on buffer zone setup; absorbed by existing role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Demand forecast & buffer quantification: 8–12 hours/event
- Working capital approval & procurement: 4–8 hours
- Buffer receipt & pre-positioning: 1–2 weeks (operational)
- In-season monitoring: 2–3 hours/week
- Post-season drawdown: 4–6 hours
- **Total per event**: ~20–30 hours of planning time + operational execution

## W964. Customer Bulk Cement, Sand & Aggregates Order & Direct-to-Site Delivery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (contractor, developer, or homeowner) orders bulk cement, sand, gravel, or aggregates for construction site delivery |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–10,000 orders/month chain-wide (~40–50/store/month) |
| **Volume** | Average order: 50–100 bags of cement (40kg each) or 1–3 cubic meters of sand/gravel; high-value orders: 500+ bags or 10+ cubic meters |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer, Receiving Clerk, Delivery Coordinator, Category Manager, 3PL/Delivery Partner |

### Background

Cement, sand, gravel, and aggregates (crushed stone, base course, filler sand) are the highest-volume, lowest-margin products in a hardware retailer's assortment. These heavy construction materials are impractical to stock in standard store backrooms and are typically sold through direct-to-site delivery arrangements where vendor trucks deliver directly to the customer's construction site, with BuildRight acting as the ordering and billing intermediary. The Philippine construction industry consumes enormous volumes of these materials — a typical residential house construction requires 400–600 bags of cement and 15–25 cubic meters of sand and gravel. BuildRight's 200-store network and vendor relationships enable competitive pricing and reliable delivery scheduling that independent hardware stores cannot match. This workflow handles the full lifecycle: customer order, vendor scheduling, site delivery coordination, proof of delivery, and financial reconciliation. This is a core B2B revenue driver, particularly for the trade and corporate customer segments.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Order Intake & Site Information**: Customer provides: (a) material type — Portland cement (bagged, 40kg), Portland cement (bulk, by ton), sand (fine, coarse, washed), gravel (3/4", 1/2", base course), aggregates as specified; (b) quantity — bags of cement, cubic meters (sand/gravel), or metric tons; (c) delivery address — construction site address with landmark description (Philippine addresses often lack precise street numbering); (d) site access conditions — truck accessibility (10-wheeler, 6-wheeler, or small truck only), road restrictions (LGU truck ban hours per W431), low-clearance obstacles (overhead wires, footbridges), unloading method (manual carry, chute, boom pump); (e) preferred delivery date and time window; (f) customer type — walk-in, trade account (per W897), corporate account (per W460), or project account (per W162) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Pricing, Availability & Quotation**: System checks: (a) current cement price per bag — subject to frequent market fluctuations (Philippine cement prices vary PHP 20–50/bag month-to-month); system pulls latest vendor contract price per W62 and adds BuildRight margin; (b) sand/gravel price per cubic meter — varies by source quarry, distance to delivery site, and current market rate; (c) delivery fee — calculated based on: distance from nearest vendor depot or BuildRight yard to delivery site, vehicle type required, unloading method, and LGU truck ban surcharge (if delivery window falls within restricted hours per W431); (d) volume discount — tiered pricing for orders ≥100 bags cement, ≥5 cubic meters sand/gravel; system generates quotation including: materials cost, delivery fee, applicable taxes (VAT 12%), and payment terms (cash on delivery for non-account customers; net 30 for trade/corporate accounts per W24); quotation valid for 3 days due to cement price volatility | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Order Confirmation & Payment**: Customer accepts quotation; payment processing: (a) walk-in/non-account — full payment at POS or upon delivery (COD); (b) trade account — charged to credit account per W24, subject to available credit limit; (c) corporate/project account — per project billing terms per W165, may include progressive billing; Sales Associate creates bulk delivery order in ERP: order type = "Bulk Material Direct Delivery"; linked to customer account; delivery site address and access conditions recorded; preferred delivery date scheduled; system sends order confirmation via SMS/email with delivery details | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Vendor Scheduling & Logistics Coordination**: Back-office Delivery Coordinator (or Sales Associate at smaller stores) contacts vendor/3PL to schedule delivery: (a) confirms material availability at vendor depot or quarry; (b) schedules truck and driver — vendor-supplied truck (standard) or BuildRight-contracted 3PL truck; (c) confirms delivery date and time window with customer via phone/SMS; (d) for multi-drop orders (large quantities requiring multiple truckloads): coordinates sequential deliveries with customer's construction foreman; (e) records truck plate number, driver name, and driver contact number in ERP for tracking; (f) generates delivery receipt (DR) with: material type, quantity ordered, delivery address, customer name, and space for receiving signature | Delivery Coordinator / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–20 min |
| 5 | **Site Delivery Execution & Proof of Delivery**: Vendor/3PL truck arrives at construction site: (a) driver contacts customer's site contact (construction foreman or homeowner) upon arrival; (b) unloading method executed per order specification: manual carry (crew stacks bags at designated staging area on site), chute (sand/gravel discharged directly from truck bed to ground pile), boom pump (for sites with restricted access — additional fee); (c) customer's site representative counts and verifies quantity received against delivery receipt; (d) driver and customer representative sign delivery receipt — customer copy and driver copy; (e) any quantity discrepancy (short delivery, spillage) noted on DR and photographed; (f) driver returns signed DR to Delivery Coordinator | Delivery Partner / Customer Site Rep | Delivery Coordinator | 30–90 min (varies by volume and unloading method) |
| 6 | **Delivery Reconciliation & Financial Settlement**: Delivery Coordinator reconciles: (a) signed DR quantity vs. ordered quantity — if short delivery: system generates credit note per W101 for undelivered quantity; if over delivery: customer charged for additional quantity; (b) delivery condition — if material damage reported (wet cement bags, contaminated sand): system creates quality complaint per W110 and vendor chargeback per W245; (c) financial posting: revenue recognized at delivery confirmation; COGS posted from vendor; delivery fee revenue recognized; for trade/corporate accounts: invoice generated and posted to AR per W8; for COD: payment reconciled against delivery receipt | Delivery Coordinator / Finance | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS bulk order module with material pricing, volume discounts, and delivery fee calculator
- Customer account and credit limit check (W24, W460) for trade/corporate accounts
- Vendor contract pricing integration (W62) for real-time cement/sand price lookup
- Delivery scheduling and route planning module (W196) with LGU truck ban awareness (W431)
- Delivery receipt generation and proof-of-delivery capture (mobile/electronic signature)
- Inventory decrement — materials bypass store inventory; goods-in-transit tracked at vendor depot until delivery
- Revenue and COGS posting triggered by delivery confirmation
- SMS/email notification engine for customer delivery updates
- Vendor 3PL performance tracking per W242

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Cement price volatility**: Philippine cement prices fluctuate due to manufacturer price adjustments, supply disruptions, and seasonal demand; quotations must have short validity (3 days); system must track latest vendor price and update POS pricing promptly; frequent price changes require clear communication to customers at order intake
- **Site access challenges**: Philippine construction sites in dense urban areas (Metro Manila, Cebu) often have narrow streets, overhead obstructions, and limited truck turning radius; incorrect site assessment leads to failed deliveries and redelivery costs; Delivery Coordinator must verify site access conditions at order intake and match appropriate vehicle type
- **Quantity disputes at delivery**: Customer's site representative may dispute the delivered quantity, especially for sand/gravel which is measured by volume (cubic meters) and can settle during transport; standardized volumetric measurement at loading (leveled truck bed measurement) and photographic evidence at delivery mitigate disputes
- **Weather disruption**: Rain damages bagged cement and makes sand/gravel unloading difficult on muddy sites; delivery postponement protocol required; system must support easy rescheduling; pre-positioning under cover or tarpaulin at vendor depot during rainy season per W960
- **Cash handling for COD deliveries**: Driver collects cash payment at site for COD orders; cash security risk for drivers carrying large amounts (a PHP 100,000+ order is common); armored car escort for orders exceeding PHP 200,000; electronic payment (GCash, bank transfer) preferred and incentivized with 1% discount


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "Customer Order Intake & Site Information" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "Pricing, Availability & Quotation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-record of "Order Confirmation & Payment" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: must track latest vendor price and update POS pricing promptly; operational: must support easy rescheduling

### Staffing Implication
- **Delivery Coordinator**: 1 per store (may be shared role with Receiving Clerk at smaller stores) handles bulk delivery scheduling; activity consumes ~1–2 hours/day at 40–50 orders/month (per the Frequency); absorbed by existing Receiving Clerk role at stores with <30 bulk orders/month
- **Training**: 8-hour bulk material ordering and logistics coordination training per W51
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Order intake & site information: 10–15 min
- Pricing & quotation: 5–10 min
- Order confirmation & payment: 5–10 min
- Vendor scheduling & logistics: 10–20 min
- Site delivery execution: 30–90 min (3PL/vendor time)
- Delivery reconciliation: 10–15 min
- **Total per order of BuildRight staff time**: 40–70 min (delivery execution is vendor-performed)

## W966. Customer Construction Loan Documentation Assistance & Partner Bank Referral

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquires about financing options for home construction, renovation, or improvement project at store |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 referrals/month chain-wide (~8–12/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1 referral per customer; estimated 30–40% conversion to approved loan |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer, Store Manager, Partner Bank Loan Officer, Category Manager |

### Background

Home construction and major renovation projects in the Philippines typically cost PHP 500,000 to PHP 5,000,000+, well beyond the cash capacity of most Filipino homeowners. Construction financing — through bank home improvement loans, Pag-IBIG (HDMF) housing loans, or SSS salary loans — is a critical enabler for BuildRight's higher-value sales. Many customers begin their renovation journey at BuildRight stores, where they select materials and discover the total project cost exceeds their budget. By offering construction loan documentation assistance and direct referral to partner banks, BuildRight: (a) converts budget-constrained prospects into completed sales; (b) earns referral fees from partner banks (typically 1–2% of loan amount); (c) differentiates from competitors who offer no financing support; (d) builds long-term customer relationships as the materials supplier for the loan-funded project. This workflow covers the in-store assistance and referral process; actual loan processing and approval is performed by the partner bank.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Financing Need Identification**: During any high-value sales interaction (typical trigger: customer's material list exceeds PHP 100,000), Sales Associate or Store Manager identifies financing need when: (a) customer expresses concern about total project cost; (b) customer asks about installment or payment plans; (c) customer is selecting materials for a major renovation/construction project; Sales Associate introduces financing option: "BuildRight works with partner banks to help customers finance their home improvement projects. Would you like to learn about construction loan options?" | Sales Associate | Store Manager | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **Financing Option Presentation & Pre-Qualification**: Store Manager or designated Sales Associate presents financing options: (a) **Bank Home Improvement Loan** — partner banks (BDO, BPI, Metrobank, Security Bank): PHP 100,000–3,000,000 loan amount, 12–60 month terms, 8–14% interest rate, requires proof of income and property documents; (b) **Pag-IBIG (HDMF) Housing Loan** — government housing loan for members: lower interest rates (3–6.5%), longer terms (up to 30 years), requires Pag-IBIG membership; (c) **SSS Salary Loan** — for employed SSS members: smaller amounts (PHP 20,000–40,000), quick processing, deducted from salary; (d) **Credit Card Installment** — 0% interest promos per W747 for qualifying cardholders; (e) **Contractor Micro-Lending** — per W953 for registered trade professionals; Sales Associate performs informal pre-qualification check: customer employment status, estimated monthly income, existing loan obligations, property ownership status; Sales Associate recommends most suitable option(s) | Store Manager / Sales Associate | Store Manager | 10–15 min |
| 3 | **Material Cost Documentation Preparation**: Sales Associate generates BuildRight Material Cost Document: (a) detailed itemized quotation from POS per W542 with all materials, quantities, and prices for the planned project; (b) quotation printed on BuildRight letterhead with store stamp — accepted by partner banks as supporting documentation for loan application; (c) quotation includes: customer name, project description, total material cost, validity period (30 days), and store contact information for bank verification; (d) for larger projects: Sales Associate attaches project scope description and material specifications from W967 (material takeoff) if available; (e) system flags quotation as "Loan Application — Pending" status to track conversion | Sales Associate | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Partner Bank Referral & Handover**: Sales Associate provides customer with: (a) BuildRight Material Cost Document (quotation); (b) partner bank referral card — includes bank name, branch, loan officer contact, and BuildRight referral code; (c) checklist of required documents for loan application: government-issued ID, proof of income (payslips, ITR, COE), proof of billing address, property documents (tax declaration, land title if applicable), bank statements (3 months); (d) system logs referral: customer ID, estimated project value, referred bank, referral date, referral code; Sales Associate encourages customer to visit partner bank within 7 days and offers to schedule a bank appointment; partner bank loan officer notified of referral via secure portal | Sales Associate / Customer | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Conversion Tracking & Follow-Up**: System tracks referral lifecycle: (a) partner bank updates referral status via secure portal: Application Submitted → Under Review → Approved → Disbursed / Declined; (b) upon loan approval and disbursement: partner bank notifies BuildRight; Store Manager or Sales Associate contacts customer to convert quotation into confirmed order; customer pays using loan proceeds (bank-to-bank transfer or manager's check); (c) referral fee: system calculates referral fee (1–2% of loan amount) and records as other income; quarterly settlement with partner bank per agreement; (d) for declined referrals: Sales Associate follows up within 7 days to discuss alternative options — smaller project scope, different materials, or alternative financing (credit card installment per W747); (e) monthly: Store Manager reviews referral conversion dashboard — referral count, conversion rate, total loan-funded revenue, referral fee income | System / Sales Associate / Store Manager | Store Manager | Ongoing |

### System Touchpoints
- POS quotation module (W542) with "Loan Application" quotation type
- Partner bank referral portal integration (secure API for referral logging and status updates)
- Customer account integration for referral tracking
- Conversion tracking dashboard (referral → loan → sale)
- Referral fee calculation and settlement tracking
- SMS/email notification for customer follow-up
- Credit card installment integration (W747)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Referral conversion rate**: Not all referrals convert to approved loans; typical Philippine bank home improvement loan approval rate is 30–40% for referred customers; store staff may invest significant time in non-converting referrals; pre-qualification screening at step 2 improves conversion; referral tracking system provides visibility
- **Customer data privacy**: Sharing customer information with partner banks requires explicit consent per RA 10173 (Data Privacy Act); consent form signed at referral; partner bank portal must comply with NPC requirements per W434; customer data shared only with customer-selected bank
- **Quotation validity vs. loan processing time**: Bank loan processing takes 2–4 weeks; BuildRight material prices may change during this period; quotation extended to 30 days for loan applications (vs. 7 days standard); if prices change significantly (>5%) during loan processing, BuildRight honors original quotation as a goodwill gesture for loan-referred customers
- **Partner bank relationship management**: Bank relationships require ongoing maintenance — joint marketing, co-branded events, staff training on loan products, and quarterly business reviews; Category Manager or Store Manager dedicates ~4 hours/month to bank relationship activities per W617


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-document of "Financing Option Presentation & Pre-Qualification" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "printed on BuildRight letterhead with store stamp — accepted by partner banks…" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-log of "Partner Bank Referral & Handover" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: provides visibility

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: Primary owner of bank referral program at store level; financing conversations consume ~10–20 min/day during peak construction season (Jan–May, Sep–Dec; ~8–12 referrals/store/month per the Frequency); absorbed by existing Store Manager role
- **Training**: 4-hour partner bank loan product training per W51; quarterly refresher from bank loan officers
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Financing need identification: 2–3 min
- Option presentation & pre-qualification: 10–15 min
- Material cost documentation: 5–10 min
- Bank referral & handover: 5–10 min
- Conversion tracking & follow-up: ongoing (5 min per follow-up call)
- **Total per referral**: 25–40 min of staff time

## W968. Customer Multi-Store Aggregated Order & Consolidated Single Delivery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer wants to purchase items available across multiple BuildRight stores and receive a single consolidated delivery |
| **Frequency** | ~1,000–1,500 orders/month chain-wide (~5–7/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 3–8 items per order; average order value PHP 15,000–45,000 |
| **Owner** | Ecommerce Manager |
| **Participants** | Customer, Sales Associate (or Ecommerce Ops), Store Operations (multiple stores), DC/Logistics, Category Manager |

### Background

With 35,000 active SKUs across 200 stores, no single store stocks every item. Customers — especially B2B trade professionals and homeowners with large project lists — frequently encounter situations where the items they need are distributed across multiple BuildRight locations. Without this workflow, customers must: (a) visit multiple stores personally; (b) place separate delivery orders from each store incurring multiple delivery fees; or (c) forgo purchasing unavailable items from BuildRight and source them from competitors. The multi-store aggregated order capability enables BuildRight to capture the customer's entire spend by consolidating items from multiple stores into a single delivery. Items are either transferred to a designated consolidation store (typically the customer's nearest store) or consolidated at the nearest DC, then delivered to the customer in a single shipment. This workflow is particularly valuable for project-based B2B customers (W162) and large home renovation projects.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Order Placement & Item Sourcing**: Customer places order via: (a) ecommerce website/app — adds all desired items to cart; system automatically identifies items not available at the customer's preferred store and sources from other locations; (b) in-store Sales Associate — customer provides shopping list; Sales Associate checks availability across stores using ERP location inventory view (W533); (c) phone/email to call center — customer provides list; agent creates order and sources items; for each unavailable item at preferred store, system identifies: nearest store with stock, stock quantity, and estimated transfer time to consolidation point | Customer / Sales Associate / System | Ecommerce Manager | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Consolidation Point Selection & Routing**: System determines optimal consolidation point: (a) if all sourced items can reach a single store within 2 business days — consolidation at customer's preferred store; (b) if items are spread across multiple regions — consolidation at nearest DC which serves as cross-dock point; (c) if any item requires vendor procurement (out of stock at all locations) — system flags item with estimated lead time and offers customer choice: (i) wait for full consolidation, (ii) partial delivery now + remaining items later, (iii) remove item from order; system calculates: transfer costs (inter-store/inter-DC), consolidation handling fee (if applicable), and single delivery fee to customer address | System / Ecommerce Ops | Ecommerce Manager | 5–10 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Inter-Location Transfer Orders Creation**: System automatically creates transfer orders: (a) for each source store: pick list generated for items to ship to consolidation point; (b) transfer orders routed per W22 (inter-store) or W218 (inter-DC); (c) priority flag set based on customer's requested delivery date; (d) source stores pick and ship items within 24–48 hours of transfer order receipt; (e) system tracks in-transit inventory per source location; (f) consolidation point receives items and stages in designated aggregation area (separate from regular BOPIS staging) | System / Store Operations | Ecommerce Manager | Automated (24–48 hrs transfer time) |
| 4 | **Consolidation Quality Check & Delivery Execution**: At consolidation point: (a) Receiving Clerk verifies all transferred items received in good condition against transfer order; (b) any damaged items flagged — replacement sourced or customer notified of refund per W101; (c) all items consolidated into single delivery package/batch; (d) delivery scheduled via: (i) BuildRight own fleet (for bulky items) per W196, (ii) 3PL partner (Lalamove, Transportify) per W548; (e) customer receives consolidated delivery notification with tracking; (f) delivery executed per customer's chosen method: home delivery per W19 or store pickup per W11 | Receiving Clerk / Delivery Team | Ecommerce Manager | 30–60 min |
| 5 | **Financial Reconciliation & Inter-Store Settlement**: System handles financial flows: (a) revenue recognized at consolidation point (delivering store/DC); (b) inter-store inventory movement accounted per W919 (intercompany inventory movement accounting); (c) cost of goods transferred from source stores to consolidation point; (d) single customer invoice generated covering all items regardless of sourcing location; (e) delivery fee charged once (consolidated delivery benefit); (f) monthly: Finance reconciles inter-store transfer settlements per intercompany accounting (W14) | System / Finance | Ecommerce Manager | Automated (monthly reconciliation) |

### System Touchpoints
- Unified order management system (W536) with multi-location sourcing engine
- Real-time inventory availability across all 200 stores and 4 DCs (W533)
- Automated transfer order creation and tracking (W22, W218)
- Consolidation point staging area management
- Delivery scheduling and routing (W196) for consolidated shipment
- Inter-store financial settlement (W14, W919)
- Customer notification engine (W708) for status updates at each phase
- Ecommerce cart with multi-source item visibility

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Transfer delay propagation**: The consolidated delivery timeline is determined by the slowest source store; if one store delays its transfer, the entire order is held; SLA of 24–48 hours for inter-store transfers must be enforced; system escalates overdue transfers to Store Manager
- **Item damage during additional handling**: Each transfer adds handling risk — items are picked at source, transported, received at consolidation point, then delivered to customer; fragile items (tiles, glass, light fixtures) require enhanced packaging at source store; damage claims per W500
- **Customer expectation management**: Customers expect multi-store orders to be as fast as single-store orders; clear communication of consolidated delivery timeline (2–4 business days vs. same-day/next-day for in-stock) at order placement prevents disappointment; real-time status updates per item per location
- **Inventory reservation race condition**: Items identified as available at order placement may be sold to another customer before the transfer pick occurs; system must reserve inventory at source stores upon order confirmation (hard allocation) rather than soft allocation


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "center — customer provides list" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "Consolidation Point Selection & Routing" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "generated for items to ship to consolidation point" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: escalates overdue transfers to Store Manager

### Staffing Implication
- **No incremental headcount** — workflow is primarily system-automated with execution absorbed by existing store operations and ecommerce teams
- **Receiving Clerk**: Additional 15–30 min/day for consolidation receiving at designated stores; absorbed by existing role

### Time Estimate
- Order placement & sourcing: 10–15 min
- Consolidation routing (automated): 5–10 min
- Transfer order fulfillment: 24–48 hrs (store operations)
- Consolidation & delivery: 30–60 min
- **Total of active BuildRight staff time per order**: 45–90 min (excluding transfer transit time)

## W970. Customer Post-Disaster Insurance Claim Material Replacement Coordination

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents insurance claim document for damaged building materials that need replacement after a natural disaster (typhoon, flood, earthquake, fire) |
| **Frequency** | ~500–1,000 coordination events/month average; surges to 3,000–5,000/month post-major typhoon |
| **Volume** | 1 insurance claim per customer; average claim material value PHP 50,000–300,000 |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Customer, Store Manager, Sales Associate, Insurance Company Representative, Category Manager, Delivery Coordinator |

### Background

The Philippines experiences an average of 20 typhoons per year, with 5–8 making landfall as severe storms. Additionally, earthquakes, flooding, and fires cause regular property damage. Post-disaster rebuilding creates massive demand surges for building materials — roofing sheets, plywood, cement, lumber, nails, paint, electrical wiring, plumbing pipes, and tiles. Customers with property insurance must navigate the claim process: documenting damage, obtaining insurance adjuster assessment, receiving claim approval with a specified payout or material replacement value, and then sourcing replacement materials. BuildRight's post-disaster insurance claim coordination service provides a critical bridge between the insurance claim and material procurement: (a) helping customers interpret their insurance claim scope and match it to BuildRight's product catalog; (b) providing itemized material lists and pricing for insurance documentation; (c) coordinating delivery to damaged properties, often in areas with compromised infrastructure; (d) managing the payment flow between insurance company, customer, and BuildRight. This service positions BuildRight as the preferred post-disaster materials supplier and generates significant community goodwill.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Intake & Insurance Claim Document Review**: Customer arrives at store (or contacts via phone/email) with insurance claim documentation: (a) insurance claim letter/notice of loss — specifies damaged items, approved claim amount, and claim reference number; (b) insurance adjuster's damage assessment report — details scope of damage and approved repair scope; (c) photographs of damage — customer-provided or adjuster documentation; (d) property insurance policy number and insurance company contact; Store Manager or designated Sales Associate reviews claim documents for: completeness, claim validity period, payment terms (reimbursement vs. direct payment to supplier), and any material specification requirements from the insurer | Store Manager / Sales Associate | Store Manager | 15–25 min |
| 2 | **Damage-to-Material Translation & Itemized List**: Sales Associate translates insurance claim scope into BuildRight material list: (a) roofing damage → roofing sheets (specify type: GI corrugated, long-span, ribbed), ridge caps, roofing nails, waterproofing sealant, insulation; (b) flood damage → replacement electrical wiring, outlets, switches, panel board (per electrical code), wall paint, floor tiles, baseboards; (c) structural damage → cement, rebar, hollow blocks, sand, gravel, lumber, plywood, nails; (d) for each item: system identifies matching BuildRight SKU, current price, and availability; (e) quantities estimated based on damage scope per insurance adjuster report; (f) itemized material list generated per W542 (quotation) with BuildRight letterhead — suitable for insurance company review and approval | Sales Associate / System | Store Manager | 20–40 min |
| 3 | **Insurance Company Coordination & Approval**: Store Manager coordinates with insurance company (if direct billing arrangement): (a) sends itemized material list and quotation to insurance adjuster/company for approval; (b) insurance company reviews and may: (i) approve list as-is; (ii) request substitutions (e.g., lower-cost alternative materials); (iii) cap total at claim amount and customer covers difference; (c) payment arrangement confirmed: (i) insurance company issues check to BuildRight directly (direct settlement); (ii) insurance company reimburses customer, customer pays BuildRight; (iii) insurance company issues guarantee letter — BuildRight bills insurance company post-delivery; (d) approved material list finalized and linked to claim reference number in ERP | Store Manager / Sales Associate | Store Manager | 30–60 min (may span multiple interactions over 2–5 days) |
| 4 | **Material Procurement & Delivery Coordination**: Given post-disaster supply constraints: (a) system checks inventory — post-disaster demand surges may deplete local stock; (b) if local stockout: Store Manager escalates to Category Manager for emergency replenishment per W60, inter-store transfer per W22, or DC emergency dispatch per W927; (c) delivery coordination challenges: damaged roads, restricted access, curfew hours, LGU checkpoint permits — Delivery Coordinator plans route per current accessibility conditions; (d) delivery scheduled with customer's site contact (may be construction foreman, not homeowner); (e) proof of delivery documentation required for insurance claim — DR with photographs of delivered materials at site | Delivery Coordinator / Store Manager | Store Manager | 30–60 min |
| 5 | **Post-Delivery Claim Closure & Follow-Up**: After delivery: (a) Store Manager sends delivery confirmation and proof of delivery to insurance company for claim closure; (b) invoice and payment processed per agreed payment arrangement; (c) system tracks insurance claim from intake to closure with full documentation trail; (d) Store Manager follows up with customer within 2 weeks: any additional materials needed? Installation service referral per W138; (e) post-disaster recovery analysis: Category Manager reviews demand surge data to improve forward stock pre-positioning per W960 for future events | Store Manager / Category Manager | Store Manager | 15–30 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS quotation module (W542) with insurance claim linkage
- Insurance claim tracking module — claim reference, status, documentation, payment tracking
- Real-time inventory check with emergency replenishment trigger (W60)
- Inter-store transfer and DC emergency dispatch (W22, W927)
- Delivery scheduling with disaster-area route planning (W196)
- Proof of delivery with photographic evidence capture
- Insurance company portal (for direct-billing partners) — claim submission, approval, invoicing
- Post-disaster demand analytics for supply chain planning (W960)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Post-disaster stockout**: Demand surges of 300–500% for key materials (roofing, plywood, cement, nails) can deplete store and DC inventory within hours; emergency procurement and inter-store transfers required; Category Manager pre-positioning per W960 mitigates but cannot eliminate stockout risk; clear customer communication on availability and expected restock timeline
- **Insurance claim processing delays**: Insurance claim approval can take 2–8 weeks; customers may need emergency materials (tarpaulins, temporary roofing) before claim is approved; BuildRight offers emergency materials at cost (no margin) as goodwill gesture to claim-registered customers, with reimbursement upon claim approval
- **Fraud risk**: Post-disaster environment attracts fraudulent claims — inflated damage reports, fictitious claims; Sales Associates must verify claim documentation authenticity (insurance company direct verification, claim reference validation); suspicious claims escalated to Store Manager and LP per W837
- **Infrastructure challenges**: Post-typhoon areas may have impassable roads, downed power lines, flooded access routes; delivery trucks may be unable to reach customer sites; creative solutions: smaller vehicle relay, customer pickup at accessible transfer point, coordination with LGU disaster response for road clearance priority


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-document of "Customer Intake & Insurance Claim Document Review" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-review of "review and approval" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "check to BuildRight directly" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Store Manager review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: Primary owner during post-disaster periods; claim coordination consumes 2–4 hours/day during surge periods; absorbed by existing role; support from designated Sales Associate at high-volume stores
- **Training**: 4-hour post-disaster insurance claim coordination training per W51; annual refresher aligned with typhoon season preparation per W576
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Customer intake & document review: 15–25 min
- Damage-to-material translation: 20–40 min
- Insurance coordination: 30–60 min (over 2–5 days)
- Procurement & delivery coordination: 30–60 min
- Post-delivery closure: 15–30 min
- **Total of active staff time per claim**: 110–215 min (spread over 1–3 weeks)
