## W1027. Customer AI-Powered Chatbot & Virtual Shopping Assistant Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer initiates chat on buildright.com.ph, mobile app, or social media messenger (Facebook Messenger, Viber) |
| **Frequency** | ~15,000–20,000 chatbot interactions/month (one register with W1340; ~60% chatbot-resolved) |
| **Volume** | 1 session per interaction; ~40% resolve without human agent |
| **Owner** | Ecommerce Operations Manager |
| **Participants** | Customer, AI Chatbot (system), Human Agent (escalation), Product Content Manager, IT AI/ML Engineer |

### Background

BuildRight Depot's digital channels serve ~42,900 ecommerce orders/month plus ~200,000+ website/app visitors browsing the 35,000 SKU catalog. Customer inquiries span product availability ("Is Makita HR2470 hammer drill available at Davao Buhangin branch?"), pricing ("How much is Wilcon 4×8 plywood?"), product comparison ("Bosch vs DeWalt angle grinder for concrete cutting?"), order status ("Where is my order #BR20260608-1234?"), and technical questions ("What size circuit breaker for 1.5HP aircon?"). The call center (30 agents) and customer service email handle ~15,000–20,000 inquiries/month (W259), with average handle time of 5–8 minutes per inquiry. An AI-powered chatbot trained on BuildRight's product catalog, store inventory, pricing, and order data can resolve 35–45% of inquiries without human agent involvement, reducing call center volume by ~6,000–8,000 interactions/month and providing 24/7 self-service support (critical for Filipino customers who browse and chat late evening, 8PM–12AM peak). This workflow manages the chatbot operations, content training, escalation to human agents, and continuous improvement. This is distinct from W200 (AI personalization engine) and W735 (customer onboarding journey); it focuses on conversational AI customer support.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Inquiry Intake & Intent Classification**: Customer initiates chat via any channel: (a) buildright.com.ph chat widget, (b) BuildRight mobile app, (c) Facebook Messenger, (d) Viber; (e) system identifies customer (if logged in via loyalty account) with purchase history, store preference, and active orders; (f) AI chatbot processes customer message using natural language understanding (NLU): classifies intent into one of ~25 predefined categories: product_availability, price_inquiry, product_comparison, order_status, delivery_tracking, return_refund, installation_inquiry, store_location_hours, loyalty_points, payment_issue, technical_specification, project_advice, bulk_order, trade_account, complaint, promotion_inquiry, and others; (g) for multi-intent messages ("Is the Bosch drill available and how does it compare to DeWalt?"), system decomposes into sequential sub-intents | System (AI Chatbot) | Ecommerce Operations Manager | < 5 sec (automated) |
| 2 | **Automated Response & Self-Service Resolution**: Based on classified intent: (a) **Product availability**: chatbot queries real-time store inventory (customer's nearest store from profile or asked); responds with availability, quantity on hand, and aisle location (e.g., "The Bosch GSB 550 Impact Drill is available at BuildRight Davao Buhangin — 7 units in stock, Aisle 4, Power Tools section. Want me to reserve one for pickup?"); (b) **Price inquiry**: responds with current SRP, any active promotions, trade price (if trade account), and competitor price match eligibility per W61; (c) **Product comparison**: generates side-by-side comparison card with specifications, price, customer ratings, and recommendation based on customer's stated use case; (d) **Order status/delivery tracking**: retrieves order from system, provides current status and estimated delivery date per W592; (e) **Technical specification**: responds with product specs from PIM (W50) including dimensions, weight, power requirements, compatible accessories; (f) **Store location/hours**: responds with store address, operating hours, phone number, and Google Maps link; (g) system attempts to resolve within 3 conversational turns; if resolved, customer rates interaction (1–5 stars) and session ends | System (AI Chatbot) | Ecommerce Operations Manager | 1–3 min (customer interaction) |
| 3 | **Human Agent Escalation**: If chatbot cannot resolve within 3 turns, or customer requests human agent, or intent is flagged as high-complexity (complaint, trade account application, project pricing): (a) system transfers chat to available human agent with full conversation history and customer context; (b) customer sees "Connecting you to a BuildRight team member..." message; (c) target transfer time: < 30 seconds during operating hours (8AM–10PM); outside hours, system offers callback or email ticket creation per W258; (d) human agent resolves per existing workflows: complaints (W41), credit application (W886), delivery issue (W1004), product question (W112); (e) upon resolution, agent tags conversation for chatbot training: "resolved by human" with intent category and resolution method — feeds step 5 | Human Agent | Customer Service Supervisor | 5–15 min per escalation |
| 4 | **Product Content & Knowledge Base Maintenance**: Product Content Manager maintains chatbot knowledge base: (a) weekly: review new product launches (W564) and update product database with specifications, comparison data, FAQs, and recommended use cases; (b) weekly: review and update promotional information, pricing changes, and store-level inventory availability logic; (c) monthly: review chatbot response accuracy metrics and identify knowledge gaps (topics where chatbot frequently escalates to human); (d) quarterly: comprehensive knowledge base audit — verify product specifications, pricing, and availability data are current; update product comparison matrices for top 500 SKUs; (e) coordinate with Category Managers for technical product training content per W920 | Product Content Manager | Ecommerce Operations Manager | 4–6 hours/week |
| 5 | **AI Model Performance Monitoring & Continuous Improvement**: IT AI/ML Engineer monitors chatbot performance: (a) daily: review escalation rate (target: < 60%), customer satisfaction score (target: ≥ 4.0/5.0), and average session duration; (b) weekly: review intent classification accuracy — analyze misclassified conversations and retrain NLU model; (c) monthly: analyze "unresolved" conversations to identify new intent categories needed; expand response library; (d) quarterly: A/B test new response templates, product recommendation strategies, and conversation flows; (e) key metrics dashboard: resolution rate, escalation rate, average handle time (chatbot vs. human), customer satisfaction, cost per interaction (chatbot: ~PHP 5–10 vs. human agent: ~PHP 80–120) | IT AI/ML Engineer | Ecommerce Operations Manager | 8–12 hours/week |

### System Touchpoints
- AI chatbot platform integrated with: NLU engine, product catalog (PIM/W50), real-time inventory system, pricing engine, order management system, delivery tracking API (W592), loyalty system
- Omnichannel messaging integration: web chat widget, mobile app SDK, Facebook Messenger API, Viber API
- Human agent handoff module with conversation history and customer context transfer
- Customer satisfaction rating system (in-chat, 1–5 stars + optional comment)
- Knowledge base / content management system for product data, FAQs, and response templates
- Analytics dashboard: resolution rate, escalation rate, intent distribution, customer satisfaction, cost savings
- NLU model training pipeline with conversation log analysis

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Hallucination risk**: AI chatbot may generate incorrect product specifications, pricing, or availability information; guardrails must enforce that all product data responses are retrieved from authoritative systems (PIM, pricing engine, inventory) rather than generated from training data; product recommendations must be factually accurate to avoid customer complaints and liability; mitigated by CTL-266 (controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1))
- **Tagalog/Visaya language support**: Filipino customers often mix English and Tagalog (Taglish) or regional languages (Cebuano, Ilonggo) in chat; the NLU model must be trained on Philippine language patterns; initial deployment should support English and Taglish; regional language support is phase 2
- **Inventory accuracy**: Chatbot tells customer "7 units in stock" but actual shelf is empty due to shrinkage or receiving lag; inventory data shown to chatbot should include buffer discount (show "available" only if system on-hand > 3 units) and include "subject to availability" disclaimer
- **Escalation during peak**: During promotional events (bi-monthly sales), chat volume may spike 300%; human agent queue may exceed capacity; chatbot must handle 50%+ during peaks to prevent abandonment; temporary agent surge staffing per W555


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "logged in via loyalty account" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "Automated Response & Self-Service Resolution" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-flag of "flagged as high-complexity" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: buffer-discount display rule (show "available" only if on-hand > 3 units) and "subject to availability" disclaimer

### Staffing Implication
- **Product Content Manager**: Chatbot knowledge base maintenance adds 4–6 hours/week; partially offset by reduced manual FAQ handling
- **IT AI/ML Engineer**: Chatbot model monitoring and improvement requires 8–12 hours/week; absorbed by existing Innovation/IT team (W200, W689)
- **Call Center Agents**: Chatbot resolves ~6,000–8,000 interactions/month without human agent; equivalent to ~4–5 full-time agents freed; reallocate to complex issue resolution and outbound proactive service per W756
- **Net headcount impact**: 0 (redistribution of existing headcount)

### Time Estimate
- Chatbot interaction (customer): 1–3 min per session
- Human escalation (agent): 5–15 min per session
- Knowledge base maintenance: 4–6 hours/week
- AI model monitoring: 8–12 hours/week
- **Total system operating effort**: ~20–25 hours/week (Product Content Manager + IT AI/ML Engineer)

## W1045. Store-Level Customer PVC Pipe Cutting, Jointing & Fabrication Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases PVC pipe (or brings project requirements) and requests custom-length cutting or jointing/fabrication |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,000 requests/month across all stores (plumbing is 12% of SKUs, ~4,200 items; PVC pipe is the highest-volume plumbing SKU) |
| **Volume** | 1–10 cuts per customer visit; average 3–5 cuts |
| **Owner** | Plumbing Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Plumbing Section), Customer |

### Background

PVC (polyvinyl chloride) pipe is the dominant plumbing material in Philippine construction, used for water supply lines (PVC Schedule 40/80), drainage (PVC DWV), and electrical conduit. BuildRight Depot sells PVC pipe in standard lengths (3m and 6m) across diameters from ½" to 6". Filipino DIY homeowners and small contractors frequently need custom-length PVC pipe for tight installations (under-sink drainage, toilet rough-in, shower lines) but lack the cutting tools (PVC cutter, hacksaw with fine-tooth blade, miter box) for clean, square cuts. Improperly cut PVC pipe has burrs that prevent solvent-weld joints from sealing, leading to leaks. This in-store cutting service provides: (a) clean, square cuts to customer-specified lengths; (b) solvent-weld joining of fittings to cut lengths (optional); (c) pipe threading for galvanized iron (GI) pipe (distinct from PVC). This is distinct from W944 (pipe threading, cutting & fabrication for metal pipe) which covers steel/GI pipe; PVC pipe requires different cutting tools and solvent cement joining. This service reduces customer frustration, increases average transaction value (customers buy full-length pipes and fittings instead of settling for pre-cut sections at higher per-meter cost), and differentiates BuildRight from competing hardware stores.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Requirement Intake**: Sales Associate gathers project requirements: (a) pipe material — PVC (Schedule 40, Schedule 80, DWV, conduit), CPVC (hot water), or PPR; (b) pipe diameter — ½", ¾", 1", 1¼", 1½", 2", 3", 4", 6"; (c) number of pieces and lengths needed (in centimeters or inches); (d) jointing requirements — solvent-weld fittings (elbow, tee, coupling, reducer, adapter) to be pre-joined; (e) intended application — water supply, drainage, electrical conduit, irrigation, other; (f) system validates that requested lengths are achievable from standard 3m/6m stock, minimizes waste (nesting algorithm for multiple cuts) | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Cutting Execution**: Sales Associate performs PVC cutting at dedicated cutting station in plumbing section: (a) select correct pipe from stock per diameter and schedule; (b) measure and mark cut points using tape measure and marker; (c) position pipe in cutting jig (or miter box for smaller diameters); (d) execute cut using dedicated PVC cutter (for diameters ≤2") or fine-tooth hacksaw with miter box (for diameters >2"); (e) deburr cut ends using chamfering tool or utility knife — remove internal and external burrs to ensure proper solvent-weld seating; (f) verify cut length within ±2mm tolerance using tape measure; (g) wipe cut ends clean of dust and shavings | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 3–8 min (depending on number of cuts) |
| 3 | **Optional Jointing / Fabrication**: If customer requests pre-joined assemblies: (a) dry-fit all components to verify alignment before solvent cement application; (b) apply PVC primer (purple primer, mandatory for Schedule 40/80) to outside of pipe end and inside of fitting socket; (c) apply PVC solvent cement to primed surfaces; (d) insert pipe into fitting with quarter-turn twist; (e) hold joint for 15–30 seconds for initial set; (f) wipe excess cement; (g) mark joint set time: 15 min for handling, 2 hours for light pressure, 24 hours for full pressure; (h) customer takes pre-joined assembly with remaining uncut pipe lengths | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 5–15 min (depending on number of joints) |
| 4 | **Transaction Processing & Waste Handling**: (a) system charges for: full-length pipe(s) consumed (prorated by meter), fittings used, primer/cement consumed (if jointing), cutting service fee (or waived for purchases >PHP 500); (b) cutting waste (offcuts <30 cm) collected in store PVC recycling bin per W502 (non-hazardous waste management); (c) offcuts ≥30 cm offered to customer for potential use; (d) system updates inventory: full-length pipe decremented, cutting waste accounted per disposition code; (e) customer receives cut pieces, any pre-joined assemblies, and remaining pipe | Sales Associate / POS Cashier | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- PVC cutting service module — cut list entry, nesting optimization, waste calculation
- Product master (W252) — pipe dimensions, cutting service SKU, fitting compatibility
- Inventory deduction — full-length pipe consumed, waste disposition (W502)
- POS service item processing (W544) — service fee SKU
- Real-time inventory availability (W533)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Cut accuracy**: PVC cutter blade wear leads to angled cuts; weekly blade replacement schedule required for high-volume stores
- **Solvent cement safety**: PVC primer and cement contain tetrahydrofuran (THF) and cyclohexanone — volatile, flammable chemicals; cutting station must be in well-ventilated area; PPE (gloves, safety glasses) required per W172
- **Customer mismeasurement**: Customers frequently provide incorrect measurements; recommend dry-fit verification before solvent cement application
- **Waste management**: PVC offcuts cannot be recycled through standard channels; partner with specialized PVC recyclers per W167


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "validates that requested lengths are achievable from standard 3m/6m stock,…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-schedule of "schedule" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-request of "requests pre-joined assemblies" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: for high-volume stores; operational: per W172

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Plumbing Section)**: Cutting adds 10–20 min per customer; at ~0.3–0.5 requests per store per day (~2,000–3,000/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this represents ~6–10 min/day; absorbed by existing department staff with rotating cutting duty
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Requirement intake: 3–5 min
- Cutting execution: 3–8 min
- Jointing (if applicable): 5–15 min
- Transaction & waste handling: 3–5 min
- **Total per customer**: 14–33 min (average ~20 min)

## W1046. Customer Door & Window Measurement, Sizing & Custom Order Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests door or window sizing assistance, custom-order door/window, or replacement for existing unit |
| **Frequency** | ~2,500–3,500 customer interactions/month across all stores |
| **Volume** | 1–4 door/window units per customer project |
| **Owner** | Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Section), Special Order Coordinator, Customer |

### Background

Doors and windows are high-value, project-critical items in Philippine home construction and renovation. A single-panel flush door costs PHP 1,500–4,000; a panel door PHP 3,000–8,000; a sliding glass window PHP 2,500–15,000; and a main entrance door with frame PHP 8,000–25,000. Unlike commodity items (cement, nails), doors and windows are dimension-critical — a 10mm error in rough opening measurement results in an unusable unit. Philippine residential construction uses non-standardized rough openings (unlike US/Europe where modular sizes are common), making custom sizing the norm rather than the exception. Key door/window types in the Philippine market: (a) flush doors (standard sizes: 0.70m × 2.03m, 0.80m × 2.03m); (b) panel doors (decorative, same standard widths); (c) sliding glass windows (jalousie/awful windows being replaced by modern aluminum sliding); (d) swing casement windows; (e) main entrance doors (solid hardwood, customized); (f) fire-rated doors (for commercial buildings, per BFP requirement). BuildRight Depot carries standard-size doors and windows as stock items, but ~40% of door/window sales involve custom orders from partner manufacturers with 7–14 day lead times. This workflow covers the measurement consultation, sizing recommendation, and custom-order placement process.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Requirement Intake**: Sales Associate identifies the door/window need: (a) new construction (rough opening not yet built — recommend standard sizes); (b) replacement (existing unit to be replaced — requires precise measurement of existing frame and rough opening); (c) renovation/upgrade (existing opening to receive new unit — requires measurement of finished opening); (d) door type — interior flush, interior panel, main entrance (solid wood, metal-clad, fiberglass), fire-rated (1-hour, 2-hour), bi-fold, sliding; (e) window type — sliding aluminum, casement, fixed, awning, jalousie (replacement blades only); (f) material preference — wood (narra, tanguile, molave), aluminum, steel, fiberglass, PVC/uPVC; (g) finish — primed, pre-finished, raw (for on-site finishing) | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Measurement Guidance & Data Collection**: Sales Associate provides measurement guidance (if customer has not yet measured) or records customer-provided measurements: (a) for door replacement: (i) existing door slab width and height; (ii) frame interior width and height (jamb-to-jamb); (iii) rough opening width and height (framing to framing, behind the jambs); (iv) wall thickness (for jamb depth); (v) hinge side (left-hand or right-hand swing); (vi) lock bore hole location; (b) for window replacement: (i) glass/viewable area width and height; (ii) frame exterior width and height; (iii) rough opening width and height; (iv) wall thickness; (v) sill height from floor; (vi) number of panels/slides; (c) system records all measurements with unit (mm or inches) and provides a printable measurement worksheet; (d) if customer cannot provide measurements, system generates on-site measurement service request per W442 (site technical survey & measurement services) with service fee | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Sizing Recommendation & Availability Check**: (a) system compares customer measurements against standard size catalog — if within 5mm tolerance of a standard size, recommend stock item for immediate purchase; (b) if no standard size matches, system flags for custom order; (c) system checks real-time inventory (W533) for matching stock items at customer's store and nearby stores within 50 km per W214 (inter-store transfer); (d) for custom orders: system generates custom specification with manufacturer — door/window type, dimensions (width × height × thickness/frame depth), material, finish, glass type (clear, tinted, frosted, tempered, laminated), hardware (lockset, hinges, handles), fire rating (if applicable); (e) system estimates custom order lead time: 7–10 days for doors, 10–14 days for windows, 14–21 days for fire-rated doors | System / Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Custom Order Placement & Deposit Collection**: (a) for custom orders: Sales Associate creates special order in system per W545 (special order & customer order processing); (b) system generates custom order quote with: specification details, manufacturer, estimated lead time, total price, 50% deposit requirement; (c) customer reviews and approves quote; (d) system collects 50% deposit at POS per W546 (deposit & progress payment collection); (e) special order transmitted to partner manufacturer via vendor portal (W866); (f) system sets expected delivery date and sends confirmation to customer via SMS/email; (g) for stock items at nearby stores: system initiates inter-store transfer per W214 with 2–3 day delivery | Sales Associate / Special Order Coordinator | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 5 | **Delivery Notification & Customer Pickup/Delivery**: (a) when custom order arrives at store (7–21 days): receiving clerk processes goods receipt per W109; (b) system sends automated notification to customer via SMS/email: "Your custom door/window order #XXX has arrived at BuildRight [Store Name]. Please pick up within 5 days or call to schedule home delivery per W5D"; (c) customer picks up at customer service counter — verify order against receipt and customer ID; (d) remaining 50% balance collected at POS; (e) for bulky items: offer home delivery scheduling per W5D with delivery fee; (f) system closes special order and updates vendor performance metrics per W44 | Receiving Clerk / Customer Service Rep / Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Door/window sizing engine — measurement-to-specification conversion
- Standard size catalog lookup against product master (W252)
- Real-time inventory availability (W533) with inter-store transfer (W214)
- Special order module (W545) — custom specification, vendor order
- Deposit collection at POS (W546) — 50% upfront, 50% on delivery
- Vendor portal (W866) — custom order transmission to manufacturer
- Customer notification engine — SMS/email delivery alerts
- Home delivery scheduling (W5D) — bulky item delivery

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Measurement error liability**: Incorrect measurements result in unusable custom products that cannot be restocked; system must display measurement verification disclaimer and recommend on-site measurement service (W442) for high-value orders (>PHP 10,000)
- **Long custom order lead times**: 7–21 day lead times may lose customer to competitors with standard sizes; offer standard-size modifications (planing/shaving) for simple size adjustments as an in-store service
- **Damage during transport**: Large doors and windows are susceptible to damage during delivery; require vertical transport packaging and delivery partner compliance per W268


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-measure of "measurement of existing frame and rough opening" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "printable measurement worksheet" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-log of "catalog — if within 5mm tolerance of a standard size, recommend stock item for…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Building Materials)**: Door/window consultation adds 15–25 min per customer; at ~3–4 consultations per store per week (~2,500–3,500/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this represents ~1–1.7 hours/week
- **Special Order Coordinator**: Manages ~50–80 active custom door/window orders at any time across the store's order book; estimated 2–3 hours/day of order tracking and vendor follow-up
- **No permanent incremental headcount**; absorbed by existing building materials team

### Time Estimate
- Requirement intake: 5–8 min
- Measurement guidance: 5–10 min
- Sizing recommendation & availability: 3–5 min
- Order placement & deposit: 5–8 min
- Delivery notification & handoff: 5–10 min
- **Total per customer**: 23–41 min (average ~30 min)

## W1054. Store-Level Customer Wire & Cable Cut-to-Length Spool Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests specific length of electrical wire or cable (shorter than full roll) |
| **Frequency** | ~6,000–8,000 cut-to-length requests/month across all stores (electrical category: 10% of SKUs, ~3,500 items; wire/cable is the highest-volume electrical SKU by linear meters sold) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 cuts per customer visit |
| **Owner** | Electrical Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Electrical Section), Customer |

### Background

Electrical wire and cable are sold in standard rolls (100m for THHN/THWN building wire, 50m for larger gauges, 305m/1000ft boxes for NM-B romex equivalent) at BuildRight Depot. Filipino electricians and DIY homeowners frequently need specific shorter lengths: (a) 3m of 12 AWG wire for a single outlet installation; (b) 10m of 10 AWG wire for an aircon circuit; (c) 5m of 14 AWG wire for a lighting circuit; (d) 2m of #8 AWG wire for a grounding electrode conductor. Purchasing a full 100m roll for a 5m need wastes ~PHP 1,500–3,000 (customer pays for 95m of unused wire). Conversely, some customers purchase pre-cut lengths from smaller hardware stores at inflated per-meter prices (often 2–3× the per-meter price of a full roll). BuildRight's cut-to-length service offers the optimal middle ground: customers purchase exactly the length they need at a per-meter price that is competitive with full-roll per-meter rates plus a small cutting service fee. This service: (a) reduces customer purchase barriers for small projects; (b) reduces wire waste; (c) increases foot traffic from small-project customers who would otherwise go to neighborhood hardware stores (sari-sari stores with limited wire selection). Wire types commonly requested: THHN/THWN solid and stranded (14 AWG, 12 AWG, 10 AWG, 8 AWG, 6 AWG), flat cord (2-wire, 3-wire), extension cord wire, speaker wire, coaxial cable, CAT6 LAN cable, and automotive wire. This is distinct from W463 (catch-weight & cut-to-length processing) which covers the general inventory management of variable-length items; this workflow is the customer-facing service process for wire cutting specifically.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Requirement Intake**: Sales Associate gathers wire/cable specifications: (a) wire type — THHN/THWN building wire, flat cord, extension cord wire, speaker wire, coaxial (RG-6/RG-59), CAT6 LAN cable, automotive wire, welding cable; (b) wire gauge/size — AWG size (14, 12, 10, 8, 6) or mm² (2.0mm², 3.5mm², 5.5mm², 8.0mm², 14mm², 22mm²); (c) conductor type — solid or stranded; (d) insulation color (for building wire: black, white, green/bare, red, blue, yellow per standard color coding); (e) required length in meters; (f) number of conductors needed (e.g., for a single-phase circuit: 1 black + 1 white + 1 green = 3 conductors, each × circuit length); (g) application — outlet wiring, lighting circuit, aircon circuit, subpanel feeder, grounding, data/communications | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Inventory Check & Pricing**: (a) system checks real-time inventory (W533) for the requested wire type/gauge/color in full-roll stock; (b) if full roll available: system calculates cut-to-length price = (per-meter price from full roll) × length requested + cutting service fee (PHP 10–20 per cut, waived for purchases >PHP 300); (c) system displays per-meter price and total cost for customer confirmation; (d) if insufficient remaining length on open roll, system checks for additional rolls in stock or at nearby stores per W214; (e) system validates remaining length on open spool — if customer request would leave <5m on the spool, offer the remaining full length at a discount to avoid small unusable offcuts | System / Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 1–2 min |
| 3 | **Cutting Execution**: Sales Associate performs wire cutting at dedicated electrical cutting station: (a) select correct wire roll from spool rack (verified by type, gauge, color, and lot/batch number per W3 inventory procedures); (b) measure required length using calibrated measuring tape or the cutting station's built-in meter counter; (c) mark cut point; (d) cut wire cleanly using wire cutter/cable snips (for wire ≤10 AWG) or cable cutter (for wire ≥8 AWG); (e) for cuts >20m: unroll from spool rack along the floor measuring path (most stores have a 10m measuring lane marked on the floor near the electrical section); (f) verify cut length within ±0.1m tolerance; (g) strip 10mm of insulation from both ends to verify conductor quality (no oxidation, correct stranding); (h) coil cut length neatly and secure with wire tie or tape; (i) label with wire type, gauge, length, and per-meter price on tag; (j) system decrements inventory from spool roll (partial unit deduction per catch-weight logic W463) | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–10 min (depending on length; longer lengths require floor unrolling) |
| 4 | **Transaction Processing**: (a) system generates line item for cut wire: SKU, type, gauge, color, length (m), per-meter price, total price, cutting service fee (if applicable); (b) customer pays at POS per W5B (in-store selling) or W542 (quotation conversion); (c) system updates spool remaining length in inventory; (d) if spool remaining length reaches zero, system auto-generates replenishment suggestion per W2A (auto-replenishment) | POS Cashier / System | Electrical Department Supervisor | 2–3 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Wire/cable product master (W252) — wire specifications, per-meter pricing
- Real-time inventory (W533) — spool remaining length tracking
- Catch-weight/partial unit inventory management (W463) — partial roll deduction
- POS service item processing (W544) — cutting service fee
- Auto-replenishment (W2A) — spool reorder trigger at minimum threshold

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Spool remaining length accuracy**: Manual measurement errors accumulate; weekly spool reconciliation (physical measurement of remaining length) required to maintain inventory accuracy per INV-001
- **Customer length disputes**: Customer claims cut length is shorter than requested; mitigate with length verification at cutting station before handoff and printed length on price tag
- **Wire tangle during long cuts**: Unrolling >20m of wire from spool can cause tangling and kinking; require two-person operation for cuts >30m (one unrolls, one manages slack)
- **Safety — cutting live wire risk**: Ensure all wire is de-energized and not connected to any power source; post safety notice at cutting station; mitigated by CTL-266 (controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1))


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "Check & Pricing" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-log of "logic W463" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-generate of "generates line item for cut wire" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: to maintain inventory accuracy per INV-001

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Electrical Section)**: Each cut-to-length request takes 5–15 min; at ~1–1.3 requests per store per day (~6,000–8,000/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this represents ~10–20 min/day; absorbed by existing electrical section staff (typically 2–3 Sales Associates assigned to electrical at any time)
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Requirement intake: 3–5 min
- Inventory check & pricing: 1–2 min
- Cutting execution: 3–10 min
- Transaction processing: 2–3 min
- **Total per customer**: 9–20 min (average ~12 min)

## W1059. Customer Kitchen Countertop Measurement & Custom Fabrication Order Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests custom kitchen countertop or bathroom vanity top measurement and fabrication |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 orders/month across all stores (home décor & furniture: 5% of SKUs; countertops are high-value items: PHP 3,000–30,000 per project) |
| **Volume** | 1 countertop per order (may include multiple sections for L-shaped or U-shaped kitchens) |
| **Owner** | Assistant Store Manager (special-order services) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Home Décor Section), Special Order Coordinator, Fabrication Partner, Customer |

### Background

Kitchen countertops and bathroom vanity tops are among the highest-value individual items in home improvement, with Filipino homeowners investing PHP 3,000–30,000 per project depending on material and size. BuildRight Depot offers countertop materials including: (a) prefabricated granite slabs (standard sizes: 600mm × 2.0m, 600mm × 2.4m, 600mm × 3.0m) — PHP 2,000–5,000 per piece; (b) prefabricated quartz/engineered stone — PHP 4,000–10,000 per piece; (c) laminate countertop boards (post-formed, standard 600mm × 2.4m) — PHP 1,500–4,000 per piece; (d) solid surface (acrylic) — PHP 5,000–15,000 per piece; (e) tile-on-concrete (DIY — customer buys tiles per W963 and cement board). Philippine residential kitchen layouts are predominantly: (a) straight/linear (1.2m–2.4m); (b) L-shaped (2 legs, each 1.2m–2.4m); (c) U-shaped (3 sides); (d) galley (2 parallel runs). Standard counter depth is 600mm (24 inches). Unlike flat boards that can be cut on-site, granite and quartz countertops require professional fabrication (cutting, edge profiling, sink cutout, polishing) using specialized wet-saw and CNC equipment. BuildRight coordinates fabrication through partnered stone fabricators (not in-store capability). This workflow covers measurement, specification, custom order placement, and delivery/installation coordination. It is distinct from W1046 (door & window custom orders) which covers different product categories; this workflow addresses countertop-specific measurement and fabrication requirements.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Kitchen/Bathroom Layout Assessment**: Sales Associate gathers project details: (a) room type — kitchen countertop, bathroom vanity top, laundry countertop, bar/counter; (b) layout shape — straight, L-shaped, U-shaped, galley, custom; (c) existing or planned cabinet dimensions — cabinet length along wall for each section; (d) counter depth — standard 600mm or custom; (e) edge profile preference — flat/eased, beveled, bullnose, half-bullnose, ogee; (f) sink type — undermount (requires sink cutout in slab), drop-in (self-rimming, no slab cutout), farmhouse/apron front (large front cutout); (g) faucet holes — number and location (1-hole, 3-hole, 4-inch spread, 8-inch spread); (h) backsplash — matching material splash (100mm or 150mm height), tile backsplash (separate per W963), or none; (i) material preference and budget | Sales Associate | Home Décor & Furniture Dept Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Measurement Verification & Template Requirement**: (a) if customer provides measurements: system validates dimensions for reasonableness (typical Philippine kitchen counter lengths: 1.2m–3.6m per section; standard depth: 600mm; typical vanity: 0.6m–1.2m × 0.5m depth); (b) if customer does not have measurements: system offers two options: (i) customer takes measurements at home using provided measurement worksheet (printable from system — wall-to-wall length, depth, corner angles, sink opening dimensions, cutouts for stove/range); (ii) on-site measurement service per W442 (site technical survey & measurement services) with service fee (PHP 500–1,000, waived for orders >PHP 10,000); (c) for natural stone (granite, marble): system recommends on-site templating by fabricator before cutting — natural stone slabs vary in pattern and color, and site conditions may require field adjustments; (d) system creates custom specification record with all dimensions and preferences | Sales Associate / Special Order Coordinator | Home Décor & Furniture Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 3 | **Material Selection, Pricing & Order Placement**: (a) system displays material options within customer's budget: granite (PHP 3,000–8,000 per linear meter installed), quartz (PHP 5,000–15,000 per linear meter installed), laminate (PHP 1,500–4,000 per linear meter, DIY install), solid surface (PHP 6,000–20,000 per linear meter installed); (b) system calculates total cost: (i) material cost = length × per-linear-meter rate (including waste factor: 10% for straight layouts, 15–20% for L/U-shaped to account for seaming and waste at corners); (ii) fabrication cost = edge profiling (per linear meter) + sink cutout (per cutout) + faucet holes (per hole) + seaming (per joint, if multiple pieces needed); (iii) installation cost = per-linear-meter installation rate (if professional install requested per W138); (iv) delivery cost = bulky item delivery fee per W5D; (c) system generates custom order quote with 50% deposit requirement per W546; (d) customer approves quote and pays deposit at POS; (e) system transmits order to fabrication partner per W866 (vendor portal — custom order); (f) estimated lead time: laminate (3–5 days), granite/quartz fabrication (7–14 days), solid surface (10–21 days) | Sales Associate / System | Home Décor & Furniture Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Fabrication, Delivery & Installation Coordination**: (a) fabrication partner manufactures countertop per specification; (b) if natural stone: fabricator performs on-site templating before cutting (arranges directly with customer); (c) upon fabrication completion, fabricator notifies BuildRight with ready date and weight/dimensions; (d) system sends delivery scheduling notification to customer; (e) customer selects delivery date; (f) for granite/quartz (heavy, fragile): system requires professional delivery per partnered logistics provider per W268 (last-mile delivery tracking); (g) if professional installation requested: system coordinates installation appointment per W138 (home installation services) — installer delivers and installs in one visit; (h) remaining 50% balance collected upon delivery/installation completion; (i) system closes custom order and logs vendor fabrication lead time for vendor scorecard per W44 | Special Order Coordinator / Fabrication Partner / Delivery Coordinator | Home Décor & Furniture Dept Supervisor | 10–15 min (coordination per order) |

### System Touchpoints
- Countertop specification module — layout input, material selection, pricing
- Custom order module (W545) — fabrication specification, vendor order
- Deposit collection (W546) — 50% upfront, 50% on completion
- Vendor portal (W866) — custom order transmission to fabricator
- Site measurement service (W442) — on-site measurement coordination
- Home installation service (W138) — installation booking
- Home delivery scheduling (W5D) — bulky item delivery
- Last-mile delivery tracking (W268) — professional delivery coordination
- Vendor scorecard (W44) — fabrication lead time and quality tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Measurement error liability**: Incorrect measurements result in countertops that don't fit — granite/quartz cuts cannot be easily modified; on-site templating by fabricator (step 4b) mitigates this for natural stone; for laminate (cut on-site), risk is lower
- **Color variation in natural stone**: Granite slab samples in-store may not match the actual slab used for fabrication; recommend customer visits fabricator's warehouse to select specific slab before cutting
- **Fragility during transport**: Granite and quartz countertops are brittle and crack if transported incorrectly (must be transported vertically, never flat); delivery partner must comply with handling standards
- **Long lead times**: 7–21 days for fabrication may lose customer to competitors who stock prefabricated standard sizes; offer prefabricated granite options (600mm × 2.0m with pre-finished edges) for immediate purchase to serve customers who cannot wait


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "countertop, bathroom vanity top, laundry countertop, bar/counter" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "printable from system — wall-to-wall length, depth, corner angles, sink opening…" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates total cost" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Assistant Store Manager (special-order services) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Home Décor)**: Consultation adds 15–25 min per customer; at ~2–3 consultations per store per week (~1,500–2,500/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this represents ~0.5–1.2 hours/week
- **Special Order Coordinator**: Manages ~20–40 active countertop orders at any time; estimated 1–2 hours/day of coordination; shared with other custom order categories (doors/windows per W1046)
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Layout assessment: 5–10 min
- Measurement verification: 5–8 min
- Material selection & order placement: 5–8 min
- Fabrication/delivery coordination: 10–15 min (per order, spread over days)
- **Sales Associate time per order**: 15–26 min (average ~20 min)

## W1062. Store-Level Customer Scaffolding Rental, Safety Harness Package & Delivery Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests scaffolding rental for construction, painting, or maintenance project at height |
| **Frequency** | ~800–1,200 rental requests/month across all stores (driven by Philippine construction activity and homeowner renovation projects) |
| **Volume** | 1 rental order per customer project; average 1–3 frame sets per order |
| **Owner** | Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Section), Tool Rental Coordinator, Customer |

### Background

Working at height is a daily reality in Philippine construction — from painting a 3-meter ceiling to building a 2-story house exterior to installing gutters on a roof. Professional scaffolding provides a safe, stable working platform compared to the common Filipino practice of using bamboo scaffolding (which lacks standard load ratings) or improvised platforms (ladders with planks). BuildRight Depot offers scaffolding rental as a value-added service that: (a) drives foot traffic and incremental merchandise sales (customers rent scaffolding + buy paint, tools, materials in the same visit); (b) generates recurring rental revenue (PHP 150–500 per frame set per day, or PHP 800–2,500 per week); (c) promotes workplace safety (DOLE DO 13 — Construction Safety Standards — requires proper scaffolding for work above 3 meters). Rental inventory typically includes: (a) steel frame scaffolding sets (standard frame: 1.7m × 1.2m, cross braces, adjustable base jacks, coupling pins); (b) aluminum scaffolding towers (lightweight, mobile with casters — for indoor painting and ceiling work); (c) ladder jacks and walk planks (for simpler 1-person jobs); (d) safety harnesses and lanyards (rented as a package with scaffolding for elevated work per W172 PPE requirements); (e) hard hats (rented or sold). This is distinct from W139 (tool & equipment rental) which covers general tool rental; this workflow addresses scaffolding-specific rental processes including safety documentation, deposit management, and height-safety compliance.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Height & Scope Assessment**: Sales Associate gathers project requirements: (a) working height — highest point where worker will stand (in meters); (b) project type — interior painting, exterior painting, gutter installation, ceiling installation, wall construction, roof work, general maintenance; (c) working platform needed — number of workers on scaffold simultaneously (typical: 1–2); (d) load requirement — light duty (painting, inspection — 120 kg/m²), medium duty (general construction — 240 kg/m²); (e) site surface — concrete/flat (stable base), soil/unpaved (requires base plates and mudsills), uneven/stepped (requires adjustable jacks); (f) indoor or outdoor (indoor: aluminum mobile tower preferred; outdoor: steel frame with base jacks); (g) rental duration — days or weeks; (h) delivery needed or customer pickup (scaffold frames are bulky — steel frame set ~25 kg per frame; 3-set tower ~100 kg; requires pickup truck or delivery per W5D) | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Scaffolding Configuration & Safety Package**: System recommends scaffolding configuration: (a) **Number of frame tiers** = working height ÷ frame height (1.7m per tier, minimum 1 tier below working platform); e.g., 4m working height = 3 tiers (1.7 + 1.7 + 1.7 = 5.1m platform height minus safety margin); (b) **Frame sets**: 2 frames per tier × number of tiers; (c) **Cross braces**: 2 per tier × number of tiers; (d) **Base jacks**: 2 (adjustable, for leveling); (e) **Coupling pins**: 2 per tier connection; (f) **Walk planks**: 1–2 per platform level (steel or wood, rated for load); (g) **Guardrails**: top rail (1.0m above platform) + mid-rail (0.5m above platform) per DOLE DO 13 safety standard; (h) **Toe boards**: 150mm minimum height at platform edge per DOLE DO 13; (i) **Safety harness package** (mandatory for work >3m per DOLE): full-body harness + shock-absorbing lanyard + anchor point; (j) **Hard hat**: mandatory per DOLE DO 13 for all construction work; (k) system checks rental inventory availability per store per W533 | System / Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Rental Agreement, Deposit & Safety Waiver**: (a) system generates rental agreement with: rental item list (each frame, brace, jack, plank — serialized with asset tags), daily/weekly rental rate per item, rental duration, total rental cost, replacement cost per item (for damage/loss), deposit amount (typically 50% of replacement cost or PHP 5,000–20,000 depending on configuration); (b) customer signs safety acknowledgment per DOLE DO 13: (i) scaffold must be erected per manufacturer instructions and DOLE standards; (ii) maximum load rating must not be exceeded; (iii) safety harness must be worn and properly anchored at all times when working >3m; (iv) scaffold must be inspected before each use; (v) BuildRight Depot is not liable for injury resulting from improper scaffold erection, overloading, or failure to use safety equipment; (c) system collects deposit at POS per W546 (deposit & progress payment collection) — held as customer deposit per W94; (d) system records rental agreement with customer ID, rental start date, expected return date | Sales Associate / POS Cashier | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Delivery (If Requested) & Customer Handoff**: (a) if delivery requested: system schedules delivery per W5D (home delivery scheduling) with bulky item delivery fee; (b) delivery team loads scaffold components and safety equipment; (c) upon delivery, customer or customer's representative signs delivery receipt acknowledging receipt of all items per serialized list; (d) if customer pickup: Sales Associate stages rental items at store loading area per W950 (vehicle loading assistance); (e) customer receives: rental agreement copy, safety instruction sheet, scaffold assembly diagram (manufacturer's instructions), and emergency contact number | Sales Associate / Delivery Coordinator | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Return, Inspection & Deposit Refund**: (a) customer returns scaffold components on or before rental end date; (b) Sales Associate inspects each serialized component: (i) frames: no bends, cracks, or weld damage; (ii) cross braces: no bends, locking pins functional; (iii) base jacks: threads functional, no corrosion; (iv) walk planks: no cracks, rot, or excessive wear; (v) safety harness: no cuts, fraying, or chemical damage; webbing intact; (vi) hard hat: no cracks, dents, or UV degradation; (c) system logs return condition per asset tag; (d) if all items returned in good condition: system releases full deposit refund per W101 (customer refund & credit processing); (e) if items damaged or missing: system deducts replacement cost from deposit per rental agreement terms; (f) system calculates final rental charge: (actual rental days × daily rate) or (rental weeks × weekly rate, whichever is lower); (g) system settles: deposit minus (rental charge + any damage deductions); (h) system updates rental asset inventory per W399 and rental equipment maintenance schedule per W650 (warehouse equipment preventive maintenance adapted for rental assets) | Sales Associate / Tool Rental Coordinator | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 10–15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Scaffolding rental module — configuration engine, inventory tracking, rental agreement
- Product/rental asset master (W252/W399) — serialized rental inventory, replacement costs
- Real-time inventory (W533) — rental availability
- Safety acknowledgment module — DOLE DO 13 compliance documentation
- Customer deposit management (W94) — rental deposit collection and refund
- POS processing (W5B) — rental agreement and deposit transaction
- Home delivery scheduling (W5D) — bulky item delivery
- Vehicle loading assistance (W950) — customer pickup
- Customer refund processing (W101) — deposit refund
- Preventive maintenance scheduling (W650) — rental asset maintenance

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Safety liability**: Scaffolding accidents can cause serious injury or death; rental agreement must include robust liability waiver and safety acknowledgment; all safety instructions must be provided in writing and verbally explained at handoff; per W285 (public liability claims management)
- **Deposit disputes**: Customers dispute damage deductions from deposits; mitigate with pre-rental condition documentation (photos of each serialized component at handoff) and post-return condition photos
- **Non-return / theft**: Customer fails to return rental equipment; deposit may not cover full replacement cost; implement ID verification (government-issued ID photocopy) at rental agreement signing
- **Maintenance between rentals**: Scaffold components that are not inspected and maintained between rentals can fail; implement post-return inspection per step 5 and scheduled maintenance per W650


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-inspect of "inspection — 120 kg/m²" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "checks rental inventory availability per store per W533" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-record of "records rental agreement with customer ID, rental start date, expected return…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Building Materials)**: Rental consultation adds 15–25 min per customer; at ~0.1–0.2 requests per store per day (~800–1,200/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this represents ~2–5 min/day
- **Tool Rental Coordinator**: Manages rental inventory, agreements, inspections, and returns; at 4–6 active rentals per store at any time, estimated 1–2 hours/day; shared with tool rental per W139
- **No permanent incremental headcount**; absorbed by existing building materials team

### Time Estimate
- Project assessment: 5–8 min
- Configuration: 2–3 min (automated)
- Rental agreement & deposit: 5–8 min
- Delivery/handoff: 5–10 min
- Return & inspection: 10–15 min
- **Total Sales Associate time per rental**: 27–44 min (average ~35 min, split across rental start and end)

## W1063. Store-Level Customer Welding Gas Cylinder Exchange & Refill Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents empty or near-empty welding gas cylinder (acetylene, argon, CO₂, oxygen, or mixed gas) for exchange or refill at store |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–5,000 cylinder transactions/month across all stores (welding & metal fabrication services are a high-value B2B segment) |
| **Volume** | 1–4 cylinders per customer visit |
| **Owner** | Tools & Hardware Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Tools/Hardware Section), Receiving Clerk, Customer |

### Background

Welding gas cylinder management is a specialized retail operation critical for BuildRight's professional contractor and metal fabricator customer base. The Philippines has a significant welding and metal fabrication industry — from small barangay-level shops to large construction contractors — and welding gas is a recurring consumable. Cylinders are typically supplier-owned (deposit-based) or customer-owned. The key gases sold through BuildRight are: (a) Acetylene (DA — Dissolved Acetylene) for oxy-acetylene cutting and welding; (b) Argon (Ar) for TIG/MIG welding of stainless steel and aluminum; (c) CO₂ and Argon/CO₂ mixes for MIG welding of mild steel; (d) Oxygen (O₂) for oxy-acetylene cutting; (e) Nitrogen (N₂) for pressure testing and pipe purging. Cylinders come in standard Philippine sizes: Size B (1 m³), Size C (2 m³), Size D (5 m³), and Size E (10 m³). The exchange model (return empty, receive full) is the dominant transaction pattern in Philippine retail because it avoids the customer needing to wait for on-site refilling. Safety is paramount — compressed gases are classified as hazardous materials per W236 (hazmat storage) and W698 (SDS management), and cylinders must be inspected for damage, valve integrity, and hydrostatic test date validity before acceptance for exchange.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Cylinder Inspection & Acceptance**: Sales Associate inspects the returned empty cylinder: (a) check cylinder body for dents, cracks, arc burns, or corrosion; (b) verify valve is intact and functional (no leaks detected by smell or sound); (c) check hydrostatic test date stamp — Philippine standards (DTI-BPS) require re-testing every 5 years; cylinders past test date must be rejected and customer directed to supplier for re-certification; (d) verify cylinder type matches gas type (color-coded per Philippine standard: acetylene = maroon, argon = dark green, CO₂ = gray, oxygen = black, nitrogen = black with white shoulder); (e) check for customer-owned vs. supplier-owned cylinder (identified by supplier stamp/label); (f) record cylinder serial number and condition in ERP per W92 (inventory adjustment for cylinder tracking) | Sales Associate | Tools & Hardware Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Cylinder Exchange Selection & Inventory Check**: System identifies available full cylinders: (a) system queries real-time cylinder inventory (W533) by gas type, size, and supplier; (b) matching: customer receives same gas type and same size as returned cylinder; (c) for supplier-owned cylinders: system verifies supplier name on returned cylinder matches available exchange pool from same supplier; (d) for customer-owned cylinders: system processes as a refill transaction with customer's own cylinder sent to refilling partner; (e) system displays gas type, cylinder size, fill pressure, and exchange/refill price; (f) if out of stock on specific size, system offers alternative nearest-size option or rain check per W772 | Sales Associate / System | Tools & Hardware Dept Supervisor | 2–3 min |
| 3 | **Companion Product Recommendation**: System recommends welding consumables based on gas type: (a) for acetylene/oxygen customers: welding torch tips, filler rods (E6013, E7018 electrodes), flux, cutting torch attachments, striker/lighter; (b) for argon customers: TIG filler rods (ER308L, ER70S-6), tungsten electrodes, gas lenses, collet bodies, cup nozzles; (c) for CO₂/mix customers: MIG wire (ER70S-6), contact tips, nozzle gel, drive rolls; (d) PPE: welding helmet/goggles (auto-darkening lens), leather gloves, leather apron, spats; (e) safety: fire extinguisher per W1051, welding blanket, flash-back arrestor check; (f) system bundles recommendation with exchange transaction | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Transaction Processing & Cylinder Handoff**: (a) system processes exchange transaction at POS with cylinder deposit tracking (if supplier-owned): deposit amount held on customer's account, credited on return, new deposit charged on exchange; (b) for trade account B2B customers: transaction charged to account with monthly billing per W8; (c) Sales Associate issues full cylinder to customer with safety briefing: "Keep cylinder upright, secure during transport, do not modify valve, store in well-ventilated area, keep away from ignition sources"; (d) system prints gas safety data sheet summary per W698 (SDS management); (e) for customer-owned refills: issue claim stub with estimated refill date (typically 1–2 business days via refill partner); (f) cylinder serial number of issued full cylinder recorded against customer transaction for traceability | Sales Associate | Tools & Hardware Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 5 | **Cylinder Inventory Reconciliation & Replenishment**: (a) system updates cylinder count: +1 empty, −1 full for exchange transactions; (b) when empty cylinder count reaches reorder threshold per store (typically 5+ empty cylinders of same gas/size), system triggers automatic refill requisition to gas supplier via W2 (purchase order cycle); (c) for supplier-owned pool cylinders, system generates weekly supplier reconciliation report matching cylinders received vs. cylinders dispatched per location; (d) monthly cylinder inventory audit per W6 (cycle counting) — cylinders tracked individually by serial number; (e) hazmat storage compliance check per W236: ensure cylinder storage area has adequate ventilation, is separated from flammable materials, and has appropriate fire suppression per W1051 | Receiving Clerk / System | Tools & Hardware Dept Supervisor | Daily reconciliation: 15 min; Monthly audit: 2–3 hrs |

### System Touchpoints
- Cylinder inventory tracking module (serialized cylinder tracking by gas type, size, supplier)
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- POS transaction module with deposit management (W5)
- Hazmat storage compliance module (W236)
- SDS management system (W698)
- Purchase order / supplier requisition system (W2)
- Cycle counting module (W6)
- Trade account / B2B billing module (W8)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Cylinder safety liability**: Damaged or expired cylinders pose explosion risk; staff must be trained to reject non-compliant cylinders per W237 (hazmat safety training); quarterly safety refresher required per W655
- **Supplier-owned cylinder reconciliation**: Cylinders from different suppliers (Linde/BOC, Air Liquide, Veritas, local refillers) must not be intermixed; supplier-owned cylinders returned to wrong supplier result in deposit disputes and penalty charges
- **Out-of-stock risk**: Welding gas is a just-in-time consumable for fabricators; stockouts drive customers to competitors; safety stock level must account for supplier delivery lead time (2–3 days for refills); mitigated by CTL-266 (controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1))
- **Regulatory compliance**: Philippine standards (DTI-BPS) require all compressed gas cylinders to have valid hydrostatic test certification; sale or exchange of expired cylinders is a regulatory violation


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-record of "record cylinder serial number and condition in ERP per W92" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "Cylinder Exchange Selection & Inventory Check" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System workflow notification of "contact tips, nozzle gel, drive rolls" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: per W655

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Tools/Hardware Section)**: Cylinder inspection and exchange adds 3–5 minutes per transaction; at ~4–6 exchanges per store per week (~3,000–5,000/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this represents ~15–30 min/week; absorbed by existing Sales Associates
- **Receiving Clerk**: Cylinder delivery receipt and storage compliance adds ~30 min per delivery (1–2 deliveries/week per store)
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Cylinder inspection & acceptance: 3–5 min
- Exchange selection & inventory check: 2–3 min
- Companion product recommendation: 1–2 min (automated)
- Transaction & handoff: 3–5 min
- **Total per exchange transaction**: 9–15 min
- Daily reconciliation: 15 min
- Monthly cylinder audit: 2–3 hrs

## W1077. Store-Level Customer Construction Site Portable Toilet & Temporary Facility Rental Coordination

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (typically contractor or homeowner managing a construction project) requests portable toilet rental or temporary facility setup for construction site |
| **Frequency** | ~1,000–1,500 requests/month across all stores (tied to active construction projects in BuildRight's trade account base of ~5,200 accounts) |
| **Volume** | 1 rental coordination per construction project |
| **Owner** | Pro Desk / Trade Counter Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Pro Desk), Third-Party Rental Partner, Customer |

### Background

Construction site facilities are a necessary part of any building project in the Philippines. DOLE Department Order No. 13 (DO 13) and the Philippine Occupational Safety and Health Standards require construction sites to provide adequate sanitation facilities for workers, including: (a) portable toilets (minimum 1 per 25 workers per DO 13); (b) handwashing stations; (c) drinking water supply; (d) temporary worker rest/shade areas; (e) first aid station. BuildRight's trade account customers (5,000+ contractors and builders) frequently request portable toilets and temporary facilities for their construction sites. While BuildRight does not own or operate portable toilet units, it serves as a coordination and referral point, connecting customers with vetted third-party rental partners. This service: (a) provides convenience to BuildRight's trade customers (one-stop-shop experience); (b) generates referral income or commission from rental partners; (c) strengthens trade customer loyalty and BuildRight's position as a project partner; (d) supports DOLE compliance per W789 (construction safety management). This workflow is distinct from W139 (tool rental) which covers BuildRight's own rental inventory; this workflow coordinates third-party rental services that BuildRight does not directly operate.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Site Requirement Assessment**: Sales Associate gathers site details: (a) construction site location (address, barangay, city/municipality); (b) project duration: expected construction period (weeks/months); (c) number of workers on site (determines number of portable toilets required: 1 per 25 workers per DO 13); (d) required facilities: portable toilet, handwashing station, temporary office/storage container, generator rental (if no power connection); (e) site access: is the site accessible by delivery truck? narrow street, gated community, high-rise construction?; (f) customer account: trade account number per W58 or new customer setup | Sales Associate | Pro Desk Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Third-Party Rental Partner Matching**: System identifies available rental partners: (a) system queries rental partner database by service area (city/municipality) and facility type; (b) partner selection criteria: service area coverage, unit availability, pricing, service frequency (how often is the unit cleaned/serviced), partner rating (based on previous BuildRight customer feedback); (c) system displays 2–3 partner options with: unit type, monthly rental rate, service frequency included (e.g., weekly cleaning), delivery/setup fee, pickup/removal fee, security deposit; (d) typical Philippine portable toilet rental: PHP 3,000–6,000/month per unit (includes weekly cleaning and chemical treatment); additional charges for delivery (PHP 1,500–3,000) and pickup (PHP 1,000–2,000) | System | Pro Desk Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Rental Coordination & Customer Handoff**: (a) customer selects preferred rental partner and confirms rental terms; (b) Sales Associate facilitates initial contact: provides customer's contact details and site address to rental partner, or provides rental partner's contact details to customer for direct booking; (c) BuildRight's role is limited to coordination/referral — the rental contract is directly between the customer and the rental partner; (d) system records referral in customer's project file per W162 (for trade accounts) for tracking and follow-up; (e) Sales Associate provides complementary construction site supplies reminder: PPE (hard hats, gloves, safety vests) per W1067 (fire suppression), W1068 (earthquake-resistant materials), W1069 (flood-resistant materials), DOLE DO 13 compliance requirements per W789; (f) for trade account customers, system generates site facility compliance checklist for DOLE inspection readiness per W505 | Sales Associate | Pro Desk Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Partner Performance Tracking & Follow-Up**: (a) system sends follow-up survey to customer 1 week after rental delivery: Was delivery on time? Is the unit in good condition? Is the partner responsive?; (b) customer feedback recorded in partner rating system; (c) quarterly partner performance review: on-time delivery rate, customer satisfaction score, issue resolution time; (d) underperforming partners removed from referral list; (e) BuildRight does NOT charge the customer for this coordination service — value is in customer loyalty and trade account stickiness | System | Pro Desk Supervisor | Automated follow-up; Quarterly review: 2–3 hrs |

### System Touchpoints
- Rental partner database (service area, pricing, availability, ratings)
- Customer project file integration (W162)
- Trade account management system (W58)
- DOLE compliance checklist generator (W789/W505)
- Customer feedback and survey system
- Partner performance tracking module

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Third-party liability**: BuildRight is not liable for the quality, timeliness, or condition of third-party rental services; coordination agreement must clearly state BuildRight's role as referral only
- **Partner availability**: During peak construction season (dry season, January–May), portable toilet rental demand may exceed partner supply; system should show real-time availability
- **Service quality**: Poor partner service (late delivery, dirty units, unresponsive customer service) reflects badly on BuildRight by association; rigorous partner vetting and performance tracking essential
- **DOLE compliance**: Customers may not be aware of DO 13 sanitation requirements; pro desk staff should proactively inform customers of requirements when discussing construction project materials


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "Site Requirement Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-match of "Third-Party Rental Partner Matching" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-record of "records referral in customer's project file per W162" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: should show real-time availability

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Pro Desk)**: Rental coordination adds 3–5 minutes per request; at ~1–2 requests per store per week (~1,000–1,500/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this represents ~5–10 min/week; absorbed by existing Pro Desk staff
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Site assessment: 3–5 min
- Partner matching: 2–3 min (automated)
- Coordination & handoff: 3–5 min
- **Total per coordination**: 8–13 min
