## W1086. Customer Construction Waste Disposal & Skip/Dumpster Rental Coordination

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | B2B contractor or B2C renovation customer requests construction waste disposal solution, or BuildRight proactively offers waste disposal coordination as a value-added service during large project quotation (W162) |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 coordination requests/month across all stores (construction waste disposal is a high-need service for BuildRight's B2B contractor base of ~5,200 trade accounts) |
| **Volume** | 1 skip/dumpster rental coordination per project |
| **Owner** | Trade Counter / Pro Desk Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Trade Counter), Partner Waste Hauler, Customer |

### Background

Construction waste disposal is a significant pain point for Filipino contractors and homeowners undertaking renovation projects. Philippine construction waste — concrete rubble, hollow block debris, rebar cut-offs, tiles and ceramic fragments, lumber scraps, paint cans, PVC pipe offcuts, wire scraps, packaging materials (cement bags, tile boxes), and general renovation debris — must be disposed of at LGU-approved disposal sites. Unauthorized dumping is illegal under DENR regulations (RA 9003 — Ecological Solid Waste Management Act) and carries significant penalties. However, the Philippine waste management infrastructure is fragmented: (a) most LGUs do not provide construction waste collection; (b) private waste haulers operate independently with varying reliability and pricing; (c) contractors typically hire dump trucks on an ad-hoc basis, which is expensive for small-to-medium jobs and risks illegal dumping by unscrupulous haulers; (d) homeowners doing DIY renovations have no easy way to arrange construction waste removal. BuildRight's position as the primary materials supplier for these projects creates a natural opportunity to offer construction waste disposal coordination as a value-added service — similar to how BuildRight coordinates home delivery. This service does not require BuildRight to own waste hauling assets; instead, it partners with vetted waste haulers in each store's service area and earns a coordination fee or commission per booking. This is distinct from W193 (waste management & circular economy) which covers BuildRight's own operational waste; this workflow addresses customer construction waste generated from materials purchased at BuildRight. It is also distinct from W502 (store-level non-hazardous waste management) which covers the store's own waste.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Waste Volume Estimation & Skip Size Recommendation**: Sales Associate estimates waste volume based on the customer's project: (a) project type: bathroom renovation (2–4 cubic meters of waste), kitchen renovation (3–6 cu.m.), full house renovation (10–30 cu.m.), new construction site clearance (20–50+ cu.m.), roofing replacement (5–10 cu.m.); (b) waste types expected: concrete/hollow block rubble, tiles/ceramics, lumber/plywood scraps, metal (rebar, GI sheets), plastic (PVC, packaging), paint/chemical containers; (c) system recommends skip/dumpster size: small skip (2 cu.m. — bathroom reno), medium skip (5 cu.m. — kitchen/single room reno), large skip (10 cu.m. — major renovation), or roll-off container (15–20 cu.m. — construction site); (d) system queries partner hauler availability in the customer's area for the requested dates | Sales Associate | Trade Counter Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Partner Hauler Booking & Pricing**: System coordinates with vetted partner waste haulers: (a) system displays available haulers in the customer's delivery area with: hauler name, LGU-registered permit number, available skip sizes, rental period (standard: 7 days), pricing (delivery, rental, pickup, disposal fee), and customer rating; (b) system auto-selects best-value hauler based on price, availability, and rating; (c) for B2B trade accounts: system applies pre-negotiated corporate rates; (d) waste classification: system flags if waste includes regulated materials (paint/chemical containers per W236 hazmat, asbestos-containing materials requiring special handling); (e) booking confirmed: hauler name, skip size, delivery date, delivery address, rental period, pickup date, total price; (f) system sends booking confirmation to customer (SMS/email) and to partner hauler via API integration | System | Trade Counter Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Documentation & Compliance**: System generates required documentation: (a) waste disposal manifest: description of waste types, estimated volume, origin address, destination disposal site (LGU-approved), hauler permit number; (b) DENR compliance: for projects exceeding 5 cu.m. of construction waste, system notes that the hauler must deliver to a DENR-registered disposal facility per RA 9003; (c) LGU permit reminder: some LGUs require a temporary waste disposal permit for construction projects; system reminds customer to check with their barangay; (d) hazardous material handling: if paint cans, solvents, or chemicals are in the waste stream, system generates separate hazmat handling instructions per W236 and recommends a specialized hazmat hauler; (e) all documentation attached to customer's project order in ERP for traceability | System | Trade Counter Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Delivery, Monitoring & Pickup Coordination**: (a) system sends automated delivery reminder to customer and hauler 1 day before scheduled skip delivery; (b) on delivery day: hauler delivers skip to customer's site, customer signs delivery receipt; system logs delivery confirmation via hauler API or manual update; (c) during rental period: system monitors rental days remaining; sends reminder to customer and hauler 1 day before scheduled pickup; (d) customer may request extension via BuildRight app or store — system checks hauler availability and adjusts pricing for extended rental; (e) on pickup day: hauler collects skip, transports waste to LGU-approved disposal facility, obtains disposal facility receipt (proof of legal disposal); (f) hauler uploads disposal receipt to BuildRight system; (g) system triggers customer satisfaction survey | System / Sales Associate | Trade Counter Supervisor | Ongoing monitoring (automated) + 5 min per extension request |
| 5 | **Settlement, Hauler Payment & Revenue Recognition**: (a) system invoices customer: skip rental total (delivery + rental + pickup + disposal fee) + BuildRight coordination fee (5–8% of hauler charge); (b) for trade account customers: added to monthly billing statement per W8; (c) for walk-in/B2C customers: payment at POS or via payment link; (d) system triggers payment to partner hauler: hauler receives total amount minus BuildRight coordination fee; payment per agreed terms (Net 15 or Net 30); (e) revenue recognition: BuildRight records coordination fee as service revenue; (f) monthly: system generates partner hauler performance report: on-time delivery rate, on-time pickup rate, customer satisfaction score, compliance incidents (missing disposal receipts); (g) quarterly: Trade Counter Supervisor reviews hauler performance and recommends continuation or replacement of underperforming haulers | System / Trade Counter Supervisor | Finance Manager | Per-transaction settlement: automated; Monthly review: 1–2 hrs |

### System Touchpoints
- Partner hauler management module (vendor sub-type in W36 — vendor onboarding)
- Real-time hauler availability & pricing API
- Waste disposal manifest generator
- DENR compliance documentation module
- POS invoicing module (W542)
- Trade account billing module (W8)
- Payment settlement module (W556)
- Customer notification engine (SMS/email/push)
- Partner hauler performance dashboard

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Illegal dumping risk**: If a partner hauler illegally dumps construction waste instead of delivering to an approved facility, BuildRight faces reputational risk even though it is only the coordinator; disposal receipt verification per step 4(f) is critical; mitigated by CTL-266 (controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1))
- **Hauler reliability**: Philippine waste hauling is a fragmented industry with many small operators; service quality varies significantly; must maintain a pool of 2–3 vetted haulers per service area for redundancy
- **Liability for hazardous waste**: Construction waste from older Philippine homes may contain asbestos (roofing, ceiling boards) or lead paint; if hazardous materials are discovered in the skip, disposal costs increase significantly and specialized handling is required per W236
- **LGU permitting complexity**: Each LGU has different construction waste disposal requirements; the system must maintain an LGU-specific compliance rules database


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-request of "Waste Volume Estimation & Skip Size Recommendation" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "Partner Hauler Booking & Pricing" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-document of "Documentation & Compliance" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: per W236; operational: must maintain an LGU-specific compliance rules database

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Trade Counter)**: Waste disposal coordination adds 10–15 minutes per booking; at ~2–3 bookings per store per week (~1,500–2,500/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this is ~20–45 min/week — absorbed by existing Trade Counter staff
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — the service generates coordination fee revenue that exceeds the labor cost

### Time Estimate
- Waste estimation & skip sizing: 5–8 min
- Hauler booking & pricing: 3–5 min
- Documentation & compliance: 2–3 min
- Ongoing monitoring: automated
- Settlement: automated
- **Total per coordination**: 10–16 min active staff time

## W1088. Customer Mortgage & Housing Loan Partner Bank Referral Desk Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquiring about major home construction, renovation, or appliance purchase expresses interest in financing options, or Sales Associate identifies that the customer's project budget exceeds their immediate purchasing capacity |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–5,000 referral interactions/month across all stores (driven by B2C customers undertaking major home improvement projects with average project cost of PHP 50,000–500,000) |
| **Volume** | 1 referral per customer interaction |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Representative |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer Service Representative, Partner Bank/Financing Representative |

### Background

Home improvement retail and housing finance are deeply interconnected in the Philippines. BuildRight's customers — whether building a new home (lumber, cement, steel, tiles), renovating (paint, fixtures, appliances), or upgrading (air conditioning, water heater, kitchen appliances) — frequently face budget constraints that limit their project scope or delay purchases. The Philippine housing finance market offers several products relevant to BuildRight customers: (a) bank home improvement loans (BDO, BPI, Metrobank, Security Bank, PSBank) — typically PHP 100,000–3,000,000, 12–60 month terms, 8–15% annual interest; (b) Pag-IBIG Fund (HDMF) home improvement loan — government-backed, lower interest (3–6.5%, per W966), up to PHP 6,000,000 for qualified members; (c) bank credit card installment conversion — 0% interest promos for 3–24 months on major purchases (BuildRight already supports this per W747); (d) in-house appliance financing via partner banks (similar to equipment leasing per W274); (e) micro-financing for small contractors via partner MFIs. BuildRight does not lend directly but earns a referral commission from partner banks for each successful loan origination. This service increases BuildRight's average transaction value (customers who finance spend 2–3× more than cash customers) and accelerates project purchases that would otherwise be delayed. This is distinct from W953 (contractor micro-lending) which focuses on small contractor working capital; this workflow addresses consumer/B2B customer housing and renovation financing. It is also distinct from W966 (construction loan documentation assistance) which is a calculator/advisory service; this workflow includes the active bank referral and application facilitation process.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Financing Need Identification**: (a) Sales Associate identifies financing need during project consultation: customer mentions budget constraint, asks about installment options, or the project estimate exceeds PHP 50,000; (b) Associate introduces financing options: "BuildRight has partner banks that offer home improvement loans with competitive rates. Would you like me to connect you with our financing desk?"; (c) Customer Service Representative (or dedicated Financing Desk Associate in high-volume stores) conducts preliminary assessment: (i) customer type: employed, self-employed, OFW, pensioner; (ii) estimated project cost; (iii) desired loan amount; (iv) preferred repayment term; (v) existing bank relationships; (vi) Pag-IBIG membership status; (d) system displays recommended financing options based on assessment | Sales Associate / Customer Service Rep | Customer Service Representative | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Financing Option Matching & Partner Bank Selection**: System matches customer profile to available financing products: (a) **Bank Home Improvement Loan** — for project costs PHP 100K+: requirements include proof of income (latest ITR or BIR Form 2316, latest 3 months payslips, COE), 2 valid government IDs, proof of billing address, marriage certificate (if applicable); processing time: 5–7 banking days; banks: BDO Home Loan, BPI Family Savings, Metrobank Home Improvement; (b) **Pag-IBIG Home Improvement Loan** — for qualified Pag-IBIG members: requires 24 monthly contributions, updated membership, property titled under borrower's name or relative's name with consent; processing time: 10–15 working days; (c) **Credit Card Installment** — for purchases PHP 5,000+: instant approval, 0% interest for 3/6/12 months (during promotional periods), no additional documents; (d) **In-Store Financing (Partner Bank Desk)** — some stores host a partner bank representative 2–3 days per week for on-the-spot application processing; (e) system displays comparison table: product, amount range, interest rate, term, processing time, monthly payment estimate | System | Customer Service Representative | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Application Facilitation & Document Assistance**: (a) Customer Service Representative assists customer with financing application: (i) provides partner bank application form (pre-printed with BuildRight referral code for commission tracking); (ii) reviews customer's documents for completeness; (iii) for Pag-IBIG loans: assists with HDMF form verification and member contribution printout; (iv) for bank loans: explains income documentation requirements and assists with self-employed customer's financial statement preparation (basic guidance); (b) for in-store bank desk days: schedules appointment with partner bank representative at the store; (c) system generates project cost estimate on BuildRight letterhead (required by banks as proof of purpose) linked to quotation per W162; (d) Customer Service Representative submits application to partner bank via email or in-person (on bank desk days); (e) system logs referral in CRM with: customer name, project description, estimated loan amount, partner bank, submission date, referral code | Customer Service Rep | Customer Service Representative | 15–25 min per application |
| 4 | **Loan Tracking & Purchase Conversion**: (a) system tracks loan application status via partner bank API (where available) or manual status update by Customer Service Representative; (b) system sends periodic status updates to customer: "Your home improvement loan application with [Bank] is being processed. Expected decision by [date]."; (c) upon loan approval: system notifies Customer Service Representative and Sales Associate who initiated the referral; (d) Customer Service Representative contacts customer: "Congratulations! Your loan has been approved for PHP [amount]. You can now proceed with your project purchase at BuildRight."; (e) customer returns to store (or purchases online) to complete the material order; system links the original project estimate to the final purchase; (f) bank disburses loan amount: either directly to customer's account (customer pays BuildRight) or via manager's check payable to BuildRight (for large B2B projects); (g) BuildRight earns referral commission from partner bank (typically 0.5–1.5% of loan amount, paid quarterly by the bank) | System / Customer Service Rep | Customer Service Representative | Ongoing: ~10 min per status check/approval follow-up |
| 5 | **Monthly Referral Performance Reporting & Partner Bank Review**: (a) system generates monthly referral performance report: number of referrals by store, number of applications submitted, approval rate, total loan amount originated, average project purchase value (financed vs. non-financed), referral commission earned; (b) Store Manager reviews financing desk performance; (c) quarterly: Category Manager (Appliances/Services) reviews partner bank relationships: approval rates, commission payments, customer satisfaction, competitive rate benchmarking; (d) annual: VP Merchandising negotiates referral commission rates and exclusive promotional terms with partner banks (e.g., 0% installment promos during BuildRight sale events per W13) | System / Store Manager | VP Merchandising | Monthly report: 30 min; Quarterly review: 2–3 hrs |

### System Touchpoints
- Customer financing profile module (CRM extension)
- Partner bank product catalog (financing product master)
- Quotation module (W162) — project estimate for bank submission
- Referral tracking & commission module
- Partner bank API (application status)
- Customer notification engine (SMS/email/push)
- POS installment processing (W747) — for credit card installment path
- Store-level P&L (W67) — referral commission revenue tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Low approval rates**: Philippine bank loan approval rates for home improvement loans are ~40–60% (lower than auto or housing purchase loans due to unsecured nature); managing customer expectations is critical to avoid disappointment and brand damage
- **Slow processing times**: Bank loan processing takes 5–15 days; during this time, customers may purchase from competitors or abandon the project; the credit card installment path provides an instant-gratification alternative
- **Document complexity**: Self-employed and OFW customers often struggle with income documentation requirements; the financing desk must provide clear guidance without crossing into financial advice (BuildRight is not a licensed financial advisor)
- **Partner bank exclusivity risk**: Over-reliance on a single partner bank creates risk if the bank changes terms or discontinues the partnership; BuildRight must maintain 3–4 banking partners per region; mitigated by the Customer Service Representative review and approval gate


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-document of "Financing Option Matching & Partner Bank Selection" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "printed with BuildRight referral code for commission tracking" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-validate of "check payable to BuildRight" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Customer Service Representative review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Customer Service Representative**: Financing desk duty adds ~15–25 minutes per application; at ~15–25 referrals per store per month (per the Frequency), this represents ~1–2 hours/month — absorbed by existing Customer Service Rep in most stores; high-volume Metro Manila stores may assign a dedicated financing desk associate
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — referral commission revenue more than covers the labor allocation

### Time Estimate
- Need identification: 5–8 min
- Option matching: 3–5 min
- Application facilitation: 15–25 min
- Loan tracking: 10 min per follow-up (ongoing)
- Monthly reporting: 30 min
- **Total per referral**: 25–40 min active staff time

## W1094. Customer Construction Equipment Rental Partner Coordination & Booking

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests construction equipment rental (excavator, backhoe, concrete mixer, scaffolding, generator, welding machine, jackhammer, plate compactor, etc.) during a project material purchase, or accesses the equipment rental section via the BuildRight app or store kiosk |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 booking requests/month across all stores (equipment rental is a growing value-added service for BuildRight's B2B contractor base) |
| **Volume** | 1 equipment rental booking per request |
| **Owner** | Trade Counter / Pro Desk Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Trade Counter), Equipment Rental Partner, Customer |

### Background

BuildRight already operates a tool rental service per W139, covering handheld and benchtop power tools for DIY customers. However, the construction equipment rental market is a distinct and much larger segment serving BuildRight's B2B contractor customers (5,200 trade accounts) and more ambitious B2C renovation customers. Construction equipment needed for typical BuildRight customer projects includes: (a) **earthmoving**: mini-excavator (1–3 ton), backhoe loader, plate compactor, rammer/tamper; (b) **concrete work**: transit mixer truck (for ready-mix concrete delivery), portable concrete mixer (bag-type, 1-bag or 2-bag capacity), concrete vibrator, power trowel; (c) **lifting & access**: scaffolding set (mobile tower), boom lift/scissor lift (for multi-story projects), material hoist; (d) **power generation**: portable generator (3KVA–10KVA for on-site power, critical in areas with unreliable electricity); (e) **demolition**: jackhammer/breaker (electric or pneumatic), concrete saw; (f) **welding**: portable welding machine (arc/MIG/TIG); (g) **material handling**: forklift (1.5–3 ton capacity), pallet jack. BuildRight does not own this heavy equipment; instead, it partners with equipment rental companies in each region and earns a booking coordination fee. This model is common in Philippine hardware retail — the customer buys materials from BuildRight and rents equipment through BuildRight's partner network, creating a one-stop-shop experience. This is distinct from W139 (tool rental) which covers BuildRight-owned tools; this workflow covers partner-owned construction equipment. It is also distinct from W1062 (scaffolding rental) which covers a specific equipment type; this workflow covers the broader equipment rental coordination process.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Equipment Requirement Identification**: Sales Associate identifies equipment needs based on the customer's project: (a) project type: foundation excavation (mini-excavator + dump truck for soil hauling), concrete pouring (portable mixer + vibrator + trowel), multi-story construction (scaffolding + material hoist), demolition (jackhammer + concrete saw), roofing (scaffolding + generator for power tools); (b) equipment specifications: capacity/size (excavator: 1-ton mini for tight spaces, 3-ton for open sites), power source (electric — requires on-site power, or diesel/petrol — self-contained), duration needed (daily, weekly, monthly rates); (c) site conditions: accessibility (narrow alley in urban barangay limits equipment width to <1.0m), ground conditions (soft soil may require tracked vs. wheeled equipment), and noise restrictions (residential area may limit operating hours per LGU ordinance); (d) operator requirement: does the customer need an equipment operator (required for excavators and heavy equipment) or will they self-operate (for mixers, generators, jackhammers)? | Sales Associate | Trade Counter Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Partner Matching & Availability Check**: (a) system queries equipment rental partners in the customer's service area per W600 (service contractor accreditation — extended to equipment rental companies); (b) system displays available equipment: equipment type, capacity, brand/model, rental rate (daily/weekly/monthly), operator availability (with or without), delivery/pickup availability, partner name and rating; (c) for equipment requiring certified operators (excavators, backhoes, forklifts): system verifies partner provides TESDA-certified operators per Philippine DOLE regulations; (d) system auto-selects best-value option (lowest total cost = rental rate + delivery + operator if needed, weighted by partner rating and availability); (e) for high-demand equipment during peak construction season (January–May dry season): system may show limited availability and offer waitlist or alternative equipment options | System | Trade Counter Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Booking Confirmation & Contract Generation**: (a) system generates equipment rental booking: equipment type, model, rental period (start date/time, end date/time), rental rate, operator (if applicable), delivery address, site contact person, total estimated cost (rental + delivery + operator + fuel surcharge if applicable); (b) system generates rental agreement with terms: (i) damage liability (customer responsible for damage beyond normal wear); (ii) late return charges (1.5× daily rate per day late); (iii) fuel policy (diesel/petrol equipment returned with same fuel level); (iv) insurance requirement (customer must provide evidence of project insurance or purchase rental insurance from partner); (v) operator conduct (for partner-supplied operators: customer may not direct operator to perform unsafe operations); (vi) cancellation policy (free cancellation 24+ hours before delivery; 50% charge for same-day cancellation); (c) customer signs rental agreement (digital or wet signature); (d) system sends confirmation to customer and partner; (e) for trade account customers: rental charges billed to account per W8; for B2C customers: pre-authorization hold on credit card or cash deposit at POS | System / Sales Associate | Trade Counter Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Delivery, Monitoring & Return Coordination**: (a) partner delivers equipment to customer site on scheduled date; (b) customer and partner conduct joint equipment inspection at delivery: document existing damage (photos + written record), verify fuel level, test operation; (c) system logs delivery confirmation; (d) during rental period: system monitors rental duration and sends return reminders 24 hours before scheduled return; (e) for rental extensions: customer notifies BuildRight (via app, phone, or store) — system checks partner availability for extension and adjusts billing; (f) at return: customer and partner conduct joint return inspection: verify equipment condition matches delivery inspection, fuel level, and hours meter reading; (g) system logs return confirmation and final charges; (h) any damage disputes: documented with photos, assessed by partner, and charged to customer per rental agreement terms | Partner / System | Trade Counter Supervisor | Delivery/return inspection: 15–30 min each; Monitoring: automated |
| 5 | **Settlement & Partner Performance Management**: (a) system generates final rental invoice: rental charges (actual duration × rate), delivery/pickup charges, operator charges, fuel charges, damage charges (if any), taxes, total; (b) for trade accounts: added to monthly billing; for B2C: credit card pre-auth converted to actual charge or deposit refunded less charges; (c) BuildRight earns coordination fee: 5–10% of total rental charge; (d) system updates partner performance record: on-time delivery rate, equipment condition rating, customer satisfaction score, damage dispute rate; (e) quarterly: Trade Counter Supervisor reviews partner performance; poorly rated partners are placed on probation or replaced | System / Trade Counter Supervisor | Finance Manager | Per-booking settlement: automated; Quarterly review: 2–3 hrs |

### System Touchpoints
- Equipment rental partner management module (W600 extension)
- Real-time equipment availability & pricing API (partner integration)
- Rental agreement generator
- Customer notification engine (SMS/email/app)
- POS invoicing & payment module (W542 / W556)
- Trade account billing module (W8)
- Credit card pre-authorization module
- Partner performance dashboard
- Equipment inspection documentation module (photo upload, damage logging)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Equipment damage liability**: Construction equipment is expensive (a mini-excavator costs PHP 2–5M); damage during rental creates disputes between customer and partner; clear inspection documentation at delivery and return is essential
- **Operator safety**: Equipment operators working on customer sites face safety risks; DOLE requires operators to have TESDA certification and the partner to provide proper PPE; any operator injury on a BuildRight-referred project creates reputational risk
- **Equipment availability during peak season**: Dry season (January–May) is peak construction season in the Philippines; popular equipment (excavators, concrete mixers) may be fully booked weeks in advance; the system must manage customer expectations and offer alternatives
- **Partner reliability**: Small equipment rental operators in provincial areas may have inconsistent service quality; maintaining a pool of 2–3 vetted partners per service area is critical


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-track of "Equipment Requirement Identification" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "Partner Matching & Availability Check" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "Booking Confirmation & Contract Generation" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: must manage customer expectations and offer alternatives

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Trade Counter)**: Equipment rental coordination adds 10–15 minutes per booking; at ~2–3 bookings per store per week (~1,500–2,500/month chain-wide per the Frequency), this represents ~20–45 min/week — absorbed by existing Trade Counter staff
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — coordination fee revenue covers the labor allocation

### Time Estimate
- Equipment requirement identification: 5–8 min
- Partner matching: 3–5 min
- Booking & contract: 5–8 min
- Delivery/return monitoring: automated + 15–30 min inspections
- Settlement: automated
- **Total per booking**: 13–21 min active staff time

## W1101. Store-Level Customer Door & Window Security Grille Custom Fabrication Coordination

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests custom-made security grilles for doors or windows during a building material purchase, or Sales Associate identifies the need during a fencing/security consultation per W1084 |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–5,000 coordination requests/month across all stores (security grilles are a standard feature in Philippine homes — nearly every ground-floor window and door has steel security grilles) |
| **Volume** | 1 fabrication project per coordination (typically covering 4–15 windows + 1–2 doors) |
| **Owner** | Trade Counter / Pro Desk Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Trade Counter / Lumber & Building Materials), Fabrication Service Partner (accredited welder/fabricator), Customer |

### Background

Steel security grilles (locally called "berde" or "rehas" in Filipino) are an essential feature of Philippine residential construction. Due to property crime concerns in both urban and rural areas, virtually every Filipino home installs steel grilles on all ground-floor windows, main doors, and back doors. The standard Philippine security grille is fabricated from: (a) flat bar steel (typically ¼" × 1" or 6mm × 25mm) for the main frame; (b) square tube (16mm × 16mm or 20mm × 20mm) or round bar (12mm or 16mm diameter) for the vertical bars; (c) welding (arc or MIG) at all joints; (d) anti-rust primer and enamel paint finish (black or custom color per W1093). The grilles are custom-made to fit each window or door opening exactly, because Philippine window sizes are not standardized — they vary by house design, builder, and era of construction. The fabrication process requires: (a) on-site measurement of each window/door opening; (b) custom cutting, welding, and finishing at the fabricator's workshop; (c) delivery and installation at the customer's home; (d) anchoring to the concrete or hollow block wall using expansion bolts or embedded steel brackets. This workflow coordinates the custom fabrication process from measurement through installation, connecting BuildRight's material supply (steel, paint, hardware) with accredited fabrication partners.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Measurement Requirements & Design Specifications**: (a) Sales Associate gathers grille specifications: (i) number of windows requiring grilles and approximate dimensions (width × height per window); (ii) number of doors requiring security doors and approximate dimensions; (iii) bar style preference: vertical bars only (most common and cost-effective), vertical + horizontal (cross-hatch for higher security), or decorative scrollwork (premium); (iv) bar spacing: standard (100–120mm between bars — prevents head entry) or tight (80mm — maximum security for high-risk areas); (v) frame material: flat bar (¼" × 1") or angle bar (1" × 1" × ¼"); (vi) bar material: square tube (16mm, 20mm) — modern look, or round bar (12mm, 16mm) — traditional; (vii) finish: primer + black enamel (standard), primer + color-matched enamel (per W1093), powder coating (premium), or stainless steel (highest grade, coastal areas); (viii) hinged or fixed: hinged grilles for windows that need opening (for cleaning or emergency egress) vs. fixed grilles; (ix) locking mechanism for hinged grilles: barrel bolt, padlock hasp, or keyed lock | Sales Associate | Trade Counter Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **Fabrication Partner Matching & Quotation**: (a) system queries accredited fabrication partners (welding shops) in the customer's area per W600 (service contractor accreditation); (b) system generates material cost estimate: (i) steel material: flat bar × total perimeter length (all windows/doors) + square tube/round bar × total bar count × standard bar length; (ii) welding consumables: welding rods/electrodes (E6013 or E7018), cutting gas (if oxy-acetylene); (iii) paint: anti-rust primer (red oxide, 1 coat) + enamel paint (2 coats) — quantity per surface area of finished grilles; (iv) hardware: expansion bolts (4 per window grille, 6 per door grille), hinges (2 per hinged grille), barrel bolts/locks, and miscellaneous (wire brush, sandpaper for surface prep); (c) system estimates fabrication labor: cutting, welding, grinding, and finishing — based on linear meters of welding and number of pieces; (d) system estimates installation labor: delivery, fitting, anchoring, and cleanup; (e) total project cost estimate = material cost + fabrication labor + installation labor; (f) typical cost ranges: window grille (1.0m × 1.2m, standard vertical bars, primer + black enamel): PHP 2,500–4,000 per window; door security gate (0.9m × 2.1m, hinged with lock): PHP 6,000–12,000 per door | System | Trade Counter Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **On-Site Measurement & Fabrication Order**: (a) for accuracy: Associate recommends that the fabrication partner perform on-site measurement before fabrication (Philippine window openings often deviate from planned dimensions by 10–30mm); (b) system schedules on-site measurement visit: partner visits customer's home within 2–3 days to measure every opening precisely; (c) partner sends measurement sheet to BuildRight system: exact width, height, and any irregularities (arched top, angled sill, pipe/conduit obstruction); (d) system generates final fabrication order with precise dimensions and updates material quantities accordingly; (e) material delivery: BuildRight delivers steel, paint, and hardware to the fabrication partner's workshop (not to the customer's home — the partner fabricates at their workshop) per W668 | Trade Counter Supervisor / Fabrication Partner | Trade Counter Supervisor | Measurement visit: 1–2 hrs; Order update: 15 min |
| 4 | **Fabrication, Quality Check & Installation**: (a) fabrication partner fabricates grilles per the measurement sheet: (i) cuts flat bar and square tube/round bar to size; (ii) assembles frame by welding; (iii) welds vertical bars at specified spacing; (iv) grinds all welds smooth (no sharp edges); (v) installs hinges and lock hardware (for hinged grilles); (vi) applies anti-rust primer (1 coat) and enamel paint (2 coats) with drying time between coats; (b) fabrication timeline: 3–7 days depending on scope (4–6 windows: 3 days; 10–15 windows + 2 doors: 5–7 days); (c) Trade Counter Supervisor performs quality check (or partner self-certifies per W600 accreditation): (i) dimensions match measurement sheet (±5mm tolerance); (ii) weld quality: no visible gaps, porosity, or undercut; (iii) bar spacing uniform and within specification; (iv) paint coverage: complete, no bare steel visible, smooth finish; (v) hinges operate smoothly, locks engage properly; (d) partner delivers and installs grilles at customer's home: anchors to wall with expansion bolts, seals around frame with silicone sealant, and verifies operation | Fabrication Partner / Trade Counter Supervisor | Trade Counter Supervisor | Fabrication: 3–7 days; Installation: 1 day |
| 5 | **Customer Acceptance, Settlement & Warranty**: (a) customer inspects installed grilles: verifies fit, finish, operation, and security; (b) customer signs acceptance; (c) system generates final invoice: material cost (BuildRight) + fabrication labor (partner) + installation labor (partner) = total project cost; (d) payment: (i) for trade account customers: added to monthly billing per W8; (ii) for B2C customers: 50% deposit at order placement, 50% upon installation acceptance; (e) BuildRight pays partner: fabrication + installation labor minus BuildRight coordination fee (8–12%); (f) warranty: fabrication partner provides 1-year workmanship warranty (weld integrity, paint adhesion); BuildRight provides material quality warranty per manufacturer terms; (g) system sends customer satisfaction survey and updates partner performance record per W600 | System / Trade Counter Supervisor | Finance Manager | Settlement: automated; Satisfaction: 5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Service contractor accreditation module (W600 — extended to fabrication partners)
- Material estimation module (steel, paint, hardware quantity calculator)
- Partner availability & scheduling module
- Measurement sheet input module
- Quality check checklist module
- POS invoicing module (W542) — split billing (materials + labor)
- Trade account billing module (W8)
- Customer notification engine (SMS/email)
- Partner performance dashboard

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Measurement accuracy**: If the on-site measurement is wrong, the fabricated grille won't fit; the 2–3 day measurement visit step is critical and cannot be skipped (despite customer impatience)
- **Weld quality**: Poor weld quality (cold welds, porosity) creates security risk — the grille fails under force; quality check per step 4c is essential
- **Corrosion in coastal areas**: Philippine coastal environments (many BuildRight stores are in coastal cities) cause rapid steel corrosion; anti-rust primer quality and paint thickness are critical for longevity; stainless steel option must be actively recommended for coastal installations
- **Emergency egress safety**: Security grilles that cannot be opened from inside create fire safety hazards; hinged grilles with inside-only locks must be recommended for bedrooms and sleeping areas per BFP fire safety regulations


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-match of "Measurement Requirements & Design Specifications" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "count × standard bar length" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "generates final fabrication order with precise dimensions and updates material…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Trade Counter)**: Coordination adds 10–15 minutes per project; at ~15–25 projects per store per month (per the Frequency), this represents ~1–2 hours/month — absorbed by existing Trade Counter staff
- **Trade Counter Supervisor**: Quality check adds ~30 minutes per project; at ~15–25 projects per store per month, this represents ~8–12 hours/month — significant but absorbed by existing role; may delegate to Senior Sales Associate in high-volume stores
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — coordination fee revenue covers the labor allocation

### Time Estimate
- Specification gathering: 8–12 min
- Quotation: 3–5 min
- Measurement coordination: 15 min
- Quality check: 30 min per project
- Settlement: automated
- **Total per coordination**: 25–35 min active staff time

---

## W1104. Customer Tool Rental Return Damage Assessment & Billing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer returns a rented tool or equipment at the store customer service counter |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–4,000 rental returns/month across all stores (complement to W139 tool rental operations) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 tools per return transaction |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Representative |
| **Participants** | Customer Service Representative, Department Supervisor (Tools), Customer |

### Background

BuildRight Depot operates a tool and equipment rental service (W139) allowing customers to rent power tools (drills, grinders, circular saws, jackhammers), specialty equipment (tile cutters, concrete mixers, scaffolding, pressure washers), and commercial-grade items not typically owned by DIY customers. The rental program is a differentiator for the trade/professional customer segment (30% of revenue) and drives foot traffic from contractors who also purchase consumables and materials during rental visits. Tool rental returns require a structured damage assessment process to: (a) determine if the tool is returned in the same condition as issued (normal wear vs. damage); (b) assess additional charges for damage beyond normal wear; (c) process the security deposit refund or partial charge; (d) determine if the tool needs repair, cleaning, or recalibration before re-rental; (e) update the tool's maintenance history in the rental asset tracking system. This workflow specifically covers the return and damage assessment process; the initial rental issuance and overall rental operations are covered under W139.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Rental Record Retrieval & Pre-Return Verification**: (a) Customer Service Representative (CSR) scans the customer's rental agreement barcode or looks up by loyalty card/phone number; (b) system displays: tool ID, tool description, rental start date/time, agreed return date/time, rental rate, security deposit amount, pre-rental condition notes, and photos of tool at issuance; (c) CSR verifies customer identity (government ID matching rental agreement); (d) system auto-calculates elapsed rental time and any overdue charges: overdue rate = 1.5× standard daily rate per day late | Customer Service Representative | Customer Service Representative | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Physical Tool Inspection & Condition Comparison**: CSR inspects returned tool against pre-rental condition: (a) visual inspection for new scratches, dents, cracks, or missing parts; (b) functional test: power on, run for 30 seconds, check for unusual noise or vibration; (c) accessory check: verify all accessories listed at issuance are returned (blades, bits, chargers, batteries, cables, manuals, carrying case); (d) cleanliness check: tool should be returned free of excessive concrete dust, paint, or mud (reasonable use debris is acceptable); (e) for electrical tools: inspect cord for cuts, check plug integrity; (f) for gas-powered equipment: check fuel level (should be returned with minimum fuel), oil condition; (g) for scaffolding: verify all pins, braces, and platforms are present and undamaged; (h) CSR photographs any damage or missing items using store tablet | Customer Service Representative | Department Supervisor (Tools) | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Damage Classification & Assessment**: CSR classifies tool condition: (a) **Normal Wear** — minor cosmetic scratches, expected consumable wear (blade dulling, brush wear), dust/debris from normal use → no additional charge; (b) **Minor Damage** — small dent, scuffed housing, missing low-value accessory (screw, washer) → flat PHP 150–500 cleaning/minor repair surcharge; (c) **Moderate Damage** — cracked housing, damaged cord, bent blade, broken accessory → repair cost estimate generated by system based on tool type and damage code, typically PHP 500–2,000; (d) **Major Damage** — motor burnout, cracked frame, missing major component (battery, blade guard) → full repair or replacement cost, capped at the tool's current replacement value; (e) **Total Loss** — tool returned non-functional, submerged in water, dropped from height, or stolen → security deposit forfeited + additional charge up to replacement value per rental agreement terms. For categories (c)–(e), Department Supervisor is called for visual confirmation and approval | Customer Service Representative | Department Supervisor (Tools) | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Security Deposit Settlement & Additional Billing**: (a) system calculates final bill: base rental charge + overdue charges (if any) + damage surcharge (if any) - security deposit held; (b) if net amount is positive (customer owes more): customer pays balance via POS (W5B); (c) if net amount is negative (deposit exceeds charges): refund processed to original payment method per W101 (customer refund processing), or issued as store credit per W781; (d) system generates itemized rental return receipt with condition assessment details; (e) customer signs digital acknowledgment of return condition; (f) if customer disputes damage assessment, escalate to Store Manager per dispute resolution hierarchy | Customer Service Representative / POS | Department Supervisor (Tools) | 5–7 min |
| 5 | **Post-Return Tool Reconditioning & Availability Update**: (a) tool placed in designated "inspection/reconditioning" area in backroom; (b) if **Normal Wear**: CSR cleans tool (compressed air, wipe down), functional test confirmation, return to rental shelf; system updates tool status to "Available"; (c) if **Minor/Moderate Damage**: tool sent to in-store repair station (W440 — power tool service & repair center) with damage assessment form; system updates status to "In Repair" with estimated availability date; (d) if **Major Damage / Total Loss**: tool dispositioned per W91 (damaged goods disposition) if uneconomical to repair, or sent to vendor for factory refurbishment; insurance claim filed per W859 (vehicle & fleet insurance) if rental tool is covered under rental equipment policy; (e) system tracks repair turnaround time and updates rental availability calendar | Stock Associate / CSR | Department Supervisor (Tools) | 10–20 min (for normal wear); varies for repair |

### System Touchpoints
- Tool rental management module — agreement lookup, inventory status, availability calendar
- Pre-rental condition photo repository (linked to tool asset ID)
- POS module (W5B) — rental return billing and deposit settlement
- Customer refund processing (W101) — deposit refund
- Store credit issuance (W781) — credit in lieu of cash refund
- Power tool repair center module (W440) — repair work order creation
- Damaged goods disposition (W91) — write-off for total loss
- Fixed asset tracking — rental tool depreciation and lifecycle (W39)
- Customer loyalty integration — rental history tracked for tier benefits

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Subjective damage assessment**: "Normal wear" vs. "minor damage" can be subjective; photography at issuance and return is critical evidence but adds processing time
- **Customer disputes**: Contractors who use tools heavily may contest damage charges; clear rental agreement terms and photographic evidence are essential
- **Rental tool downtime**: Damaged tools in repair reduce available rental fleet, potentially losing rental revenue; a 5–10% fleet buffer is recommended
- **Hygiene concerns**: Returned tools that have been used in construction sites (concrete dust, paint, adhesives) require thorough cleaning before re-rental
- **Liability for tool malfunction**: If a tool is returned damaged but not flagged, and subsequently rented to another customer who is injured, BuildRight faces liability exposure


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-scan of "scans the customer's rental agreement barcode or looks up by loyalty card/phone…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "check for unusual noise or vibration" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "generated by system based on tool type and damage code, typically PHP 500–2,000" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Customer Service Representative review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Customer Service Representative**: Tool return processing adds 15–30 minutes per return; at ~15–20 returns/store/month (per the Frequency), this represents ~1–1.5 hours/month — absorbed by existing CSR (1 per store)
- **Department Supervisor (Tools)**: Damage escalation review adds ~1–2 hours/week during peak rental periods
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — damage surcharge revenue (estimated PHP 100,000–200,000/month chain-wide) more than covers processing labor

### Time Estimate
- Record retrieval: 3–5 min
- Physical inspection: 5–10 min
- Damage classification: 5–8 min
- Billing settlement: 5–7 min
- Tool reconditioning: 10–20 min (normal wear)
- **Total per return**: 28–50 min

## W1109. Customer Project Material Surplus Buy-Back & Recycling Program

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer brings unused/surplus building materials to store for buy-back, or customer requests a project completion surplus pickup |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–5,000 surplus buy-back transactions/month across all stores |
| **Volume** | 1–10 items per transaction |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Representative |
| **Participants** | Customer Service Representative, Department Supervisor, Customer |

### Background

Filipino contractors and DIY homeowners frequently end up with surplus building materials after project completion: unused cement bags (leftover from a pour), extra tiles (bought 10% extra for wastage but didn't use all), unopened paint cans, extra lumber pieces, surplus electrical wire, and leftover plumbing fittings. In the Philippines, the common practice is to either: (a) store the surplus indefinitely (cluttering homes and job sites), (b) sell to neighbors at a discount, or (c) discard as construction waste. BuildRight Depot's surplus buy-back program creates a structured channel for customers to return unused materials for a partial refund or store credit, which BuildRight can then resell as open-box/discounted items (per W549) or return to vendors (per W88). This program: (i) drives customer loyalty by reducing the perceived risk of over-buying; (ii) generates foot traffic as customers return to the store for the buy-back and often purchase additional items; (iii) provides a source of discounted inventory for budget-conscious customers; (iv) supports sustainability goals by reducing construction waste. This workflow is distinct from W12 (returns & exchanges) which handles defective or unwanted items within the standard return window; this workflow specifically handles post-project surplus materials that may be beyond the standard return period but are in resalable condition.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Surplus Material Intake & Eligibility Check**: (a) Customer brings surplus materials to the customer service counter; (b) CSR verifies eligibility: (i) items must be BuildRight-branded or identifiable as BuildRight inventory (receipt, loyalty account purchase history, or SKU barcode scan); (ii) items must be in resalable condition: unopened packaging (cement bags intact, tile boxes sealed, paint cans unopened) or minimally used and clean (lumber without damage, full-length); (iii) items must not be hazardous materials requiring special handling (paint/chemicals must be unopened); (iv) items must be in the current active catalog (not discontinued); (c) system looks up original purchase price from customer's transaction history; (d) items not meeting eligibility criteria are declined with explanation and disposal guidance offered per W502 (non-hazardous waste management) | Customer Service Representative | Customer Service Representative | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Condition Grading & Buy-Back Valuation**: (a) CSR grades each item's condition: **Grade A** — unopened, original packaging intact, current catalog item → buy-back at 70% of original purchase price; **Grade B** — opened but complete and undamaged, or minor cosmetic imperfection → buy-back at 50% of original purchase price; **Grade C** — partial quantity (e.g., half a bag of cement, cut lumber), clean and usable → buy-back at 30% of original purchase price; (b) system auto-calculates buy-back values based on grade and original purchase price; (c) customer chooses compensation: (i) store credit (additional 10% bonus on buy-back value, incentivizing reinvestment at BuildRight), (ii) cash refund via GCash or bank transfer (3–5 day processing), (iii) loyalty points (converted at standard rate); (d) Department Supervisor reviews and approves any single transaction exceeding PHP 10,000 in buy-back value | Customer Service Representative | Department Supervisor (if > PHP 10K) | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Surplus Item Processing & Resale Preparation**: (a) surplus items are processed for resale: Grade A items restocked to original shelf location via standard inventory putaway; Grade B items labeled as "Open Box — Surplus Buy-Back" with discounted price per W549 (damaged/open-box item processing) and placed in designated clearance area; Grade C items labeled as "Partial/Remnant — Surplus" with calculated price and placed in the remnant/cutoff section (common for lumber, wire, pipes); (b) system creates inventory receipt at buy-back cost (not original cost) to ensure accurate margin tracking; (c) resale price set by system: Grade A at 90% SRP, Grade B at 70% SRP, Grade C at 50% SRP (or per-unit pricing for remnants); (d) buy-back inventory flagged in system for shrinkage reporting exclusion (to avoid inflating shrinkage numbers with intentional markdowns) | Stock Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **Accounting & Reporting**: (a) buy-back transaction recorded: debit Inventory (at buy-back cost), debit Buy-Back Expense (difference between buy-back cost and original cost if still on hand), credit Cash/Store Credit/GC Payable; (b) monthly buy-back program report: total items bought back by category, total buy-back cost, total resale revenue, net program margin, customer participation rate; (c) program ROI calculated: (resale revenue − buy-back cost − processing labor) / buy-back cost; (d) report shared with Category Manager for pricing optimization and program viability assessment | System / Finance | Category Manager | automated monthly |

### System Touchpoints
- Customer purchase history lookup (loyalty account / transaction search)
- Inventory receipt module — buy-back inventory creation at buy-back cost
- Open-box / clearance pricing module (W549) — resale pricing
- Store credit issuance (W781) — compensation processing
- GCash / bank transfer module (W99) — cash refund
- Loyalty points module (W17) — points conversion
- GL posting — buy-back expense and inventory accounting
- Shrinkage reporting module (W845) — buy-back exclusion flag
- Category performance reporting (W101) — program analytics

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Fraud risk**: Customers may attempt to buy back items purchased at other retailers or items they did not actually purchase; receipt/loyalty account verification mitigates this
- **Storage and display**: Grade B/C items require dedicated clearance floor space that may compete with full-price merchandise display
- **Cement bag integrity**: Opened cement bags are effectively worthless (cement hardens quickly); Grade C cement is not accepted
- **Tile shade matching**: Surplus tiles from different production batches may have slight shade variations; resale must note "surplus — shade may vary from current stock"
- **Margin erosion**: If too many customers over-buy expecting easy buy-back, it shifts inventory risk back to BuildRight; buy-back pricing at 30–70% of original creates a natural deterrent


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-scan of "Surplus Material Intake & Eligibility Check" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "catalog item → buy-back at 70% of original purchase price" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "discounted price per W549" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Customer Service Representative review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Customer Service Representative**: Buy-back processing adds 15–25 minutes per transaction; at ~15–25 per store per month (per the Frequency), this represents ~1–2 hours/month — absorbed by existing CSR
- **Stock Associate**: Resale preparation adds ~2–3 hours/week across all grades
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — program is expected to be margin-positive from resale revenue

### Time Estimate
- Intake & eligibility: 5–8 min
- Grading & valuation: 5–10 min
- Resale preparation: 10–15 min
- **Total per transaction**: 20–33 min

## W1113. Customer E-Gift Card Purchase & Digital Delivery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases an e-gift card via BuildRight website, mobile app, or in-store |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–6,000 e-gift card purchases/month (within the W1476 program register; growing segment, especially during holidays) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 e-gift cards per purchase transaction |
| **Owner** | Ecommerce Operations Manager |
| **Participants** | Customer (purchaser), Customer (recipient), Ecommerce Platform, POS System, Finance (GL) |

### Background

Gift cards are an important revenue and customer acquisition channel for retail. BuildRight currently manages physical gift cards (W28 — gift card & store credit lifecycle) but the e-gift card segment — digital gift cards purchased online and delivered via email or SMS — is growing rapidly, driven by: (a) OFW (Overseas Filipino Worker) gift-giving — OFWs sending digital gift cards to family in the Philippines for home improvement projects; (b) corporate bulk purchases — companies buying e-gift cards as employee rewards or Christmas gifts; (c) last-minute gifting — customers who need a gift immediately and cannot visit a store; (d) birthday/holiday gifts — especially during ber months (Sep–Dec) when gift-giving peaks in the Philippines. E-gift cards differ from physical gift cards in several operational aspects: (i) no physical inventory to manage or secure; (ii) instant delivery via email/SMS; (iii) redemption via QR code on mobile screen (no physical card to present); (iv) higher fraud risk (digital theft, unauthorized purchases); (v) different accounting treatment (immediate liability recognition vs. deferred for physical cards awaiting shipment). This workflow covers the end-to-end e-gift card lifecycle from purchase to redemption, complementing W28 (physical gift card) and W896 (corporate/bulk gift card purchases).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **E-Gift Card Purchase & Personalization**: (a) customer selects e-gift card on BuildRight website/app or at POS: (i) chooses denomination: PHP 500, PHP 1,000, PHP 2,000, PHP 5,000, or custom amount (min PHP 200, max PHP 50,000); (ii) selects design/theme: Birthday, Wedding, Housewarming, Congratulations, Holiday, Generic; (iii) enters recipient name, email address, and mobile number; (iv) writes personal message (max 200 characters); (v) selects delivery date: immediate or scheduled future date (up to 90 days ahead); (vi) pays via CC, GCash, Maya, or bank transfer; (b) system validates: purchaser's payment is successful, recipient email/mobile is valid format, total purchase amount is within anti-money laundering limits (single purchase ≤ PHP 50,000 per BSP guidelines) | Customer / System | Ecommerce Operations Manager | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **E-Gift Card Generation & Digital Delivery**: (a) system generates unique e-gift card: 16-digit card number, 4-digit PIN, QR code, and barcode — all cryptographically generated with checksum validation; (b) system creates personalized e-gift card image with selected design, recipient name, personal message, sender name, and amount; (c) system records liability: debit Cash/Receivable, credit Gift Card Liability (deferred revenue) at full face value; (d) delivery: (i) email with e-gift card image as attachment and embedded redemption instructions; (ii) SMS to recipient's mobile with shortened link to e-gift card; (iii) both include: card number, PIN, QR code, expiration date (24 months from purchase per BuildRight policy), terms and conditions, and customer service contact; (e) purchaser receives confirmation email with delivery status; (f) for scheduled delivery, system queues the email/SMS for the selected date | System | automated | < 1 min |
| 3 | **E-Gift Card Redemption at POS or Online**: (a) recipient uses e-gift card at any BuildRight store or online: (i) **In-Store**: customer presents e-gift card QR code on mobile screen at POS; cashier scans QR code; system validates: card number exists, PIN matches, card has remaining balance, card is not expired; balance displayed on cashier screen; customer enters PIN on pin pad for security; transaction amount deducted from card balance; (ii) **Online**: customer enters card number and PIN at checkout; system validates and applies available balance; remaining balance (if any) displayed after transaction; (b) system updates: (i) gift card liability reduced by transaction amount; (ii) revenue recognized at redemption (not at purchase, per revenue recognition per W487 PFRS 15); (iii) remaining balance tracked on card record; (c) partial redemption is allowed; remaining balance stays on the card for future use; (d) if transaction amount exceeds gift card balance, customer pays the difference via another tender type (split payment per multi-tender POS support) | Cashier / System / Customer | Store Manager / Ecommerce Operations Manager | 2–3 min additional at POS |
| 4 | **E-Gift Card Balance Inquiry & Re-Send**: (a) customer can check balance online (buildright.com.ph/giftcard-balance), via mobile app, or at any store POS; (b) if recipient did not receive the e-gift card email/SMS: purchaser can request re-send from order confirmation page or by contacting customer service (W258); system re-sends to the same or updated email/mobile; (c) e-gift card cannot be re-sent to a different recipient after original delivery (to prevent fraud) | System / Customer Service | automated | 1–2 min |
| 5 | **Monthly E-Gift Card Reconciliation & Liability Reporting**: (a) Finance performs monthly reconciliation: (i) total e-gift card liability (unredeemed balance); (ii) redemption rate by cohort (month of purchase vs. month of redemption); (iii) breakage estimate (expected unredeemed portion recognized as revenue per historical patterns); (iv) fraud incidents and chargebacks; (b) system auto-recognizes breakage revenue for cards older than 24 months (expired, unredeemed) per W939 (store credit expiration management); (c) report submitted to CFO for liability management and program health assessment | System / Finance | CFO | 2–3 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- E-commerce platform — e-gift card purchase flow, personalization
- Payment gateway (GCash, Maya, CC, bank transfer) — payment processing
- Gift card management module — unique card generation, balance tracking, redemption processing
- POS system (W5B) — QR code scanning, balance inquiry, split tender
- Email/SMS gateway — digital delivery
- GL module — gift card liability accounting, breakage revenue recognition
- Anti-fraud module — purchase velocity limits, suspicious transaction detection
- Customer service ticketing (W258) — re-send requests, balance inquiries

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Digital gift card fraud**: Stolen credit cards used to purchase e-gift cards, which are then resold or used for merchandise; velocity limits (max 3 purchases per day per purchaser) and 24-hour hold on first-time purchasers mitigate this
- **Email delivery failures**: Philippine email spam filters or incorrect email addresses can prevent delivery; SMS backup and re-send functionality address this
- **Mobile QR code scanning issues**: Cracked phone screens, low brightness, or expired mobile data can prevent successful QR code scanning at POS; manual card number + PIN entry is the fallback
- **Liability management**: Large unredeemed gift card balances represent a financial liability; breakage recognition must comply with PFRS 15 (W487)
- **AML compliance**: Per BSP regulations, gift card purchases may be subject to AML reporting (W961) if used for money laundering; PHP 50,000 single-purchase limit mitigates this


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters recipient name, email address, and mobile number" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-record of "E-Gift Card Generation & Digital Delivery" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-scan of "E-Gift Card Redemption at POS or Online" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Staffing Implication
- **Ecommerce Operations Manager**: Program oversight adds ~2–3 hours/week
- **Finance**: Monthly reconciliation adds 2–3 hours/month
- **Customer Service**: Re-send and balance inquiry requests add ~1–2 hours/week across the call center
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — system is highly automated

### Time Estimate
- Purchase: 3–5 min (customer self-service)
- Generation & delivery: < 1 min (automated)
- Redemption: 2–3 min additional at POS
- Monthly reconciliation: 2–3 hours/month (Finance)
- **Total per transaction**: 6–9 min (customer-facing); < 1 min system processing
