## W1114. Customer Product Availability Real-Time Stock Lookup & Reservation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquiry about product availability via mobile app, website, phone call, or in-store associate |
| **Frequency** | ~50,000–80,000 stock lookup requests/month across all channels |
| **Volume** | 1–5 SKUs per lookup |
| **Owner** | Ecommerce Operations Manager (digital channels); Customer Service Representative (phone/in-store) |
| **Participants** | Customer, Sales Associate, Customer Service Representative, E-commerce Platform |

### Background

BuildRight Depot carries 35,000 active SKUs across 200 stores and 4 DCs. Customers frequently need to know if a specific item is available at their preferred store before making a trip — especially for bulky items (cement, plywood, tiles) where a wasted trip is costly and frustrating. Current inventory visibility is near-real-time (< 30 seconds latency per POS-013), but customers need an easy way to check availability and optionally reserve items for pickup. Key use cases: (a) contractor checking if 200 bags of cement are available at the nearest store before dispatching a truck; (b) DIY homeowner confirming a specific faucet model is in stock before driving 30 minutes to the store; (c) online shopper seeing "Available at [Store Name] — Reserve for pickup" on the product page; (d) call center agent checking stock for a phone inquiry. This workflow manages the real-time stock lookup and optional reservation process, complementing W11 (BOPIS order fulfillment) which handles the full purchase-and-pickup cycle, and W930 (back-in-stock notification) which handles out-of-stock alerts. This workflow specifically covers the inquiry and reservation (hold) function without requiring immediate payment.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Real-Time Stock Inquiry**: (a) customer initiates stock lookup via: (i) BuildRight mobile app — search by SKU, barcode scan, or product name; app shows availability at all stores within selected radius (default: 20km); (ii) website — product page shows "Check availability at your store" with store selector dropdown; (iii) phone call to customer service (W259) — agent looks up SKU in system; (iv) in-store — Sales Associate checks via handheld RF device or store tablet; (b) system queries real-time inventory: for each store within range, displays: (i) current available-to-promise (ATP) quantity (on-hand minus reserved for existing orders); (ii) stock status indicator: ● In Stock (≥ display threshold), ○ Low Stock (1–3 units), ◌ Out of Stock; (iii) aisle/section location within the store (e.g., "Aisle 3, Bay 7 — Plumbing"); (iv) next expected replenishment date (from supply planning module); (c) for high-demand items with limited stock, system shows "X other customers are viewing this item at [Store]" to create urgency | System / Customer | automated | < 3 seconds |
| 2 | **Reservation Request & Stock Hold**: (a) if item is in stock, customer can choose to reserve: (i) select quantity and store; (ii) choose reservation duration: 2 hours (free), 4 hours (free), end of today (free), or until store closing tomorrow (PHP 50 reservation fee, refundable upon purchase); (iii) provide name and mobile number for notification; (b) system creates a stock reservation: (i) ATP quantity reduced by reserved amount (visible to other customers as "X reserved"); (ii) customer receives SMS confirmation with reservation ID, item, quantity, store name, and hold expiration time; (iii) store receiving desk and customer service counter receive reservation notification; (c) reservation limits per customer: max 5 reservations active at any time, max 10 units per SKU per reservation, max reservation value of PHP 100,000 | Customer / System | Ecommerce Operations Manager | 1–2 min |
| 3 | **Reservation Fulfillment & Expiry Management**: (a) customer arrives at store and presents reservation ID (SMS on phone) at customer service counter; (b) CSR verifies reservation: (i) reservation ID matches active reservation; (ii) customer name/ID matches; (iii) reservation has not expired; (c) CSR directs customer to the item's aisle location or retrieves from backroom if pre-staged; (d) customer proceeds to POS for purchase — reservation ID scanned, hold released, inventory deducted; (e) if customer does not arrive before expiration: (i) system automatically releases the reservation; (ii) ATP quantity restored; (iii) customer receives SMS notification: "Your reservation for [Item] at [Store] has expired. The item is now available to other customers."; (iv) if reservation fee was paid (PHP 50): fee forfeited and recorded as miscellaneous revenue | System / Customer Service Representative | Customer Service Representative | 3–5 min at pickup |
| 4 | **Stock Lookup Analytics & Demand Signal Capture**: (a) system captures all stock lookup events (whether reserved or not) as demand signals: (i) items frequently searched but out of stock → potential lost sales, flagged to Category Manager for assortment review (W679); (ii) stores frequently searched for items they don't carry → potential assortment gap; (iii) items with high lookup-to-reservation conversion but low reservation-to-purchase conversion → possible store-level fulfillment issues; (b) analytics dashboard updated daily for Category Managers and Supply Planners; (c) data feeds into demand forecasting (W31) as a leading indicator | System | Category Manager | automated |

### System Touchpoints
- Real-time inventory module — ATP calculation across all locations
- Mobile app — stock lookup, reservation request
- Website — product page availability check
- Customer service module (W259) — agent-assisted lookup
- RF handheld devices — in-store associate lookup
- Reservation management module — hold creation, tracking, expiry
- SMS gateway — reservation confirmation and expiry notifications
- POS system — reservation ID scanning, hold release
- Demand signal analytics — lost sale tracking, search-to-conversion funnels
- Supply planning module (W31) — demand signal feed

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Inventory accuracy dependency**: Stock lookups are only as good as inventory data; if physical stock doesn't match system stock (accuracy target ≥ 97%), customers will have a poor experience
- **Gaming by customers**: Customers may reserve items speculatively to "hold" stock while shopping around competitors; the 2–4 hour free hold window and PHP 50 next-day fee mitigate this
- **ATP calculation complexity**: ATP must account for: on-hand stock, existing reservations, pending BOPIS orders, pending transfer orders, and incoming replenishment — any calculation error leads to over-promising
- **Store associate awareness**: If store associates are not aware of reservations, they may sell reserved stock to walk-in customers before the reserving customer arrives; system must prevent this through POS-level ATP deduction
- **Mobile app performance in low-signal areas**: Philippine provinces may have poor mobile data coverage; app must cache recent lookups and show last-known availability with timestamp


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-scan of "scan, or product name" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "counter receive reservation notification" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-record of "recorded as miscellaneous revenue" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: stock (accuracy target ≥ 97%), customers will have a poor experience; operational: must prevent this through POS-level ATP deduction

### Staffing Implication
- **Customer Service Representative**: Reservation pickup handling adds 3–5 minutes per pickup; at ~10–15 pickups/store/week, this represents ~1 hour/week — minimal impact
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — the service is primarily digital and automated

### Time Estimate
- Stock lookup: < 3 seconds (automated)
- Reservation creation: 1–2 min (customer self-service)
- Pickup: 3–5 min (in-store)
- Expiry management: automated
- **Total per customer interaction**: 4–7 min (customer time); minimal staff time

---

## W1122. Customer Project Material Delivery Photo Documentation & Proof Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Delivery of project materials to a customer's construction site or home, especially for B2B/trade accounts and staged deliveries |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 project deliveries/month requiring photo documentation (primarily B2B/trade, high-value, and staged deliveries) |
| **Volume** | 5–15 photos per delivery |
| **Owner** | Delivery Driver / Logistics Coordinator |
| **Participants** | Delivery Driver, Customer (Site Representative), Logistics Coordinator, Trade Account Manager |

### Background

BuildRight Depot delivers materials to construction sites and homes for both B2C (home delivery per W19) and B2B (project deliveries per W164, W980) customers. Material deliveries to construction sites carry unique risks that standard delivery proof (signature on delivery receipt) does not adequately address: (a) **Dispute risk**: Customer claims not all items were delivered ("I ordered 500 bags of cement but only received 450") — without photographic evidence, BuildRight must absorb the shortage cost; (b) **Damage in transit**: Materials damaged during unloading at site (cement bags torn, tiles chipped, lumber scratched) — without before/after photos, it's unclear whether damage occurred during BuildRight's handling or the customer's; (c) **Staged delivery reconciliation**: For project accounts with 5–10 staged deliveries over months (W164), photographic proof of each stage provides an auditable trail for milestone billing (W165); (d) **Site condition documentation**: Photos of the delivery location (driveway accessibility, unloading area condition, existing structures) protect BuildRight from claims of property damage during delivery; (e) **Insurance claims**: If materials are damaged by weather or theft after delivery but before installation, delivery photos prove the materials were delivered in good condition, supporting the customer's insurance claim (W858). This workflow establishes a systematic photo documentation protocol for project material deliveries, complementing W811 (wholesale delivery proof & POD reconciliation) and W592 (ecommerce delivery tracking & POD management).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Pre-Delivery Preparation & Delivery Order Assignment**: (a) Logistics Coordinator assigns delivery to driver with delivery order details: (i) customer name, trade account number (if applicable); (ii) delivery address and site contact person/number; (iii) itemized material list with quantities; (iv) special delivery instructions (e.g., "Enter through gate on south side," "Unload inside garage," "Forklift delivery — ensure site has compacted surface"); (v) whether photo documentation is required: mandatory for all B2B/trade deliveries, B2C deliveries > PHP 20,000, and all staged project deliveries; (b) driver reviews delivery order on handheld tablet and plans route | Logistics Coordinator / Driver | Logistics Coordinator | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **On-Site Arrival & Condition Documentation**: (a) upon arrival at delivery site, driver photographs: (i) **Site approach photo**: truck at the delivery location, showing the site entrance and surrounding area (establishes delivery was made to the correct address); (ii) **Unloading area condition photo**: the area where materials will be placed, showing pre-existing conditions (existing damage, puddles, obstructions) — this protects BuildRight from claims of property damage; (iii) **Material on truck photo**: all materials visible on the truck before unloading, establishing the "before" condition of goods in BuildRight's custody; (b) photos are timestamped and GPS-tagged by the tablet camera (verifying location and time of arrival); (c) if site conditions are unfavorable (e.g., muddy, no access, no one to receive), driver contacts Logistics Coordinator for instructions before unloading | Driver | Logistics Coordinator | 5 min |
| 3 | **Unloading & Material Placement Documentation**: (a) driver unloads materials at the designated location; (b) during/after unloading, driver photographs: (i) **Material offloaded — grouped photo**: all materials placed at the delivery point, arranged so individual items/quantities are visible; (ii) **Close-up of material condition**: representative close-ups showing that cement bags are intact, tile boxes are sealed, lumber is undamaged, paint cans are unopened; (iii) **Quantity verification photo**: for counted items (bags, boxes, bundles), a photo showing the count — e.g., a row of 50 cement bags clearly showing the full count; (iv) **Special items photo**: any high-value or fragile items (power tools, glass, appliances) photographed individually with serial number visible if applicable; (v) **Placement photo**: materials in their final resting position, showing proximity to structures and weather protection (if covered/uncovered); (c) all photos automatically uploaded to cloud storage in real-time via driver's tablet (4G/LTE connection) | Driver | Logistics Coordinator | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **Customer Sign-Off & Digital POD**: (a) customer's site representative reviews the delivery: (i) verifies item count matches delivery order; (ii) inspects material condition; (b) if everything is correct: (i) site representative signs digital POD on driver's tablet (stylus or finger signature); (ii) system captures signature image, signer name, signer role (foreman, homeowner, site engineer), and timestamp; (iii) system generates digital delivery receipt with: item list, quantities, photos, signature, GPS coordinates, timestamp; (iv) receipt emailed to customer and Trade Account Manager; (v) receipt stored in document management (W255) linked to the delivery order and sales order; (c) if there are discrepancies: (i) driver photographs the discrepancy (e.g., 2 broken tile boxes); (ii) discrepancy noted on POD with quantity short/damaged; (iii) system triggers a shortage/damage investigation per W91 (damaged goods disposition) and W500 (transfer order in-transit damage claim); (iv) driver does not leave until discrepancy is documented and acknowledged by both parties | Driver / Customer Site Representative | Logistics Coordinator | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Post-Delivery Photo Archive & Analytics**: (a) all delivery photos archived in cloud storage: (i) organized by delivery order number, date, and customer account; (ii) retained for 10 years (BIR requirement for transaction documentation); (iii) accessible to: Logistics Coordinator (operations), Trade Account Manager (customer service), Finance (billing dispute resolution), Legal (liability claims); (b) monthly delivery documentation compliance report: (i) % of required deliveries with complete photo documentation (target: ≥ 98%); (ii) common photo quality issues (blurry, missing categories, no GPS tag); (iii) dispute resolution effectiveness: % of delivery disputes resolved using photo evidence vs. requiring further investigation; (iv) average photos per delivery and average file size (for storage planning); (c) photo analytics used for: (i) driver performance scoring (documentation completeness); (ii) site risk assessment (difficult access sites flagged for future delivery planning); (iii) damage trend analysis (specific routes or handling methods causing more damage) | System / Logistics Coordinator | Logistics Manager | 1–2 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Delivery order management module (W19) — order assignment, item list
- Driver handheld tablet — camera, GPS, timestamping
- Cloud photo storage — real-time upload, 10-year retention
- Digital POD module — signature capture, receipt generation
- Document management (W255) — delivery receipt archival
- Delivery tracking module (W592) — real-time delivery status
- Customer notification module — email delivery confirmation with photos
- Shortage/damage investigation module (W91, W500) — discrepancy handling
- Driver performance module — documentation compliance scoring
- BI analytics (W880) — compliance reporting

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Driver photo compliance**: Drivers may rush or skip photo documentation under time pressure; mandatory photo categories must be enforced by the tablet app (cannot complete delivery without all required photos)
- **Photo quality**: Poor lighting (early morning/late evening deliveries), rain (camera lens drops), or shaky hands produce unusable photos; photo quality check before submission is recommended
- **Storage cost**: 8,000–12,000 deliveries × 5–15 photos × 3–5 MB per photo = ~120–900 GB/month of photo storage; cloud storage cost must be budgeted (~PHP 50,000–150,000/month)
- **Customer privacy concerns**: Photographing a customer's home or construction site may raise privacy concerns; photo policy should be communicated in delivery terms and conditions, and photos should focus on materials, not residential interiors
- **Network connectivity**: Remote Philippine construction sites may have poor 4G/LTE signal; tablet must cache photos for upload when connectivity is restored


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "Enter through gate on south side" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "Logistics Coordinator for instructions before unloading" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-document of "Unloading & Material Placement Documentation" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Delivery Driver / Logistics Coordinator review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Delivery Driver**: Photo documentation adds 15–20 minutes per delivery; at 5–8 deliveries/day, this adds 1–2 hours to the driver's daily schedule — factored into delivery route planning
- **Logistics Coordinator**: Monthly compliance review adds 1–2 hours/month
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — photo documentation reduces dispute resolution time (estimated saving of 10–15 hours/month chain-wide in dispute investigation)

### Time Estimate
- Pre-delivery preparation: 10–15 min
- On-site arrival photos: 5 min
- Unloading & material photos: 10–15 min
- Customer sign-off: 5–10 min
- **Total additional time per delivery**: 20–30 min (beyond standard delivery time)

## W1126. Customer Bulk Cement & Sand Order Direct-to-Site Delivery Tracking

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer places bulk order for cement, sand, gravel, or aggregates requiring direct-to-construction-site delivery |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–10,000 bulk DSD orders/month across all stores (one register with W964: ~40–50/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 deliveries per order; ~50–500 bags of cement or 1–10 cubic meters of sand/gravel per delivery |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Receiving Clerk, Delivery Partner (3PL), Customer |

### Background

Cement, sand, gravel, and aggregates are fundamental construction materials that are bulky, heavy, and uneconomical to double-handle through BuildRight's DC network. These items are delivered direct-to-site (DSD) from vendor or local quarry to the customer's construction site, bypassing the DC network entirely — a significant portion of COGS value. Bulk DSD orders present unique tracking challenges: (a) deliveries often go to informal construction sites without standard addresses; (b) cement bags are counted manually at point of delivery; (c) sand and gravel are measured by truck volume (cubic meters) with potential for short delivery; (d) customer may not be present at delivery site to receive; (e) delivery timing is critical for construction schedules — delayed cement delivery can halt concrete pouring schedules; (f) proof of delivery documentation is essential for invoicing and dispute resolution. This workflow complements W18 (DSD receiving at store) by focusing on customer-facing direct-to-site delivery, and W980 (construction site delivery scheduling) by adding bulk material-specific tracking.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Bulk Order Intake & Delivery Scheduling**: (a) customer (typically contractor or project account) requests bulk cement/sand/gravel delivery; specifies: delivery address (or GPS coordinates for construction sites), delivery date/time window, quantity (bags of cement, cubic meters of sand/gravel), product specifications (cement brand/grade, sand type — washed, fine, coarse); (b) sales associate enters order in ERP as DSD sales order with delivery scheduling; (c) system validates customer credit limit (W888 credit hold check) for project/trade accounts; (d) delivery fee calculated based on distance from nearest source (vendor depot or quarry) and volume; (e) order confirmed with customer: delivery date, time window (4-hour), driver contact number, and delivery instructions | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Vendor/Querry Coordination & Dispatch**: (a) ERP sends purchase/delivery order to cement vendor or sand/gravel supplier; (b) for BuildRight-managed delivery: 3PL truck dispatched from vendor depot with delivery manifest; (c) for vendor-managed delivery: vendor dispatches directly with BuildRight tracking requirement; (d) system sends delivery confirmation SMS/email to customer with: driver name, plate number, estimated arrival time, delivery quantity, and site contact instructions; (e) GPS tracking activated for BuildRight-managed deliveries via fleet telematics (W199) | Supply Chain Coordinator (HQ) / 3PL | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **On-Site Delivery Verification**: (a) driver arrives at construction site, contacts customer's site representative; (b) for cement: site representative counts bags unloaded against delivery manifest; (c) for sand/gravel: volume verified by truck measurement (standard truck volumes: 1 cu.m., 3 cu.m., 6 cu.m., 10 cu.m.); (d) driver photographs delivery: loaded truck before unloading, unloading process, delivered material on-site, and customer's site representative; (e) site representative signs delivery receipt on driver's mobile device (digital POD); (f) if short delivery or quality issue: discrepancy noted on digital POD with photos; (g) GPS coordinates and timestamp auto-captured by delivery app | Delivery Driver / Customer Site Rep | Department Supervisor | 15–30 min |
| 4 | **Delivery Confirmation & Invoicing**: (a) delivery app syncs POD data to ERP in near-real-time; (b) system matches delivery quantity to sales order: full delivery → auto-invoice generated and sent to customer; partial delivery → system creates backorder for remaining quantity per W56; (c) for COD customers: payment collected by driver (cash) or via mobile payment (GCash) at delivery; (d) for trade/project accounts: invoice added to account with agreed payment terms; (e) delivery exception (shortage, damage, refusal) triggers customer service follow-up within 4 hours | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Monthly DSD Performance Review**: (a) monthly report generated: on-time delivery rate, delivery accuracy (quantity match rate), customer complaint rate, average delivery lead time by region; (b) vendor DSD performance reviewed per W44; (c) 3PL delivery partner performance reviewed per W242; (d) delivery fee accuracy reviewed and adjusted quarterly | Supply Chain Coordinator (HQ) | VP Supply Chain | 2–3 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP sales order module — DSD order creation with delivery scheduling
- Credit management module (W888) — credit limit check
- Fleet telematics / GPS tracking (W199) — real-time delivery tracking
- Mobile delivery app — digital POD capture with GPS and photos
- Invoice auto-generation module — delivery-triggered billing
- Backorder management (W56) — partial delivery processing
- 3PL performance module (W242) — delivery partner scoring
- Customer notification engine — SMS/email delivery status updates

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Informal site addresses**: Many Philippine construction sites lack formal addresses; GPS coordinates and landmark descriptions are essential
- **Volume measurement disputes**: Sand and gravel delivered by volume is inherently imprecise; customer may claim short delivery; photographic evidence is critical
- **Weather delays**: Heavy rain makes construction site roads impassable; delivery may need to be rescheduled with < 4 hours notice
- **Cash handling risk**: COD deliveries in remote areas with large cash amounts (PHP 50,000–200,000 for bulk orders) create security risk; mobile payment adoption should be encouraged; mitigated by the Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) review and approval gate
- **Site access**: Construction sites may have limited truck access (narrow roads, low overhead clearance, weight-restricted bridges); pre-delivery site assessment recommended for first-time delivery locations


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters order in ERP as DSD sales order with delivery scheduling" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-send of "sends purchase/delivery order to cement vendor or sand/gravel supplier" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "counts bags unloaded against delivery manifest" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: Order intake adds ~10–15 min per bulk order; at ~40–50 orders/store/month (per the Frequency), ~8–15 hours/month — absorbed by existing 12 sales associates
- **Department Supervisor**: Delivery exception handling adds ~2–3 hours/week during peak construction season
- **Supply Chain Coordinator (HQ)**: 1 coordinator manages DSD vendor coordination across all stores — existing role
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Order intake & scheduling: 10–15 min
- Vendor coordination & dispatch: 5–10 min
- On-site delivery verification: 15–30 min
- Confirmation & invoicing: 5–10 min
- **Total per order**: 35–65 min (store-side); delivery transit time varies by distance

## W1131. Customer Construction Project Milestone Photo Documentation & Progress Tracking Portal

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer enrolls project in BuildRight's project tracking portal, or project milestone delivery is scheduled |
| **Frequency** | ~500–800 active tracked projects at any time; ~2,000–3,000 milestone photo uploads/month |
| **Volume** | 3–8 photos per milestone; 5–10 milestones per project |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (Project Liaison) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Project Liaison), Customer, Contractor, Delivery Partner |

### Background

BuildRight Depot's project/trade customers (30% of revenue) and corporate accounts (10%) often manage multi-week or multi-month construction projects with phased material deliveries. Both the customer and BuildRight benefit from systematic photo documentation at each project milestone: (a) the customer gets a visual record of construction progress and material installation for their own records and for social media sharing (driving word-of-mouth referrals); (b) BuildRight gets verification that delivered materials were correctly used (reducing fraudulent damage claims), insights into project progress (triggering timely next-phase material orders), and marketing content (with customer consent — "Built with BuildRight" project showcase). This workflow establishes a digital project tracking portal where milestone photos are uploaded, linked to material orders, and used to trigger proactive sales follow-up. It complements W905 (project photo gallery & social proof platform), W793 (project close-out), and W918 (project budget tracking).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Enrollment & Portal Setup**: (a) when a trade/project customer places their first project order, Sales Associate offers enrollment in BuildRight's Project Tracker; (b) customer creates account on BuildRight mobile app or web portal; (c) project profile created: project name, location, scope (bathroom renovation, house construction, commercial fit-out), estimated timeline, assigned store, project liaison; (d) milestone schedule defined based on project scope: demolition, plumbing rough-in, electrical rough-in, masonry, tiling, painting, fixture installation, final walkthrough; (e) customer grants consent for "Built with BuildRight" photo usage in marketing (opt-in) | Sales Associate (Project Liaison) | Customer | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Milestone Photo Capture & Upload**: (a) at each material delivery or project milestone, designated party (customer, contractor, or BuildRight delivery driver) photographs progress: overall room/site photo showing work completed, close-up of installed BuildRight materials (tiles, paint, fixtures), before/after comparison at key milestones; (b) photos uploaded to BuildRight Project Tracker via mobile app with timestamp and GPS metadata; (c) system auto-tags photos with relevant project milestone based on delivery schedule; (d) customer can add notes and annotations to photos | Customer / Contractor / Delivery Driver | Customer | 5–10 min per milestone |
| 3 | **System-Triggered Next-Phase Recommendation**: (a) when milestone photos show completion of a phase (e.g., tiling complete), system triggers next-phase material recommendation: grout and sealant for tile phase, paint and primer for painting phase, fixtures and accessories for installation phase; (b) notification sent to customer via app: "Your tiling looks great! Next, you'll need grout and sealant. [View Recommended Products]"; (c) project liaison reviews notification and follows up with proactive call per W744; (d) system estimates remaining material needs based on project scope and completed milestones | System / Sales Associate | Sales Associate | Automated (5 min review) |
| 4 | **Project Completion & Content Curation**: (a) when customer marks project complete, system generates project summary: timeline, total material cost, before/after photos, products used; (b) customer invited to write a review per W510 and share project on social media with #BuiltWithBuildRight hashtag; (c) with customer consent, project featured on BuildRight's website gallery (W905) and social media; (d) project liaison sends completion congratulations and W914 incentive if applicable; (e) project record archived in customer's account for future reorder per W969 | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Customer mobile app / web portal — project tracker interface
- Photo storage service (cloud) — milestone photo repository
- ERP order module — material delivery schedule linked to milestones
- Recommendation engine (W200 AI-driven personalization) — next-phase product suggestions
- Customer notification engine — milestone alerts and recommendations
- Social media integration — #BuiltWithBuildRight content aggregation
- CRM module — project history and reorder capability

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer adoption**: Getting busy contractors to upload photos requires a compelling value proposition; potential incentives include loyalty points per upload, project completion discount, or free next-phase delivery
- **Photo quality**: User-submitted photos may be poor quality, poorly lit, or unrepresentative; system should accept all uploads and let marketing team curate
- **Privacy concerns**: Construction site photos may show identifiable people, neighboring properties, or sensitive information; consent and privacy review required before marketing use
- **Platform maintenance**: Project tracker requires ongoing IT support, mobile app updates, and cloud storage costs


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "account on BuildRight mobile app or web portal" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-schedule of "Milestone Photo Capture & Upload" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-review of "reviews notification and follows up with proactive call per W744" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: should accept all uploads and let marketing team curate; operational: before marketing use

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Project Liaison)**: Portal enrollment adds ~10 min per new project; milestone review adds ~5 min per milestone; at ~2–4 active projects per store at any time (~500–800 chain-wide per the Frequency) = ~15–30 min/week — absorbed by existing project liaison role
- **Marketing Team (HQ)**: 1 content curator manages gallery and social media from project photos — existing role
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project enrollment: 10–15 min
- Photo upload: 5–10 min per milestone
- Next-phase review: 5 min
- Project completion: 10–15 min
- **Total per project lifecycle**: 60–90 min (spread over weeks/months)

## W1136. Customer Product Knowledge Video Library & How-To Tutorial Content Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Content creation schedule (monthly), new product launch, seasonal topic (e.g., waterproofing before rainy season), or customer FAQ trend |
| **Frequency** | ~20–30 new videos/quarter; continuous content management |
| **Volume** | Library target: 200–300 videos within Year 1; ~50,000–100,000 monthly views |
| **Owner** | Marketing Content Manager (HQ) |
| **Participants** | Marketing Content Manager, Category Managers, Sales Associates (on-camera talent), IT Digital Team, Vendor Marketing Partners |

### Background

BuildRight Depot's customer base includes a large DIY segment (~60% of revenue) that often lacks technical knowledge for home improvement projects. Common barriers to purchase include: "I don't know how to install this," "I'm not sure which product is right for my project," and "What tools do I need?" A comprehensive video library addressing these knowledge gaps serves multiple purposes: (a) reduces purchase hesitation by building customer confidence; (b) decreases product returns caused by incorrect usage or wrong product selection; (c) drives website traffic and SEO rankings (complementing W563 SEO & digital merchandising); (d) provides shareable content for social media (complementing W142 social media management); (e) supports in-store sales associates who can show videos to customers on tablets during consultation; (f) differentiates BuildRight from competitors who don't provide educational content. Video content categories include: product selection guides, installation tutorials, tool usage basics, project planning guides, safety tips, and product comparison reviews.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Content Calendar & Topic Planning**: (a) Marketing Content Manager develops quarterly content calendar aligned with: seasonal relevance (waterproofing in May, aircon sizing in March, Christmas lights in October); new product launches (W564, W1128); customer FAQ trends from call center (W259) and in-store feedback; search analytics (most-searched product categories on buildright.com.ph); vendor co-marketing opportunities; (b) each video planned with: topic, target length (2–8 minutes), target audience (DIY beginner, experienced DIYer, professional), products featured, filming location (store, HQ studio, customer project site), vendor involvement (co-branded content) | Marketing Content Manager | CMO | 2–3 hours/quarter |
| 2 | **Video Production**: (a) filming: in-store product demonstrations, step-by-step installation tutorials, tool comparison reviews; (b) talent: Sales Associates with strong product knowledge and camera presence (identified during training sessions); Category Managers for expert commentary; vendor product specialists for co-branded content; (c) production quality: smartphone or professional camera, external microphone for clear audio, good lighting (ring lights for indoor, reflectors for outdoor), simple editing (titles, overlays, chapter markers); (d) branding: BuildRight logo watermark, intro/outro bumper, "Available at BuildRight Depot" call-to-action overlay; (e) average production time: 4–8 hours per video (including filming, editing, review, revision) | Marketing Content Manager / Videographer | Marketing Content Manager | 4–8 hours/video |
| 3 | **Content Review & Compliance**: (a) legal review: ensure no misleading claims, proper disclaimers ("Consult a licensed professional for structural/electrical/plumbing work"), trademark compliance; (b) product accuracy: Category Manager verifies product information, specifications, and pricing; (c) safety review: all tutorials involving power tools, chemicals, or structural work include safety warnings and PPE demonstrations; (d) DTI compliance: no deceptive advertising per Consumer Act RA 7394; (e) accessibility: Filipino and English subtitles/captions for all videos | Marketing Content Manager / Legal | CMO | 1–2 hours/video |
| 4 | **Content Publishing & Distribution**: (a) videos published on: BuildRight YouTube channel, buildright.com.ph video library, BuildRight mobile app (in product detail pages and dedicated "How-To" section), in-store tablets (for sales associate reference), Facebook and Instagram (short-form clips); (b) SEO optimization: keyword-rich titles, descriptions, and tags per W563; (c) product page integration: relevant videos embedded in product detail pages on ecommerce site; (d) notification: subscribers notified of new content via email and app push notification | Marketing Content Manager / IT Digital Team | CMO | 30–60 min/video |
| 5 | **Performance Tracking & Content Refresh**: (a) monthly analytics review: views, watch time, engagement rate (likes, comments, shares), click-through to product pages, attributable sales; (b) top-performing videos: promoted more heavily and used as templates for future content; (c) underperforming videos: assessed for topic relevance, thumbnail quality, title optimization; revised or retired; (d) outdated content: products discontinued, pricing changed, or techniques superseded — videos updated or removed; (e) customer feedback: video comments and in-store feedback used to identify new topic opportunities | Marketing Content Manager | CMO | 2–3 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Content management system (CMS) — video hosting and publishing
- Ecommerce platform — product page video embedding
- YouTube Analytics / Google Analytics — view tracking and engagement metrics
- CRM module — video engagement tracked in customer profile
- SEO module (W563) — search optimization
- Social media management platform (W142) — distribution
- Mobile app content management (W615) — app video delivery
- POS mobile tablet — in-store video reference for associates

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Production cost**: Professional-quality video production costs PHP 15,000–50,000 per video; 20–30 videos/quarter = PHP 300,000–1,500,000/quarter (PHP 1.2M–6M/year)
- **Talent availability**: Sales Associates are not professional presenters; on-camera coaching and multiple takes increase production time
- **Content shelf life**: Product changes, price updates, and seasonal relevance limit video lifespan; quarterly review needed
- **Vendor content control**: Co-branded videos with vendors require vendor approval, which can delay publishing
- **Language considerations**: Philippine market is bilingual (Filipino/English); producing content in both languages doubles production effort


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "Content Calendar & Topic Planning" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "logo watermark, intro/outro bumper" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-review of "Review & Compliance" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Marketing Content Manager review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Marketing Content Manager (HQ)**: Video content management adds ~15–20 hours/week to existing role — partially absorbed, may need 1 additional content specialist if library scales beyond 300 videos
- **Videographer (HQ)**: 1 dedicated videographer/editor for ongoing production — new hire or outsourced production partner
- **Sales Associates**: On-camera talent is voluntary and compensated with recognition points; ~2–4 hours/quarter per participating associate
- **Potential incremental headcount**: 1 Content Specialist if video library scales significantly

### Time Estimate
- Content planning: 2–3 hours/quarter
- Video production: 4–8 hours/video
- Review & compliance: 1–2 hours/video
- Publishing: 30–60 min/video
- Performance tracking: 2–3 hours/month
- **Total per video**: 6–12 hours end-to-end

---

## W1140. Customer Post-Construction Punch List Material Fulfillment & Callback Order

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (project account or contractor) identifies punch list items after construction walkthrough, or defect-liability period claim |
| **Frequency** | ~300–500 punch list orders/month across all stores |
| **Volume** | 5–20 line items per punch list order |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (Project Liaison) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Project Liaison), Customer, Department Supervisor, Vendor (if warranty claim) |

### Background

In construction, a "punch list" is a document prepared at project completion listing remaining work items, deficiencies, or corrections needed before final project acceptance. For BuildRight Depot's project/trade customers, punch list items often include: (a) replacement tiles for chipped or cracked pieces discovered after installation; (b) additional trim pieces, transition strips, or edge profiles not included in original order; (c) paint touch-up quantities for damaged walls; (d) replacement hardware (hinges, handles) that were defective or damaged during installation; (e) missing accessories (towel bars, shower caddies, switch plates) not ordered in initial project scope; (f) fixture replacements under warranty (W33). Punch list fulfillment is critical for customer satisfaction: contractors need these items quickly to close out the project and receive final payment from the project owner. BuildRight's ability to rapidly fulfill punch list orders differentiates it from competitors and drives repeat project business. This complements W793 (project close-out), W918 (project budget tracking), and W33 (warranty claim processing).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Punch List Receipt & Item Identification**: (a) customer (typically contractor or project manager) provides punch list: item description, quantity needed, location in project, urgency; (b) project liaison reviews punch list against original project order to identify: items already purchased from BuildRight (may be under warranty — route to W33), items not in original order (new sale), items needing exact batch/shade match (tiles — route to W421 batch/shade reconciliation); (c) for each punch list item, system checks: current stock availability at assigned store and nearby stores, exact product match (same SKU or approved substitute per W279), vendor lead time if out-of-stock | Sales Associate (Project Liaison) | Department Supervisor | 15–25 min |
| 2 | **Priority Order Processing**: (a) punch list order flagged as priority in ERP: expedited picking (if in-stock), priority replenishment from DC (24-hour delivery SLA), or emergency vendor order (W60); (b) for batch-critical items (tiles, paint): system verifies batch/shade match against original project order; if exact batch unavailable: customer notified of closest available shade and samples offered per W999; (c) for warranty items: separate warranty claim order created per W33 with no charge to customer (vendor bears cost if within warranty period); (d) delivery scheduled: site delivery per W980 or BOPIS pickup per W11 — customer's preference; (e) rush delivery option: same-day delivery via 3PL partner (Lalamove, Transportify) for urgent punch list items at subsidized delivery cost | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 3 | **Order Fulfillment & Delivery**: (a) if picking from store stock: Stock Associate picks items with extra care (punch list items are individually critical — no damaged pieces); (b) items verified by project liaison before dispatch: correct shade/batch, correct specifications, correct quantity; (c) delivery arranged per customer preference; (d) for items requiring vendor order: vendor PO issued with "Punch List Priority" flag, delivery tracked via W1132 logistics scorecard; (e) customer notified of expected delivery time and any partial shipment status | Stock Associate / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 20–30 min |
| 4 | **Punch List Close-Out & Project Completion**: (a) upon delivery confirmation, project liaison follows up with customer to verify satisfaction; (b) all punch list items crossed off; (c) if any items still outstanding: additional order placed immediately; (d) final project order value updated in ERP including punch list additions; (e) project marked "Complete — Punch List Cleared"; (f) retention release initiated per W1134 if applicable; (g) customer satisfaction survey sent per W65 | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- CRM / Project account module — original project order history
- ERP sales order module — punch list priority order creation
- Batch/shade matching module (W421) — tile batch reconciliation
- Inventory module — stock availability check across stores
- Emergency procurement module (W60) — vendor priority order
- Warranty claim module (W33) — warranty item processing
- Delivery scheduling module — site delivery or BOPIS pickup
- 3PL integration — same-day rush delivery
- Project close-out module (W793) — punch list clearance tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Exact match difficulty**: Tiles and paint from the original order may no longer be available in the exact shade/batch; substitutions require customer approval and may be visible after installation
- **Urgency pressure**: Contractors are often under time pressure to complete punch list before final payment milestone; BuildRight must deliver quickly or risk contractor sourcing elsewhere
- **Small order economics**: Punch list orders are typically small (5–20 items, PHP 2,000–20,000) but require disproportionate service effort; margin is lower but customer retention value is high
- **Warranty ambiguity**: Some punch list items may be caused by installation errors rather than product defects; determining warranty eligibility can be contentious


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "reviews punch list against original project order to identify" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-create of "created per W33 with no charge to customer" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-log of "Order Fulfillment & Delivery" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Sales Associate (Project Liaison) review and approval gate; operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Project Liaison)**: Punch list processing adds ~1–1.5 hours per order; at ~1.5–2.5 orders/store/month, ~2–4 hours/month — absorbed by existing project liaison role
- **Stock Associate**: Priority picking adds ~20–30 min per order — absorbed by existing team
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Punch list receipt & identification: 15–25 min
- Priority order processing: 10–15 min
- Fulfillment & delivery: 20–30 min
- Close-out: 10–15 min
- **Total per punch list**: 55–85 min

## W1143. Customer Construction Material Pre-Order Reservation & Price Lock Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests reservation of construction materials for a future project with guaranteed pricing, or customer presents an approved project quotation requiring material price lock |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 pre-order reservations/month across all stores (primarily B2B/project accounts) |
| **Volume** | 1 reservation per request; average PHP 80,000–500,000 per reservation |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Department Supervisor, Customer, Supply Chain Planning Team (HQ) |

### Background

Construction projects in the Philippines often span several months from planning to execution. During this period, material prices — particularly cement, steel rebar, lumber, and plywood — can fluctuate significantly due to commodity price movements, foreign exchange changes (for imported items), and seasonal demand spikes. For BuildRight's B2B/project customers (30% of revenue from trade accounts and 10% from corporate accounts), price certainty is critical for accurate project budgeting and bid submissions. This workflow provides a formal pre-order reservation service where customers can: (a) reserve specific quantities of construction materials for future delivery; (b) lock in pricing at current rates for a defined period (typically 30–90 days); (c) pay a deposit (10–25% of estimated order value) to secure the reservation; (d) call off materials in staged deliveries aligned with project milestones per W164. This service differentiates BuildRight from competitors who sell only at prevailing prices, increases customer commitment, and provides demand visibility for procurement planning. It complements W979 (B2B blanket purchase agreements) by offering a lighter-weight, deposit-based price lock for smaller or first-time project accounts, and W1159 (price escalation protection) which focuses on long-term contract-based forward pricing.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Reservation Request & Price Lock Assessment**: (a) customer presents project material list and requests price lock; (b) sales associate verifies customer account status (active, good credit standing, no overdue AR per W888); (c) associate enters material list into ERP reservation module with requested quantities and preferred delivery dates; (d) system checks current inventory and incoming supply for each item across relevant DCs; (e) system calculates total estimated order value at current prices; (f) associate reviews price lock eligibility: cement, rebar, lumber, plywood, roofing, pipes — eligible for 30–90 day lock; electrical, plumbing fixtures, tiles — eligible for 30–60 day lock; promotional and seasonal items — not eligible for price lock | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Deposit Collection & Reservation Confirmation**: (a) customer pays reservation deposit: 10% for trade accounts with credit history, 25% for new or cash accounts; (b) deposit collected via cash, card, or GCash at POS; (c) system creates formal reservation order with unique reservation number; (d) reserved quantities deducted from available-to-promise (ATP) inventory but not from physical stock; (e) price lock expiry date set (30/60/90 days based on material category and customer tier); (f) customer receives reservation confirmation via email/SMS: reserved items, quantities, locked prices, expiry date, deposit amount, and delivery scheduling instructions | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Reservation Monitoring & Expiry Management**: (a) system generates daily report of active reservations approaching expiry (7-day warning, 3-day warning, 1-day warning); (b) sales associate contacts customer for reservations within 7 days of expiry: confirm delivery schedule, request extension (one-time 15-day extension permitted with supervisor approval), or convert to order; (c) if customer confirms delivery: reservation converted to sales order with delivery scheduling per W1126; (d) if reservation expires without action: deposit forfeited per terms (deposit refundable minus 5% restocking fee if cancelled within first 7 days, non-refundable after); (e) expired reservation quantities released back to available inventory | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min/reservation |
| 4 | **Partial Delivery Call-Off & Balance Tracking**: (a) customer calls off partial delivery as project progresses; (b) each call-off creates a delivery order from reservation balance; (c) delivered quantities billed at locked price regardless of current market price; (d) deposit applied proportionally against delivered value (e.g., 10% deposit credited against each delivery invoice); (e) system tracks reservation balance: original quantity, delivered quantity, remaining quantity, remaining deposit; (f) if customer requests additional quantity beyond reservation: additional quantity priced at prevailing rate | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Reservation Closure & Deposit Reconciliation**: (a) when all reserved quantities delivered, or customer confirms remaining quantities no longer needed, reservation closed; (b) final invoice generated reflecting all deliveries at locked prices; (c) remaining deposit balance applied to final invoice or refunded within 7 business days; (d) system generates reservation performance summary: price saved vs. market (positive if prices rose during lock period, zero/negative if prices fell), fill rate (quantity delivered vs. reserved), delivery timeliness; (e) summary stored in customer account record for future quotation reference | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP reservation / pre-order module — reservation creation, price lock, ATP deduction
- POS module — deposit collection and receipt
- Customer account module — credit standing verification, reservation history
- Inventory ATP module — available quantity check, reservation allocation
- Pricing module — current price capture and lock, expiry date management
- Delivery scheduling module (W1126) — call-off delivery creation
- CRM module — customer communication for expiring reservations
- Financial module — deposit tracking, refund processing, invoice generation
- Analytics module — reservation performance, price lock value tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Price risk**: If commodity prices rise significantly during lock period, BuildRight absorbs the margin differential; deposit (10–25%) may not cover full price increase for large orders; mitigated by CTL-266 (controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1))
- **Inventory commitment**: Reserved quantities reduce ATP for other customers; over-reservation can lead to stockouts for walk-in customers
- **Expired reservation stock**: If large reservations expire, previously committed inventory may now be excess
- **Administrative overhead**: Tracking 1,500–2,500 active reservations with varying expiry dates requires automated system management
- **Dispute risk**: Customers may contest deposit forfeiture on expired reservations; clear terms and acknowledgment required; mitigated by CTL-266 (controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1))


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters material list into ERP reservation module with requested quantities and…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "accounts with credit history, 25% for new or cash accounts" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "generates daily report of active reservations approaching expiry" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: management

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: Reservation processing adds ~15–25 min per reservation; at ~8–12 reservations/store/month across 12 associates, ~1–2 hours/month per associate — absorbed by existing role
- **Department Supervisor**: Extension approvals and dispute resolution add ~2–3 hours/week — absorbed by existing role
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Reservation request & assessment: 10–15 min
- Deposit & confirmation: 5–10 min
- Monitoring (per reservation over lifetime): 2–3 min
- Call-off & balance tracking: 5–10 min per delivery
- Closure & reconciliation: 5 min
- **Total per reservation lifecycle**: 30–50 min (spread over 30–90 days)

## W1144. Store-Level Customer Hazardous Material Transport Regulation Advisory

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases hazardous materials (paint, solvents, adhesives, pesticides, LPG, chemicals) requiring transport, or customer inquires about transport regulations |
| **Frequency** | ~15,000–20,000 transactions/month involving hazmat items across all stores |
| **Volume** | 1–10 hazmat items per transaction |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate / Cashier |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer, Delivery Partner (if delivery selected) |

### Background

BuildRight Depot sells numerous products classified as hazardous materials under Philippine regulations: paints and solvents (flammable), adhesives and thinners (flammable, toxic), pesticides and herbicides (toxic, environmental hazard), LPG tanks (compressed gas), chlorine and pool chemicals (oxidizer, corrosive), muriatic acid and cement (corrosive, irritant), and construction adhesives (toxic, flammable). Philippine transportation regulations (DOTr Department Order, DENR DAO, and PPA/Marina rules for maritime transport) restrict or require special handling for transporting these materials, particularly: (a) private vehicles carrying certain quantities may violate transport regulations; (b) maritime shipping between islands has strict hazmat cargo restrictions — passengers cannot carry hazmat on roll-on-roll-off (RORO) ferries; (c) some chemicals require dangerous goods (DG) placarding for commercial transport; (d) certain materials cannot be co-loaded (e.g., oxidizers with flammables). Many BuildRight customers are unaware of these regulations and may inadvertently violate them by transporting hazmat in private cars, multicabs, or on ferries. This workflow provides point-of-sale advisory and transport guidance to ensure customer safety and regulatory compliance, reducing BuildRight's liability exposure. It complements W236 (hazmat storage at DC), W237 (hazmat safety training), W238 (spill response), and W5B.9 (age-restricted prompts) by addressing the transport leg of the hazmat lifecycle.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Hazmat Item Detection at POS**: (a) POS system scans item and identifies hazmat classification from item master flag (linked to SDS data per W698); (b) system displays hazmat transport advisory prompt with hazard class: flammable (Class 3), corrosive (Class 8), toxic (Class 6.1), compressed gas (Class 2), oxidizer (Class 5.1); (c) system checks total quantity of hazmat items in transaction against threshold limits for private vehicle transport: paint/thinners ≤ 60L per vehicle (per DOTr guidelines for personal use exemption), LPG ≤ 1 tank per private vehicle, pesticides ≤ 5L per private vehicle; (d) if any item exceeds private transport limit, system flags for delivery recommendation | System / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 15–30 sec |
| 2 | **Customer Transport Advisory & Safety Briefing**: (a) cashier informs customer of transport advisory: material classification, quantity restrictions, and safe transport recommendations; (b) key safety instructions provided verbally and printed on receipt: "Keep containers upright. Do not expose to direct sunlight. Ensure vehicle ventilation. Do not transport in enclosed passenger compartment."; (c) for island customers (especially Visayas/Mindanao): "Maritime regulation prohibits carrying flammable liquids, LPG, and toxic substances as passenger baggage on RORO ferries. These items must be declared as cargo and transported in the vehicle hold or via separate cargo booking."; (d) for items exceeding private transport limits: customer offered BuildRight delivery service (W5D) or 3PL hazmat-certified delivery (W19) with DG-compliant vehicle | Cashier | Cashier | 1–2 min |
| 3 | **Delivery Option Processing (If Required)**: (a) if customer selects delivery for hazmat items: delivery order created in ERP with DG classification flag; (b) system routes order to DG-certified delivery partner or BuildRight's own fleet (if hazmat-equipped); (c) delivery fee includes hazmat handling surcharge (10–15% premium); (d) delivery partner ensures proper DG placarding, segregation (incompatible materials separated), and documentation (dangerous goods declaration if applicable); (e) customer signs DG transport acknowledgment at delivery | Cashier / Delivery Partner | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Compliance Logging & Reporting**: (a) POS logs hazmat advisory delivery with transaction record: items sold, quantities, hazard classes, advisory type given, customer response (self-transport or delivery); (b) monthly compliance report generated: total hazmat transactions, delivery conversion rate, advisory completion rate; (c) data feeds into hazmat regulatory compliance reporting per DENR requirements (W433 SMR/CMR) and internal safety audit (W140); (d) recurring customers who repeatedly self-transport large hazmat quantities flagged for safety follow-up | System | Department Supervisor | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS module — hazmat item detection, advisory prompt, transport advisory printing
- Item master (W252) — hazmat classification flag, hazard class, transport threshold quantities
- SDS management module (W698) — safety data sheet data linked to item master
- Delivery module (W5D, W19) — hazmat delivery order creation with DG flag
- Fleet management (W199) — DG-certified vehicle assignment
- Compliance reporting module — hazmat transaction logging, advisory completion tracking
- Receipt printer — transport advisory text on customer receipt

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer resistance**: Customers may view advisory as an inconvenience or upsell tactic; clear safety-first framing is essential
- **Cashier knowledge**: Cashiers need basic hazmat transport knowledge to answer customer questions; training required (W518)
- **Liability**: Even with advisory, BuildRight may face liability if a customer has an accident while transporting hazmat purchased from the store; signed acknowledgment recommended
- **RORO enforcement gaps**: Philippine maritime enforcement of hazmat transport on RORO ferries is inconsistent; customers may disregard advisory
- **Delivery cost**: Hazmat-certified delivery is expensive; customers in remote areas may have no alternative to self-transport


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-scan of "scans item and identifies hazmat classification from item master flag" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "printed on receipt" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-document of "Delivery Option Processing" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: (W518)

### Staffing Implication
- **Cashier**: Advisory adds ~1–2 min per hazmat transaction; at ~75–100 hazmat transactions/store/month across 3 cashiers (per the Frequency), ~6–8 min/month per cashier — absorbed by existing role
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Hazmat detection: 15–30 sec (automated)
- Advisory & safety briefing: 1–2 min
- Delivery processing (if needed): 3–5 min
- **Total per transaction**: 1.5–8 min (depending on delivery need)
