## W1146. Store-Level Customer Cement & Bagged Material Shelf Life Monitoring & Freshness Guarantee

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily stock check identifies cement or bagged material approaching shelf life limit, or customer concern about material freshness, or receiving inspection flags age concern |
| **Frequency** | ~6,000 shelf-life checks/month across all stores (daily for cement at each store, weekly for other bagged materials) |
| **Volume** | 35,000 active SKUs; ~200–300 cement/bagged material SKUs per store requiring shelf life monitoring |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Stock Associate, Department Supervisor, Receiving Clerk, Vendor (for returns) |

### Background

Cement and other bagged construction materials have finite shelf lives that directly affect product performance: (a) Portland cement has a shelf life of approximately 3 months when stored in dry conditions, but degrades faster in humid Philippine tropical weather (especially during rainy season); (b) bagged mortar mix, thin-set adhesive, and grout have shelf lives of 6–12 months from manufacture date; (c) expired or moisture-exposed cement loses compressive strength, leading to structural failures in customer construction projects; (d) chemical-based products (waterproofing, sealants, adhesives) degrade after expiry, causing application failures. BuildRight's reputation as a trusted building materials supplier depends on ensuring customers receive fresh, performance-guaranteed materials. Selling expired or degraded cement can result in: customer project failures, liability claims, product returns, and brand damage. Philippine construction industry standards (DPWH specifications) require fresh cement for structural applications. This workflow monitors shelf life of bagged materials at store level, ensures FEFO (first-expired-first-out) rotation, and provides customers with freshness verification. It complements W1005 (shelf stock rotation for paint/chemicals), W4.5 (FEFO picking at DC), and W3.4 (shelf-life capture at receiving).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Receiving Date Capture & Shelf Life Assignment**: (a) when cement or bagged materials are received at store (from DC per W4 or DSD per W18), receiving clerk scans batch/lot number and manufacture date from packaging; (b) system calculates expiry date: cement — 90 days from manufacture date (reduced to 75 days during June–November rainy season); mortar/grout mix — 12 months from manufacture; waterproofing compounds — 18 months from manufacture; (c) expiry date assigned in inventory record; (d) system alerts if received batch already exceeds 50% of shelf life (e.g., cement received at 50+ days old) — flags for priority shelving and early sale | Receiving Clerk / System | Department Supervisor | 30–60 sec/pallet |
| 2 | **Daily Shelf Life Monitoring & FEFO Rotation**: (a) each morning, stock associate performs shelf life spot-check on cement and bagged materials display; (b) system-generated daily alert lists items within 14 days of expiry; (c) stock associate physically rotates stock using FEFO: oldest stock pulled to front, newest stock placed behind; (d) for cement: check for hardened lumps, torn bags, water stains — remove and quarantine any compromised bags; (e) physical rotation confirmed in system with scan of batch barcode on oldest stock | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 15–20 min/day |
| 3 | **Customer Freshness Guarantee at POS**: (a) when customer purchases cement or age-sensitive materials, cashier scans batch barcode at checkout; (b) system displays manufacture date and remaining shelf life; (c) if batch is within 30 days of expiry for cement, or 60 days for other bagged materials: POS prompts cashier to offer freshest batch; (d) if fresher batch available in backroom, stock associate retrieves it; (e) receipt prints manufacture date and "Freshness Guaranteed" statement with batch number; (f) for large cement orders (50+ bags): customer may request freshness verification certificate for project documentation | Cashier / Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 30–60 sec/transaction |
| 4 | **Near-Expiry Markdown & Disposal**: (a) items reaching 14 days before expiry automatically flagged for markdown: 20% discount at 14 days, 40% at 7 days; (b) markdown applied in POS system with "Near-Expiry — Use Immediately" shelf label; (c) customer informed of reduced shelf life and advised to use within stated period; (d) items past expiry removed from shelf, quarantined, and dispositioned: return to vendor if return agreement exists per W88 (RTV); dispose via authorized construction waste hauler if non-returnable; (e) disposal recorded in system with waste code and documentation for DENR compliance (W82) | Stock Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min/batch |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP inventory module — batch/lot tracking, shelf life assignment, expiry alerting
- POS module — batch scan at checkout, freshness date display, near-expiry markdown
- Receiving module (W109) — manufacture date capture at goods receipt
- FEFO rotation module — stock rotation prioritization
- Markdown module (W93) — near-expiry pricing automation
- RTV module (W88) — vendor return for expired stock
- Waste disposal module (W82, W502) — authorized disposal documentation
- Analytics module — shelf life performance, waste rates, freshness compliance

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Climate impact**: Philippine humidity and rainy season accelerate cement degradation; shelf life assumptions must account for actual storage conditions
- **Customer awareness**: Most customers don't check cement manufacture dates; relying on BuildRight's freshness guarantee builds trust but creates liability
- **Waste cost**: Expired cement represents full cost write-off; estimated 2–3% waste rate on cement = ~PHP 25M–40M/year across chain
- **Vendor cooperation**: Not all cement vendors accept returns of aged stock; negotiation during vendor onboarding (W36) critical
- **Physical handling**: Rotating 40kg cement bags (per W964) is physically demanding; ergonomic training required for stock associates


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-scan of "scans batch/lot number and manufacture date from packaging" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "check on cement and bagged materials display" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "prints manufacture date" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: for stock associates

### Staffing Implication
- **Stock Associate**: Daily shelf life rotation adds ~15–20 min/day for 4 stock associates = ~1–1.3 hours/day total — absorbed by existing role
- **Receiving Clerk**: Date capture adds ~30–60 sec per pallet — absorbed by existing role
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Receiving date capture: 30–60 sec/pallet
- Daily monitoring & rotation: 15–20 min/store/day
- POS freshness check: 30–60 sec/transaction
- Near-expiry markdown: 5–10 min/batch
- **Total per store per month**: ~8–10 hours

---

## W1148. Store-Level Customer Construction Site Vehicle Access Assessment & Delivery Feasibility

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer orders delivery of construction materials to a construction site address, or customer requests delivery feasibility check before placing order |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,000 delivery feasibility assessments/month across all stores |
| **Volume** | 1 assessment per delivery address |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate / Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer, Delivery Partner, Store Receiving Clerk (for feedback loop) |

### Background

Philippine construction sites frequently present challenging delivery access conditions: (a) narrow residential streets in urban areas that cannot accommodate 10-wheeler delivery trucks; (b) low-hanging utility wires, overhead pedestrian bridges, or tree branches that restrict vehicle height; (c) weight-restricted bridges on rural roads leading to construction sites; (d) informal or temporary access roads that become impassable during rainy season; (e) construction sites in gated subdivisions with delivery truck restrictions or limited operating hours; (f) sites on hillsides or elevated areas requiring steep access roads; (g) island and coastal sites accessible only by small boats (not trucks). When a delivery truck arrives at a site it cannot access, the result is a failed delivery: wasted fuel and driver time, delayed construction schedule, customer frustration, and potential return-to-DC logistics cost. This workflow proactively assesses delivery feasibility before dispatch, ensuring the right vehicle is assigned to each delivery. It complements W1126 (bulk cement DSD delivery tracking), W980 (construction site delivery scheduling), and W1031 (delivery service area geo-fencing) by adding the physical access assessment dimension.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Delivery Address Capture & Preliminary Assessment**: (a) customer provides delivery address (or GPS coordinates for construction sites without formal addresses); (b) sales associate enters address into ERP delivery module; (c) system checks against delivery address database: previously delivered addresses show access notes (e.g., "10-wheeler accessible," "6-wheeler only," "hand-carry last 50m"); (d) for new addresses: system performs geo-fencing check (W1031) to confirm address is within delivery coverage area; (e) associate asks customer key access questions: "Can a 10-wheeler truck access the site directly?", "Are there height restrictions (overhead wires, gates)?", "Is the road paved or unpaved?", "Any weight-restricted bridges on the route?", "Is the site accessible during rainy weather?" | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Vehicle Type Matching & Route Assessment**: (a) based on assessment, system recommends vehicle type: 10-wheeler wing van (standard — for sites with full access), 6-wheeler truck (for narrow streets — reduced payload ~60%), forward/multi-cab (for very tight access — reduced payload ~25%), or motorcycle + sidecar (for extremely tight access — bags of cement, small items only); (b) if customer's order exceeds recommended vehicle capacity: order split into multiple trips, or staged delivery scheduled per W974; (c) for remote/island sites: system identifies need for inter-island ferry (W66) or boat transfer, with additional logistics cost and lead time; (d) delivery fee recalculated based on vehicle type and route complexity; (e) customer informed of delivery method, vehicle type, and any access limitations | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min |
| 3 | **Delivery Execution with Access Contingency**: (a) delivery dispatched with correct vehicle type and driver briefed on access conditions; (b) driver confirms arrival and access assessment upon reaching site area — if access is worse than expected: contacts store for guidance; (c) contingency options: transfer goods to smaller vehicle (if pre-arranged), hand-carry materials from nearest accessible point (labor surcharge applies), or reschedule with appropriate vehicle; (d) for sites requiring manual carry (common in Philippine urban residential areas): driver and helper carry materials from truck to site, with carry distance and labor time documented; (e) delivery confirmation and POD captured per W1126 | Delivery Driver / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | Variable (delivery transit + access time) |
| 4 | **Post-Delivery Access Record Update**: (a) after delivery, driver submits access assessment feedback via mobile app: actual vehicle used, access conditions encountered, any problems, recommended vehicle for future deliveries; (b) feedback updates delivery address database in system for future reference; (c) if access was worse than assessed: root cause reviewed (was customer's information inaccurate? did conditions change? was assessment insufficient?); (d) systematic access issues (e.g., a subdivision regularly denying truck entry) escalated to supply chain team for route optimization and carrier selection adjustment | Delivery Driver / System | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP delivery module — address capture, vehicle recommendation, delivery scheduling
- Delivery address database — historical access notes, vehicle recommendations per address
- Geo-fencing module (W1031) — coverage area verification
- Fleet management module (W52, W199) — vehicle type availability, driver assignment
- Delivery partner API (W242) — 3PL vehicle selection and dispatch
- Mobile delivery app — driver access feedback, GPS coordinates
- Delivery fee calculator — vehicle-based pricing, distance surcharge, manual carry surcharge
- Analytics module — failed delivery rate by access issue, vehicle utilization, cost per delivery by vehicle type

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Inaccurate customer information**: Customers may not know exact access conditions or may underestimate difficulties; verification photos (if customer can provide) are helpful
- **Weather-dependent access**: Unpaved roads passable in dry season may be impassable after rain; delivery scheduling must account for weather forecasts
- **Cost of wrong vehicle**: Sending a 10-wheeler to a site that can only accept a 6-wheeler wastes truck capacity and requires re-dispatch; conservative vehicle selection when in doubt
- **Island logistics complexity**: Inter-island deliveries involving ferries add 1–3 days lead time and significant cost; customer expectation management critical
- **Manual carry labor**: Carrying 40kg cement bags (per W964) or large sheets of plywood is physically demanding and time-consuming; labor costs and safety risks must be managed


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters address into ERP delivery module" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "logistics cost and lead time" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-document of "Delivery Execution with Access Contingency" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Sales Associate / Department Supervisor review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: Access assessment adds ~3–5 min per delivery order — absorbed by existing role
- **Delivery Driver**: Access feedback adds ~2–3 min per delivery — absorbed by existing role
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Address capture & assessment: 3–5 min
- Vehicle matching & route assessment: 2–3 min
- Post-delivery feedback: 2–3 min
- **Total per delivery (store-side)**: 7–11 min

---

## W1150. Store-Level Customer Lumber & Plywood Moisture Content Testing & Quality Verification

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests moisture content verification for lumber or plywood purchase, or receiving inspection identifies potential moisture damage, or routine quality sampling |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 moisture tests/month across all stores (higher during rainy season) |
| **Volume** | 1–10 boards/sheets per test |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Lumber Yard), Customer |

### Background

Lumber and plywood are among BuildRight's highest-value product categories (14% of active SKUs, ~4,900 SKUs), and their quality is directly affected by moisture content. In the Philippines' tropical climate with 70–90% ambient humidity and distinct rainy seasons: (a) lumber stored outdoors or in poorly ventilated areas absorbs moisture, leading to warping, swelling, mold growth, and reduced structural strength; (b) plywood exposed to rain delaminates as adhesive bonds weaken; (c) kiln-dried lumber (target moisture content: 12–18%) reabsorbs moisture if stored improperly; (d) construction projects using wet lumber experience nail pops, joint separation, and surface defects as wood dries and shrinks in place. Professional contractors and quality-conscious customers increasingly demand moisture content verification before purchasing lumber, especially for: structural framing (floor joists, roof trusses), cabinetry and furniture-grade wood, and marine/coastal construction. This workflow provides in-store moisture content testing using pin-type moisture meters, building customer confidence and differentiating BuildRight from competitors who sell lumber without quality verification. It complements W976 (lumber grade selection), W1139 (lumber yard seasonal rotation), and W1103 (lumber yard rain protection).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Moisture Test Request**: (a) customer selects lumber or plywood from yard display; (b) customer requests moisture content test, or sales associate proactively offers test for structural-grade lumber; (c) sales associate retrieves pin-type moisture meter from tool station; (d) selects appropriate test setting on meter: softwood, hardwood, or plywood mode | Sales Associate | Sales Associate | 1–2 min |
| 2 | **Moisture Content Measurement**: (a) sales associate inserts meter pins into wood surface at 2–3 points along the board length (middle and both ends); (b) meter displays moisture content percentage; (c) associate records readings in mobile ERP app linked to the specific board/batch; (d) moisture content interpreted against standards: kiln-dried lumber: target 12–18% (acceptable), 18–22% (borderline — advise customer to acclimate before use), >22% (too wet — do not sell as kiln-dried, offer as air-dried at reduced price); plywood: target ≤14% (acceptable), 14–18% (borderline), >18% (potential delamination risk — do not sell for structural use); (e) associate communicates results to customer with explanation of implications | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Quality Grading & Pricing Adjustment**: (a) if moisture content is within acceptable range: board sold at standard kiln-dried price with moisture reading noted on sales receipt; (b) if moisture content is borderline: customer informed and given option to purchase at standard price with advisory to acclimate wood before installation, or select a different board; (c) if moisture content is excessive: board reclassified from "kiln-dried" to "air-dried/green" in system with corresponding markdown (10–20% discount per W93); (d) if board shows visible defects (warping, mold, delamination): quarantined and dispositioned per W91 (damaged goods) | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min |
| 4 | **Batch-Level Quality Tracking**: (a) moisture readings aggregated by supplier batch/lot for quality tracking; (b) batches with consistently high moisture content (>25% of readings above threshold) flagged for vendor feedback per W110 (supplier quality CAPA); (c) seasonal moisture trend analysis: during rainy season (June–November), expect higher moisture readings → adjust storage practices (cover lumber stacks, improve ventilation); (d) monthly lumber quality report generated: average moisture by species and grade, moisture-related markdown cost, vendor quality scores | System / Department Supervisor | Store Manager | Automated + 30 min/month review |

### System Touchpoints
- Mobile ERP app — moisture reading capture linked to board/batch
- Item master module — lumber species, grade, and target moisture range
- Pricing/markdown module — moisture-based reclassification pricing
- Inventory module — batch/lot quality tracking
- Vendor quality module (W110) — batch-level quality feedback
- Analytics module — moisture trend analysis, seasonal patterns, vendor performance

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Meter accuracy**: Pin-type moisture meters can vary ±2–3%; calibration checks required weekly
- **Surface vs. core moisture**: Pins measure surface moisture which may differ from core moisture in thick lumber; deep-pin meters needed for beams and posts
- **Customer education**: Many customers don't understand moisture content implications; sales associate must explain in practical terms ("This wood will shrink and your nails will pop")
- **Testing throughput**: Testing 10 boards for a large contractor adds 10–15 minutes; scheduling during low-traffic periods preferred
- **Moisture meter maintenance**: Pins bend and break; regular replacement needed (~PHP 500/pin set)


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-request of "Customer Moisture Test Request" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-record of "records readings in mobile ERP app linked to the specific board/batch" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "discount per W93" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: weekly

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: Testing adds 3–5 min per request; at ~8–12 tests/store/month across lumber yard associates (per the Frequency), ~30–60 min/month total — absorbed by existing 4 stock associates
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — moisture testing service estimated to increase lumber sales by 5–8% through improved customer confidence

### Time Estimate
- Request & setup: 1–2 min
- Measurement: 3–5 min
- Grading & pricing: 2–3 min
- **Total per test**: 6–10 min

## W1159. Customer Construction Material Price Escalation Protection & Forward Pricing Agreement

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Corporate/project customer requests fixed pricing for a multi-month construction project, or bid/tender submission requires price commitment for project duration |
| **Frequency** | ~100–200 forward pricing agreements/year (large corporate/project accounts) |
| **Volume** | 20–200 SKUs per agreement; agreement value PHP 500,000–50,000,000 |
| **Owner** | VP for Merchandising |
| **Participants** | Category Manager, VP Merchandising, Finance Manager, Customer (Corporate Purchasing), Head of Trade / Account Management |

### Background

Large construction projects in the Philippines typically span 6–24 months from groundbreaking to completion. During this period, material prices can escalate significantly: (a) cement prices increased 15–25% during 2022–2023 supply chain disruptions; (b) steel rebar prices follow global commodity indices with 10–30% annual volatility; (c) imported tiles and fixtures are subject to foreign exchange fluctuations (PHP/USD moved from 48 to 56 during 2022); (d) lumber prices are affected by both global supply chains and domestic typhoon-related demand spikes. For corporate/project customers (10% of revenue) bidding on construction projects, price uncertainty is a critical risk: if they quote a project price based on today's material costs and prices rise 15% during construction, their profit margin evaporates. Competitors who offer forward pricing agreements win these accounts. This workflow provides BuildRight's most strategic customers with formal forward pricing agreements (FPAs): contractual commitments to supply specified materials at fixed (or capped-escalation) prices for the project duration. FPAs are distinct from W1143 (pre-order reservation — deposit-based, shorter duration) by being formal contracts with legal commitments, covering larger value, and spanning longer durations. They complement W163 (contract pricing), W165 (project retention billing), and W979 (blanket purchase agreements) by adding the price escalation protection dimension.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Forward Pricing Request & Material Scope Definition**: (a) customer (developer, government agency, or large contractor) submits forward pricing request with project details: project name and location, estimated project duration (months), material list with estimated quantities per month, current market pricing reference, required delivery schedule and locations; (b) Category Manager reviews material scope: which items are eligible for forward pricing (cement, rebar, lumber, plywood, tiles, pipes — commodities with volatile pricing), which are excluded (specialty items, seasonal items, items with less than 10% annual price volatility); (c) preliminary pricing assessment: current BuildRight cost, current market price, historical 12-month price trend, projected price risk (using commodity indices and FX forecasts) | Category Manager | VP Merchandising | 1–2 days |
| 2 | **Pricing Risk Assessment & Margin Modeling**: (a) finance manager performs pricing risk analysis: for each eligible item, calculate maximum expected price increase over project duration using Monte Carlo simulation with inputs from commodity price indices (cement: Philippine Cement Manufacturers Association index, steel: London Metal Exchange, FX: BSP historical volatility); (b) margin modeling: at proposed fixed price, what is BuildRight's expected margin if (a) prices remain flat, (b) prices increase by expected amount, (c) prices increase by 2× expected amount (stress scenario); (c) risk mitigation options: BuildRight hedges commodity exposure through forward purchase contracts with own vendors; price escalation cap (e.g., fixed for first 6 months, then capped at 5% increase per quarter); material substitution clause (if specific brand unavailable, equivalent quality substitute at same price); (d) minimum volume commitment required from customer to justify price lock (typically 80% of estimated quantities) | Finance Manager / Category Manager | CFO | 2–3 days |
| 3 | **Forward Pricing Agreement Negotiation & Execution**: (a) Category Manager and VP Merchandising present pricing proposal to customer: fixed price per item for agreement duration, minimum volume commitment, delivery terms and locations, escalation cap (if applicable), material substitution clause, early termination terms, force majeure clause (typhoon, supply chain disruption); (b) negotiation on key terms: customer wants lowest possible fixed price, BuildRight needs margin protection; (c) agreement executed with legal review (W230) and signed by both parties; (d) FPA loaded into ERP: customer account linked to agreement, eligible items with agreed pricing, minimum volume tracking, expiry date, escalation schedule (if any); (e) customer's trade account updated: FPA pricing automatically applied to qualifying purchases | VP Merchandising / Category Manager | CEO (for agreements > PHP 10M) | 1–2 weeks |
| 4 | **Ongoing Monitoring & Margin Tracking**: (a) monthly FPA margin report: actual cost vs. FPA price per item, cumulative margin impact, volume consumed vs. minimum commitment; (b) if actual costs exceed FPA price (negative margin): margin shortfall tracked and reported to CFO; (c) if customer purchasing below minimum volume commitment: account manager contacts customer for volume catch-up plan; (d) if commodity prices spike >20% above FPA price: force majeure review and potential renegotiation per agreement terms; (e) at agreement expiry: actual margin performance compared to risk model; lessons learned feed into next FPA risk assessment | Finance Manager / Category Manager | CFO | 2–3 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP pricing module — FPA pricing loaded per customer, automatic application at POS/order
- Commodity price tracking module (W760) — cement, steel, FX indices
- Financial risk modeling module — Monte Carlo simulation, margin stress testing
- Customer account module — FPA linkage, volume tracking
- Contract management module (W402) — FPA document storage, expiry tracking
- AP module — vendor forward purchase cost tracking
- Analytics module — FPA margin performance, risk model accuracy, cumulative P&L impact

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Margin erosion**: If commodity prices spike beyond risk model projections, BuildRight absorbs significant margin loss; FPAs must include adequate margin buffer or escalation caps
- **Volume commitment enforcement**: Customers may purchase less than committed (project delays, scope changes); minimum volume enforcement is difficult in practice
- **Administrative complexity**: Each FPA is a custom contract with unique terms; system must handle multiple active FPAs with different pricing, durations, and terms simultaneously
- **Competitive pressure**: If competitors offer lower forward pricing, customer may split purchases; relationship management critical
- **Force majeure disputes**: Defining what constitutes a force majeure event (typhoon clearly qualifies; a 20% price increase is debatable) requires precise contract language


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "reviews material scope" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "calculate maximum expected price increase over project duration using Monte…" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "account linked to agreement, eligible items with agreed pricing, minimum volume…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: must handle multiple active FPAs with different pricing, durations, and terms simultaneously

### Staffing Implication
- **Category Manager**: FPA management adds ~2,400–7,200 monitoring hours/year across ~100–200 agreements (~2–3 hours/month each) plus ~20–40 hours per new-FPA setup — absorbed by existing 5 category managers
- **Finance Manager**: Risk assessment and monitoring adds ~30–40 hours/year — absorbed by existing FP&A team
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Request & scope definition: 1–2 days
- Risk assessment & modeling: 2–3 days
- Negotiation & execution: 1–2 weeks
- Ongoing monitoring: 2–3 hours/month per active FPA
- **Total per FPA**: 20–40 hours (spread over agreement duration)

## W1160. Store-Level DC-to-Store Delivery Inter-Island Ferry Scheduling & Coordination

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | DC replenishment order for a store requiring inter-island ferry crossing, or weekly ferry schedule review, or ferry service disruption alert |
| **Frequency** | ~5–10 inter-island ferry shipments/week across the chain (one register with W1164's ~30–40 inter-island shipments/month) (stores on islands without bridge access from DC) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 trucks per ferry crossing per store |
| **Owner** | Supply Chain Logistics Coordinator (HQ) |
| **Participants** | Logistics Coordinator, DC Dispatch Team, Truck Driver, Ferry Operator, Store Receiving Clerk |

### Background

BuildRight's 200 stores span the Philippine archipelago across 4 DC regions, and many stores require inter-island ferry crossings for DC-to-store replenishment: (a) Mindanao Hub (DC1, Davao) → stores in Visayas require ferry crossing; (b) Visayas Hub (DC2, Cebu) → stores on other Visayan islands (Negros, Bohol, Leyte, Samar) require ferry; (c) South Luzon Hub (DC3, Laguna) → some Visayas-bound stores may route through Matnog port; (d) North/Central Luzon Hub (DC4, Clark) → primarily land-based but some Batanes/Babuyan island stores require maritime. Philippine inter-island ferry operations present unique challenges: (a) limited sailing schedules — some routes have only 1–2 sailings per day; (b) frequent schedule disruptions due to weather (typhoon signals cancel sailings), mechanical breakdowns, and port congestion; (c) RORO (roll-on-roll-off) vessel capacity constraints during peak periods (holidays, post-typhoon resupply); (d) varying ferry operators per route (Philippine Coast Guard-licensed operators, not standardized); (e)hazmat restrictions on passenger ferries per W1144 (cement, paint, chemicals may require cargo-only vessel); (f) documentation requirements (manifest, booking confirmation, cargo declaration). This workflow manages the inter-island ferry scheduling, booking, and coordination for DC-to-store deliveries. It complements W66 (inter-island logistics), W196 (route planning), and W4 (store replenishment) by focusing on the ferry-specific scheduling and coordination.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Weekly Ferry Schedule Review & Shipment Planning**: (a) each Monday, Logistics Coordinator reviews upcoming week's inter-island delivery requirements from replenishment orders (W4); (b) coordinator checks ferry operator schedules for required routes: Davao–Cebu (2GO, Trans-Asia), Cebu–Tagbilaran (Oceanjet, Lite Shipping), Cebu–Dumaguete (Oceanjet, Cokaliong), Batangas–Calapan (Starhorse, Besta), Matnog–Allen (Philippine Fast Ferry); (c) maps delivery orders to available sailings: priority orders on earliest sailing, standard orders on scheduled sailing, hazmat orders on cargo-only vessel; (d) confirms ferry bookings for the week: truck plate number, driver name, cargo manifest, estimated crossing time; (e) contingency plan for routes with single daily sailing: if sailing missed, next available option | Logistics Coordinator | VP Supply Chain | 3–4 hours/week |
| 2 | **Truck Dispatch & Ferry Check-In**: (a) DC dispatches truck per standard outbound process (W106) with inter-island delivery flag; (b) driver proceeds to ferry terminal and presents: booking confirmation (printed or digital), cargo manifest (listing all items and hazmat classification), vehicle registration, driver's license; (c) ferry terminal check-in: vehicle weighed, cargo inspected (spot check for undeclared hazmat), boarding pass issued; (d) driver boards RORO vessel: truck driven onto vehicle deck, driver may stay in vehicle cabin or proceed to passenger deck; (e) system updated with "in transit to ferry" status via fleet telematics (W199) | DC Dispatch / Truck Driver | Logistics Coordinator | 30–60 min at terminal |
| 3 | **Crossing Monitoring & Disruption Management**: (a) system tracks vessel via AIS (Automatic Identification System) or ferry operator API for estimated arrival time; (b) Logistics Coordinator monitors crossing for disruptions: weather-related delay (PAGASA advisory), port congestion, vessel mechanical issue; (c) if crossing delayed >4 hours: store receiving clerk notified of updated ETA; (d) if crossing cancelled: contingency options — re-route to alternative port (longer drive), reschedule to next sailing (24–48 hour delay), or dispatch from alternative DC if closer to store; (e) disruption communicated to store and supply chain planning team for inventory impact assessment | Logistics Coordinator / System | VP Supply Chain | Ongoing during crossing |
| 4 | **Port Arrival, Disembarkation & Store Delivery**: (a) vessel arrives at destination port; truck disembarks via RORO ramp; (b) driver proceeds to store (typically 30 min – 3 hours drive from port); (c) system updates to "en route to store" status; (d) goods receipt at store per standard receiving process (W109); (e) any ferry-related damage (water exposure, shifting cargo) documented and claimed per W500 (transfer order in-transit damage); (f) delivery completion confirmed in system with POD | Truck Driver / Store Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 1–4 hours (port to store) |
| 5 | **Monthly Ferry Performance & Cost Analysis**: (a) monthly report: on-time delivery rate for inter-island shipments (target: ≥85%), average crossing delay, cancellation rate, ferry cost per delivery, damage claim rate; (b) ferry operator performance comparison: on-time rate, cost competitiveness, handling quality; (c) route optimization review: are there alternative routes or ferry operators that reduce cost or improve reliability? (d) annual ferry operator contract review and negotiation; (e) inter-island logistics cost benchmarked against total logistics cost (target: inter-island cost ≤15% premium over land-based delivery) | Logistics Coordinator | VP Supply Chain | 2–3 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP logistics module — inter-island delivery planning, ferry schedule integration
- Ferry operator API/portal — booking, schedule confirmation, vessel tracking
- Fleet telematics module (W199) — truck GPS tracking, status updates
- DC outbound module (W106) — dispatch with inter-island flag
- Store receiving module (W109) — goods receipt with damage documentation
- Damage claim module (W500) — ferry-related damage processing
- PAGASA weather integration — sailing disruption alerts
- Analytics module — inter-island delivery performance, ferry cost analysis, route optimization

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Schedule unreliability**: Philippine ferry schedules are frequently disrupted; building buffer time into delivery planning essential
- **Port congestion**: Major ports (Cebu, Batangas) experience congestion during holidays and post-typhoon periods; 4–8 hour wait times possible
- **Hazmat restrictions**: Not all ferries accept hazmat cargo; separate cargo vessel booking adds cost and lead time
- **Cost**: Inter-island ferry adds PHP 8,000–25,000 per crossing (depending on route and vehicle size) plus driver meal allowance; inter-island delivery cost is 30–50% higher than land-based
- **Weather dependency**: Typhoons can shut down ferry routes for 2–5 days; stores must maintain higher safety stock for island locations


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "Logistics Coordinator reviews upcoming week's inter-island delivery…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "printed or digital" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-route of "route to alternative port" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Supply Chain Logistics Coordinator review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Logistics Coordinator**: Inter-island coordination adds ~3–4 hours/week — absorbed by existing logistics team
- **DC Dispatch**: Standard dispatch process with inter-island flag — no incremental effort
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Weekly planning: 3–4 hours
- Ferry check-in: 30–60 min per crossing (driver time)
- Crossing monitoring: ongoing during crossing (coordinator time)
- Port to store: 1–4 hours
- Monthly review: 2–3 hours
- **Total weekly coordinator effort**: 5–8 hours

## W1232. Customer In-Store Paint Color Archival, Historic Color Retrieval & Match-from-Sample Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer brings paint sample (chip, photo, or object) to store for color matching, or requests to retrieve a previously mixed color |
| **Frequency** | ~40–60 color match requests per store per month; ~8,000–12,000 requests/month chain-wide |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~50 matches/month = ~10,000 matches/month; ~120,000/year |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (Paint Section) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Department Supervisor (Paint & Finishes), Customer |

### Background

BuildRight Depot operates a paint mixing station in every store (Section 12.1), generating custom SKUs per W168 for each custom-tinted paint. Customers frequently need to: (a) match an existing wall color for touch-ups or additions — they bring a paint chip, a piece of drywall, or photograph; (b) retrieve a color they purchased previously (perhaps months ago) for repainting or additional rooms; (c) match a color from a competitor's paint brand. Paint color archival is critical because: homeowners paint rooms in phases, contractors return for matching paint on multi-phase projects, and customers who previously purchased custom paint need exact reproduction. Without a systematic color archival system, customers get inconsistent color matches across purchases, leading to dissatisfaction and returns. The tinting machine calibration per W1130 ensures accuracy, but the color formula data must be stored and retrievable.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer request intake**: Sales Associate determines request type: (a) match from sample (paint chip, object, photo); (b) retrieve historic color (customer knows previous purchase details); (c) match competitor color (brand + color name/code) | Sales Associate | — | 2 min |
| 2 | **Historic color retrieval**: If customer has previous order reference: (a) search by customer loyalty number, transaction date, or receipt number in POS history; (b) system retrieves previous custom paint formula (base + colorant codes and volumes); (c) verify formula matches customer's physical sample (visual comparison under store lighting); (d) if match confirmed: proceed to mixing per W168 | Sales Associate | — | 5 min |
| 3 | **Color matching from sample**: If no historic record: (a) use spectrophotometer (color scanner) to read customer's sample; (b) system calculates closest color formula from paint brand's colorant library; (c) Sales Associate verifies match visually (sample vs. system recommendation); (d) small test batch (50ml) mixed for customer approval before full can | Sales Associate | — | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **Competitor color cross-reference**: If customer provides competitor brand + color code: (a) system looks up cross-reference table (maintained per W252 item master — competitor-to-BuildRight color mapping); (b) if exact match exists: retrieve formula; (c) if no exact match: use spectrophotometer on customer's sample per step 3 | Sales Associate | — | 5 min |
| 5 | **Color archival**: Upon successful match and customer approval: (a) system saves color formula with unique color ID; (b) link to customer's loyalty account (if registered); (c) record: base paint SKU, colorant codes, volumes per colorant, final RGB/LAB values; (d) customer receives printed color card with formula code for future reference | System / Sales Associate | — | 2 min |
| 6 | **Paint mixing & sale**: Mix approved formula per W168; process sale at POS per W5B | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | Per W168 |
| 7 | **Quarterly color data analytics**: Category Manager reviews: (a) most popular custom colors by region; (b) color match accuracy rate (customer returns/complaints); (c) competitor brand match frequency (competitive intelligence); (d) tinting machine calibration compliance per W1130 | Category Manager | VP Merchandising | 2 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- W168 custom paint mixing for tinting operations
- W1130 paint tinting machine calibration for equipment accuracy
- W252 item master for competitor color cross-reference data
- POS transaction history for previous purchase color retrieval
- W17 loyalty program for customer color history linking
- W543 consignment/custom SKU processing for unique formula SKU creation

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Lighting differences** — color matching in store lighting (fluorescent/LED) may look different under home lighting (warm white, natural daylight); customer dissatisfaction when paint looks different at home than in store
- **Metamerism** — two colors that match under one light source may not match under another; spectrophotometer readings may not capture this; test batch in step 3 mitigates but adds time
- **Tinting machine drift** — even with monthly calibration per W1130, tinting machines drift between calibrations; archived formula mixed 6 months later may not be identical to original
- **Customer sample quality** — faded, dirty, or textured samples produce inaccurate spectrophotometer readings; Sales Associate must set customer expectations for approximate matches from degraded samples
- **Data privacy** — linking color history to loyalty account requires customer consent per W53; customers who decline loyalty enrollment lose historic retrieval capability


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-match of "match from sample" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "verify formula matches customer's physical sample" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-scan of "Color matching from sample" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: Sales Associate (Paint Section) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication

- Sales Associate (Paint): ~15 min per color match request. Monthly per store: ~12 hours. Absorbed by existing Sales Associate role. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

- Per match request: ~15–25 minutes (including test batch). Monthly chain-wide: ~2,500–4,200 hours. Annual: ~30,000–50,000 hours.

## W1430. Paint Tinting Color Formula Master Management, New Vendor Color Onboarding & Seasonal Formula Library Update

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New paint vendor onboarded per W36; or vendor introduces new color collection (annual); or seasonal color trend update; or paint tinting machine recalibration per W1130 reveals formula drift |
| **Frequency** | New vendor onboarding: 2–3 times/year; new color collections: 1–2 times/year per vendor; seasonal formula updates: quarterly; formula corrections: as needed |
| **Volume** | ~8 paint vendors (Boysen, Davies, Welcoat, Nippon Paint, etc.); each vendor offers 1,000–3,000 standard colors; total formula library: ~8,000–15,000 color formulas across 200 stores |
| **Owner** | Merchandise Planner (Paint Category) |
| **Participants** | Merchandise Planner, Category Manager (Paint), Paint Vendor Technical Representative, IT Specialist, Store Department Supervisor (Paint) |

### Background

BuildRight Depot's paint mixing stations (1 per store, 200 total) are a critical differentiating service that allows customers to select any color from a vendor's color library and have it mixed on-site. Behind this service is a complex master data management challenge: (a) each paint vendor has proprietary base paints and tinting colorants with specific formulas for each color code, (b) formulas must be loaded into the tinting machine's computer at each store — a formula error means wrong color output and customer complaint, (c) vendors periodically update their color collections (new colors, discontinued colors, reformulated existing colors due to environmental regulations), (d) BuildRight carries 8+ paint vendors, each with their own formula system that must be loaded into BuildRight's unified tinting machines, (e) seasonal color trends (e.g., Pantone Color of the Year, Philippine interior design trends) may require adding specialty formulas. This workflow manages the paint color formula master data lifecycle: creation, vendor onboarding, distribution to stores, seasonal updates, and accuracy verification.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **New vendor formula onboarding**: When a new paint vendor is onboarded per W36: (a) Paint Vendor Technical Representative provides: (i) complete color library file (typically Excel or proprietary format) with color codes, color names, base paint required, and tinting formula (mL of each colorant per base size), (ii) machine calibration parameters specific to their paints, (iii) base paint SKU codes for ERP integration, (b) Merchandise Planner converts vendor format to BuildRight's standardized formula format: (i) validate each formula: colorant volumes within machine dispensing range, (ii) map vendor color codes to BuildRight SKU system, (iii) load into formula master database, (c) IT Specialist pushes formula update to all 200 store tinting machines via remote deployment | Merchandise Planner / IT Specialist | Category Manager | 3–5 days/vendor |
| 2 | **Annual color collection update**: When a vendor releases new color collection (typically annually): (a) Merchandise Planner receives updated color library from vendor, (b) compare new vs. existing: (i) new colors to add (typically 50–200 new colors per collection update), (ii) discontinued colors to flag (do not delete — needed for reorders of existing customer matches), (iii) reformulated colors (vendor changed formula for existing color code — update and note change), (c) load updates into formula master and deploy to stores, (d) Store Department Supervisors (Paint) notified of changes: new color swatches for display rack, discontinued colors to remove from customer-facing swatch books | Merchandise Planner | Category Manager | 2–3 days/vendor/update |
| 3 | **Seasonal formula additions**: Quarterly, Merchandise Planner adds seasonal specialty formulas: (a) trending colors per Philippine interior design forecasts (e.g., earth tones for 2026, pastel accents), (b) Pantone Color of the Year and related palette, (c) custom formulas requested by trade customers (architects/designers) that may have broader appeal, (d) seasonal promotional colors (e.g., Christmas reds/greens for Dec), (e) each new seasonal formula must be tested: mix sample, compare to reference, verify accuracy before deploying to stores | Merchandise Planner | Category Manager | 1–2 days/quarter |
| 4 | **Store-level formula verification**: (a) during W1130 (paint tinting machine calibration verification), Store Department Supervisor also verifies formula accuracy: (i) mix test colors from each vendor and compare to reference swatches, (ii) flag formulas that produce incorrect color output (possible formula data corruption during transmission), (b) if discrepancy found: (i) Merchandise Planner investigates (formula error vs. machine calibration issue vs. expired colorant), (ii) corrected formula re-deployed to affected stores | Store Department Supervisor / Merchandise Planner | Category Manager | 30 min/store/month (absorbed in W1130) |
| 5 | **Formula master governance**: (a) Merchandise Planner maintains formula master governance: (i) version control: each formula has a version number and effective date, (ii) audit trail: who added/modified a formula and when, (iii) store deployment tracker: which stores have the latest formula version, (iv) exception report: stores running outdated formula versions (> 30 days behind) — escalate for IT intervention, (b) annual formula master audit: verify all 8,000–15,000 formulas are current, no orphaned or duplicate entries | Merchandise Planner | Category Manager | 2–3 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Paint formula master database: centralized repository of all vendor color formulas with version control (W1430.1)
- Store tinting machine remote management: formula deployment, version tracking, and machine health monitoring across 200 locations (W1430.1)
- Vendor color library import tool: automated format conversion from vendor files to BuildRight standard (W1430.2)
- Seasonal formula library: specialty and trending color formulas with seasonal tags (W1430.3)
- Store deployment tracker: formula version per store with exception reporting for outdated machines (W1430.5)
- Integration with W1130 (paint tinting machine calibration — accuracy verification), W36 (vendor onboarding — new vendor trigger), W29 (product information & content — color swatches for ecommerce), W1282 (paint mixing station daily setup — operational execution)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Multi-vendor formula complexity**: 8+ vendors with different formula systems loaded into a single tinting machine at each store; formula conflicts or machine memory limitations may arise as the library grows to 15,000+ formulas
- **Remote deployment failures**: deploying formula updates to 200 stores remotely depends on store network connectivity; stores with poor internet (provincial areas) may run outdated formulas for extended periods
- **Customer mismatch complaints**: if a customer returns to match a previously mixed color and the formula has been reformulated by the vendor, the new mix may not match exactly — customer frustration and potential complaint per W12 (returns)
- **Colorant inventory management**: each formula requires specific colorants in the tinting machine; if a colorant runs out, the store cannot produce any formula requiring that colorant until replenished — this is a micro-inventory management challenge at store level


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "validate each formula" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-match of "Annual color collection update" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-forecast of "Seasonal formula additions" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-266 (ensure controlled execution — Custom Fabrication & Processing (PA-09.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Time Estimate
- New vendor onboarding: 3–5 days × 2–3 vendors/year = 6–15 days/year
- Annual collection updates: 2–3 days × 8 vendors = 16–24 days/year
- Seasonal formulas: 1–2 days/quarter = 4–8 days/year
- Formula verification: absorbed in W1130
- Governance: 2–3 hours/month = 24–36 hours/year
- Total annual: ~230–410 hours/year (26–47 project days ≈ 208–376 h + governance 24–36 h)

### Staffing Implication
- Merchandise Planner (Paint Category): ~25–40 hours/month averaged. This is a specialized data management role; absorbed within existing Merchandise Planner position with paint category focus.
- IT Specialist: ~3–5 days/year for remote deployment support. Absorbed within IT team.
- Store Department Supervisors (Paint): ~30 min/month for formula verification. Absorbed in W1130 duties.
- No incremental headcount.
